## The Goophered Grapevine / 被下咒的葡萄藤

===Original===
Some years ago my wife was in poor health, and our family doctor, in whose skill and honesty I had implicit confidence, advised a change of climate. I shared, from an unprofessional standpoint, his opinion that the raw winds, the chill rains, and the violent changes of temperature that characterized the winters in the region of the Great Lakes tended to aggravate my wife’s difficulty, and would undoubtedly shorten her life if she remained exposed to them. The doctor’s advice was that we seek, not a temporary place of sojourn, but a permanent residence, in a warmer and more equable climate. I was engaged at the time in grape-culture in northern Ohio, and, as I liked the business and had given it much study, I decided to look for some other locality suitable for carrying it on. I thought of sunny France, of sleepy Spain, of Southern California, but there were objections to them all. It occurred to me that I might find what I wanted in some one of our own Southern States. It was a sufficient time after the war for conditions in the South to have become somewhat settled; and I was enough of a pioneer to start a new industry, if I could not find a place where grape-culture had been tried. I wrote to a cousin who had gone into the turpentine business in central North Carolina. He assured me, in response to my inquiries, that no better place could be found in the South than the State and neighborhood where he lived; the climate was perfect for health, and, in conjunction with the soil, ideal for grape-culture; labor was cheap, and land could be bought for a mere song. He gave us a cordial invitation to come and visit him while we looked into the matter. We accepted the invitation, and after several days of leisurely travel, the last hundred miles of which were up a river on a sidewheel steamer, we reached our destination, a quaint old town, which I shall call Patesville, because, for one reason, that is not its name. There was a red brick market house in the public square, with a tall tower, which held a four-faced clock that struck the hours, and from which there pealed out a curfew at nine o’clock. There were two or three hotels, a courthouse, a jail, stores, offices, and all the appurtenances of a county seat and a commercial emporium; for while Patesville numbered only four or five thousand inhabitants, of all shades of complexion, it was one of the principal towns in North Carolina, and had a considerable trade in cotton and naval stores. This business activity was not immediately apparent to my unaccustomed eyes. Indeed, when I first saw the town, there brooded over it a calm that seemed almost sabbatic in its restfulness, though I learned later on that underneath its somnolent exterior the deeper currents of life﻿—love and hatred, joy and despair, ambition and avarice, faith and friendship﻿—flowed not less steadily than in livelier latitudes.

===Chinese===
若干年前，我妻子身体不好，我们的家庭医生——他的医术与人品我都绝对信服——建议换一个气候环境调养。五大湖地区冬季寒风砭骨、冷雨连绵、气温骤升骤降，我虽非行内人，却也赞同他的判断：这样的气候只会加重内人的病，若再继续受它侵袭，必定折损她的寿数。医生的主张是：不要寻个地方暂住，而要迁往气候温暖、寒暖均匀之地，长住下来。我当时正在俄亥俄州（Ohio）北部经营葡萄栽培，一来喜欢这门营生，二来也下过许多钻研的功夫，便决定另觅一处适宜继续经营的地方。我想过阳光明媚的法国、闲适慵懒的西班牙，还有南加利福尼亚，可它们各有各的不便。我转念想到，不妨在我们自家的南方某州里找找。那时南北战争已过去相当时日，南方各地的局面已渐趋安定；退一步讲，即便找不到试种过葡萄的地方，我也算得上一名拓荒者，大可开出一门新营生。我给一位去北卡罗来纳州中部做松脂生意的表兄写了信。他复信答复我的种种询问，说南方再找不出比他住的这一州这一带更好的地方了：气候于养身再好不过，配上那片土壤，种葡萄简直是天造地设；人工便宜，土地更是贱得近乎白送。他热诚相邀，请我们趁考察之际先到他府上小住。我们应了邀，从容盘桓了数日——末一段一百来英里是乘一艘明轮蒸汽船溯河而上——抵达了目的地：一座古雅的老镇。我姑且称它作帕茨维尔（Patesville），理由之一嘛，这并不是它的真名。公共广场上立着一座红砖市场楼，楼上高塔里装着一座四面钟，按时报时，晚上九点还有宵禁的钟声从塔里荡出来。镇上有两三家旅馆，还有法院、监狱、店铺、事务所，县城治所兼商业重镇应有的一应俱全；帕茨维尔居民虽不过四五千，肤色深浅俱全，却是北卡罗来纳州数得着的大镇之一，棉花和松脂买卖做得不小。这般生意景气，我这不惯的眼睛一时竟瞧不出来。说实话，我初见这镇子时，只觉一层静谧笼罩着它，安适得近乎安息日的安息；后来我才知道，在那副昏昏欲睡的外表底下，生活更深的潜流——爱与恨、欢乐与绝望、雄心与贪欲、信念与情谊——流得丝毫不比更热闹的纬度缓慢。

===Original===
We found the weather delightful at that season, the end of summer, and were hospitably entertained. Our host was a man of means and evidently regarded our visit as a pleasure, and we were therefore correspondingly at our ease, and in a position to act with the coolness of judgment desirable in making so radical a change in our lives. My cousin placed a horse and buggy at our disposal, and himself acted as our guide until I became somewhat familiar with the country.

I found that grape-culture, while it had never been carried on to any great extent, was not entirely unknown in the neighborhood. Several planters thereabouts had attempted it on a commercial scale, in former years, with greater or less success; but like most Southern industries, it had felt the blight of war and had fallen into desuetude.

===Chinese===
那时正当夏末，我们觉得天气好极了，所到之处也备受款待。主人家境殷实，显然把我们的到访当作一桩乐事，我们也便相应地安之若素，得以从容冷静地斟酌行事——要给生活来这么一场翻天覆地的变动，正需要这样一份清醒的判断。表兄拨了一匹马和一辆轻便马车任我们使用，还亲自充当向导，直到我把这一带乡野大致摸熟。

我发现，葡萄栽培这一行，虽从不曾形成大气候，在这一带倒也不是全然陌生的。左近几位种植园主早年都曾试过商业化经营，成败不一；但正如南方的大多数营生一样，它挨过战争的摧折，就此荒废了下来。

===Original===
I went several times to look at a place that I thought might suit me. It was a plantation of considerable extent, that had formerly belonged to a wealthy man by the name of McAdoo. The estate had been for years involved in litigation between disputing heirs, during which period shiftless cultivation had well-nigh exhausted the soil. There had been a vineyard of some extent on the place, but it had not been attended to since the war, and had lapsed into utter neglect. The vines﻿—here partly supported by decayed and broken-down trellises, there twining themselves among the branches of the slender saplings which had sprung up among them﻿—grew in wild and unpruned luxuriance, and the few scattered grapes they bore were the undisputed prey of the first comer. The site was admirably adapted to grape-raising; the soil, with a little attention, could not have been better; and with the native grape, the luscious scuppernong, as my main reliance in the beginning, I felt sure that I could introduce and cultivate successfully a number of other varieties.

One day I went over with my wife to show her the place. We drove out of the town over a long wooden bridge that spanned a spreading millpond, passed the long whitewashed fence surrounding the county fairground, and struck into a road so sandy that the horse’s feet sank to the fetlocks. Our route lay partly up hill and partly down, for we were in the sand-hill county; we drove past cultivated farms, and then by abandoned fields grown up in scrub-oak and short-leaved pine, and once or twice through the solemn aisles of the virgin forest, where the tall pines, well-nigh meeting over the narrow road, shut out the sun, and wrapped us in cloistral solitude. Once, at a crossroads, I was in doubt as to the turn to take, and we sat there waiting ten minutes﻿—we had already caught some of the native infection of restfulness﻿—for some human being to come along, who could direct us on our way. At length a little negro girl appeared, walking straight as an arrow, with a piggin full of water on her head. After a little patient investigation, necessary to overcome the child’s shyness, we learned what we wished to know, and at the end of about five miles from the town reached our destination.

===Chinese===
有一处地方我瞧着中意，前后去看过好几趟。那是一座颇具规模的种植园，从前属于一位姓麦卡杜（McAdoo）的有钱人。这份产业因几房继承人相争，缠讼多年；就在官司拉扯的年月里，耕作敷衍潦草，地力几乎耗竭。园中原本有一片不小的葡萄园，可自开战以来便无人照管，彻底荒废了。那些藤蔓——有的半架在朽坏坍塌的棚架上，有的缠上从藤间窜出的细瘦幼树的枝条——长得野性繁茂、无人修剪，零星结出的几串葡萄，谁先来便归谁，从无争议。这地方种葡萄再合适不过；那片土壤，稍加经管便是上上之选；有本地品种、甘美多汁的斯卡珀农葡萄（scuppernong）——起先全指望它——我深信日后还能引种培育成功好些别的品种。

一天，我带妻子一道去看那地方。我们驱车出镇，驶过一座横跨开阔磨坊水池的长木桥，绕过围着县集市场地的那道长长的白灰栅栏，拐上一条沙路，沙深得马蹄陷到蹄踝。我们的路忽而上坡、忽而下坡——这正是沙岗地带；我们驶过耕种齐整的农场，又经过撂荒的田地，地里长满了矮栎和短叶松，还有一两次穿过原始森林庄严的廊道，高大的松树在窄路上头几乎枝叶相接，遮断了日头，把我们裹进修道院一般的幽寂里。有一回在岔路口，我拿不准该拐向哪边，我们便坐在车上干等——此地安闲的风气我们已沾染了几分——一等就是十分钟，才等来一个能指点路径的人。末了来了个黑人小姑娘，头上顶着一小桶水，身子挺直得像一支箭。那孩子认生，我们耐着性子慢慢套问，总算把想打听的事问明白了。出镇约五英里，我们到了目的地。

===Original===
We drove between a pair of decayed gateposts﻿—the gate itself had long since disappeared﻿—and up a straight sandy lane, between two lines of rotting rail fence, partly concealed by jimson-weeds and briers, to the open space where a dwelling-house had once stood, evidently a spacious mansion, if we might judge from the ruined chimneys that were still standing, and the brick pillars on which the sills rested. The house itself, we had been informed, had fallen a victim to the fortunes of war.

We alighted from the buggy, walked about the yard for a while, and then wandered off into the adjoining vineyard. Upon Annie’s complaining of weariness I led the way back to the yard, where a pine log, lying under a spreading elm, afforded a shady though somewhat hard seat. One end of the log was already occupied by a venerable-looking colored man. He held on his knees a hat full of grapes, over which he was smacking his lips with great gusto, and a pile of grapeskins near him indicated that the performance was no new thing. We approached him at an angle from the rear, and were close to him before he perceived us. He respectfully rose as we drew near, and was moving away, when I begged him to keep his seat.

===Chinese===
我们从一对朽坏的门柱间驶入——门扇本身早已不知去向——沿一条笔直的沙土车道上行，车道两侧是两排朽烂的木栅栏，半掩在曼陀罗草和荆棘丛里，尽头是一块空场；那里从前立着一所宅子，看那还立着的断烟囱和垫承底梁的砖墩，当年想必是座宽敞的大宅。至于宅子本身，我们听人说，早已毁于战火了。

我们下了车，在院子里蹓跶了一会儿，又信步走进紧邻的葡萄园。安妮说乏了，我便领路回到院中；一棵枝叶如盖的大榆树下横着一根松木，正好当个荫凉坐处，虽说是硬了些。木头一头已经坐着一位望之庄重的有色老者。他膝上捧着一帽子葡萄，正咂着嘴唇吃得津津有味，身旁一堆葡萄皮表明，这桩营生他早已不是头一回。我们从他身后斜里走近，到他跟前他才察觉我们。见我们过来，他恭恭敬敬起身要走，我忙请他只管坐着。

===Original===
“Don’t let us disturb you,” I said. “There is plenty of room for us all.”

He resumed his seat with somewhat of embarrassment. While he had been standing, I had observed that he was a tall man, and, though slightly bowed by the weight of years, apparently quite vigorous. He was not entirely black, and this fact, together with the quality of his hair, which was about six inches long and very bushy, except on the top of his head, where he was quite bald, suggested a slight strain of other than negro blood. There was a shrewdness in his eyes, too, which was not altogether African, and which, as we afterwards learned from experience, was indicative of a corresponding shrewdness in his character. He went on eating the grapes, but did not seem to enjoy himself quite so well as he had apparently done before he became aware of our presence.

===Chinese===
「别让我们搅扰了您，」我说，「这儿尽坐得下咱们大家。」

他带着几分局促重新落座。方才他站在那里时，我已看出他是个高个子，虽让岁月的分量压得微微伛偻，却显然还相当硬朗。他肤色并非纯黑，再加上头发的质地——约有六英寸长，蓬蓬茸茸，唯独头顶秃得精光——可见血脉里掺了些黑人之外的血统。他眼里还有一种精明，不全是非洲人的精明；这一点，我们后来凭亲身领教才知道，正映着他性子里的精明。他接着吃葡萄，可那副享用模样，比起察觉我们在场之前，分明打了折扣。

===Original===
“Do you live around here?” I asked, anxious to put him at his ease.

“Yas, suh. I lives des ober yander, behine de nex’ san’-hill, on de Lumberton plank-road.”

“Do you know anything about the time when this vineyard was cultivated?”

===Chinese===
「您就住在这左近吗？」我问，一心想让他自在些。

「是咧，先生。俺就住在这边，下一道沙岗后头，伦伯顿木板路（Lumberton plank-road）边上。」

「这葡萄园还有人栽种的那些年月的事，您可知道些么？」

===Original===
“Lawd bless you, suh, I knows all about it. Dey ain’ na’er a man in dis settlement w’at won’ tell you ole Julius McAdoo ’uz bawn en raise’ on dis yer same plantation. Is you de Norv’n gemman w’at’s gwine ter buy de ole vimya’d?”

“I am looking at it,” I replied; “but I don’t know that I shall care to buy unless I can be reasonably sure of making something out of it.”

“Well, suh, you is a stranger ter me, en I is a stranger ter you, en we is bofe strangers ter one anudder, but ’f I ’uz in yo’ place, I wouldn’ buy dis vimya’d.”

===Chinese===
「老天爷保佑，先生，这事儿俺全知道。这一带没有一个人不会告诉您，老朱利叶斯·麦卡杜（Julius McAdoo）就是在这座种植园里出生、在这座种植园里长大的。您就是那位要买下这老葡萄园的北方先生吧？」

「我正看着呢，」我答道，「不过要是不能有几分把握靠它挣出些名堂来，我说不定还不乐意买。」

「唉，先生，您跟俺不熟，俺跟您也不熟，咱俩彼此都是生人。可俺要是处在您的位子上，俺就不买这葡萄园。」

===Original===
“Why not?” I asked.

“Well, I dunno whe’r you b’lieves in cunj’in’ er not﻿—some er de w’ite folks don’t, er says dey don’t﻿—but de truf er de matter is dat dis yer ole vimya’d is goophered.”

“Is what?” I asked, not grasping the meaning of this unfamiliar word.

===Chinese===
「为什么不能买呢？」我问。

「呃，俺不知道您信不信巫术（conjure）这一套——有些白人老爷不信，或者嘴上说不大信——可这事情的底细是：这个老葡萄园是叫人下了咒（goopher）的。」

「下了什么？」我问，一时没有听懂这个眼生的词。

===Original===
“Is goophered﻿—cunju’d, bewitch’.”

He imparted this information with such solemn earnestness, and with such an air of confidential mystery, that I felt somewhat interested, while Annie was evidently much impressed, and drew closer to me.

“How do you know it is bewitched?” I asked.

===Chinese===
「下了咒——就是施了巫法、中了邪。」

他透露这个消息时那副郑重其事的恳切劲儿，加上那股推心置腹的神秘模样，令我不禁起了几分兴致；安妮显然深受触动，朝我身边挨得更近了。

「您怎么知道这园子中了邪呢？」我问。

===Original===
“I wouldn’ spec’ fer you ter b’lieve me ’less you know all ’bout de fac’s. But ef you en young miss dere doan’ min’ lis’nin’ ter a ole nigger run on a minute er two w’ile you er restin’, I kin ’splain to you how it all happen’.”

We assured him that we would be glad to hear how it all happened, and he began to tell us. At first the current of his memory﻿—or imagination﻿—seemed somewhat sluggish; but as his embarrassment wore off, his language flowed more freely, and the story acquired perspective and coherence. As he became more and more absorbed in the narrative, his eyes assumed a dreamy expression, and he seemed to lose sight of his auditors, and to be living over again in monologue his life on the old plantation.

===Chinese===
「俺不指望您信俺的话，除非您把事情的前前后后都听全了。不过要是您和这位太太趁着歇脚，不嫌听一个老黑奴絮叨一两分钟，俺就能把这一切是怎么来的，原原本本讲给您听。」

我们向他担保，很乐意听听这一切是怎么发生的，他便开了腔。起初，他记忆——或者想象——的水流似乎还有些滞涩；可随着局促渐渐褪去，他的话头愈见流畅，故事也有了远近层次、前后照应。他越讲越入神，眼里浮起一种悠悠出神的光，仿佛看不见眼前的听众，只顾在独白里把他在老种植园上的一生重新活了一遍。

===Original===
“Ole Mars Dugal’ McAdoo,” he began, “bought dis place long many years befo’ de wah, en I’member well w’en he sot out all dis yer part er de plantation in scuppernon’s. De vimes growed monst’us fas’, en Mars Dugal’ made a thousan’ gallon er scuppernon’ wine eve’y year.

===Chinese===
「老杜加尔·麦卡杜老爷（Mars Dugal’ McAdoo），」他开了腔，「早在南北战争前好多年就买下了这片地方，俺还记得清清楚楚，他把种植园这边这一整片全栽上了斯卡珀农葡萄。藤子长得飞快，杜加尔老爷每年能酿一千加仑斯卡珀农葡萄酒。

===Original===
“Now, ef dey’s an’thing a nigger lub, nex’ ter possum, en chick’n, en watermillyums, it’s scuppernon’s. Dey ain’ nuffin dat kin stan’ up side’n de scuppernon’ fer sweetness; sugar ain’t a suckumstance ter scuppernon’. W’en de season is nigh ’bout ober, en de grapes begin ter swivel up des a little wid de wrinkles er ole age﻿—w’en de skin git sof’ en brown﻿—den de scuppernon’ make you smack yo’ lip en roll yo’ eye en wush fer mo’; so I reckon it ain’ very ’stonishin’ dat niggers lub scuppernon’.

===Chinese===
「要说黑人顶馋的东西，除了负鼠、鸡和西瓜，就轮到斯卡珀农了。天底下没有一样东西，论甜能跟斯卡珀农并排站着的；糖搁在斯卡珀农跟前，简直不值一提。等季节差不多过完，葡萄开始起了皱、添上几道老年纪的褶子——皮也软了、成褐色了——那斯卡珀农能叫你咂着嘴、转着眼珠，一心只盼再来一颗；所以俺想，黑人爱吃斯卡珀农，实在算不得多稀奇的事。

===Original===
“Dey wuz a sight er niggers in de naberhood er de vimya’d. Dere wuz ole Mars Henry Brayboy’s niggers, en ole Mars Jeems McLean’s niggers, en Mars Dugal’s own niggers; den dey wuz a settlement er free niggers en po’ buckrahs down by de Wim’l’ton Road, en Mars Dugal’ had de only vimya’d in de naberhood. I reckon it ain’ so much so nowadays, but befo’ de wah, in slab’ry times, a nigger didn’ mine goin’ fi’ er ten mile in a night, w’en dey wuz sump’n good ter eat at de yuther een’.

===Chinese===
「葡萄园左近一带，黑人可多了去了。有老亨利·布雷博伊老爷（Mars Henry Brayboy）家的黑奴，有老詹姆斯·麦克莱恩老爷（Mars Jeems McLean）家的黑奴，还有杜加尔老爷自家的黑奴；另外，威尔明顿大道（Wilmington Road）边上还住着一伙自由黑人，夹着些穷白人。而这一带，只有杜加尔老爷家有葡萄园。俺估摸如今不大这样了，可从前，还没打仗、还当奴隶的那阵子，只要另一头有好吃的候着，黑人不嫌夜里赶五英里十英里的路。

===Original===
“So atter a w’ile Mars Dugal’ begin ter miss his scuppernon’s. Co’se he ’cuse’ de niggers er it, but dey all ’nied it ter de las’. Mars Dugal’ sot spring guns en steel traps, en he en de oberseah sot up nights once’t er twice’t, tel one night Mars Dugal’﻿—he ’uz a monst’us keerless man﻿—got his leg shot full er cow-peas. But somehow er nudder dey couldn’ nebber ketch none er de niggers. I dunner how it happen, but it happen des like I tell you, en de grapes kep’ on a-goin’ des de same.

===Chinese===
「这么着过了一阵子，杜加尔老爷就开始觉出斯卡珀农少了。不用讲，他把这账算在黑人头上，可黑人们到末了都不认。杜加尔老爷支起弹枪，下了铁夹子，还和监工（oberseah）守过一两回夜，直到有一天夜里，杜加尔老爷——他是个天大的粗心人——一条腿叫弹枪崩了个满满一腿豇豆。可也不知怎么弄的，他们愣是一个黑人也没拿住。俺不知道这里头的窍门，反正事情就像俺讲的这样，葡萄照旧一个劲儿地往外少。

===Original===
“But bimeby ole Mars Dugal’ fix’ up a plan ter stop it. Dey wuz a cunjuh ’oman livin’ down ’mongs’ de free niggers on de Wim’l’ton Road, en all de darkies fum Rockfish ter Beaver Crick wuz feared er her. She could wuk de mos’ powerfulles’ kin’ er goopher﻿—could make people hab fits, er rheumatiz, er make ’em des dwinel away en die; en dey say she went out ridin’ de niggers at night, fer she wuz a witch ’sides bein’ a cunjuh ’oman. Mars Dugal’ hearn ’bout Aun’ Peggy’s doin’s, en begun ter ’flect whe’r er no he couldn’ git her ter he’p him keep de niggers off’n de grapevimes. One day in de spring er de year, ole miss pack’ up a basket er chick’n en poun’-cake, en a bottle er scuppernon’ wine, en Mars Dugal’ tuk it in his buggy en driv ober ter Aun’ Peggy’s cabin. He tuk de basket in, en had a long talk wid Aun’ Peggy.

===Chinese===
「可后来老杜加尔老爷想出了个法子，要绝了这个祸根。威尔明顿大道那边自由黑人住的地方，有一个巫女（conjure woman），从罗克菲什（Rockfish）到比弗溪（Beaver Creek），这一带的黑人个个怕她。她能下顶厉害的那种咒——能叫人害羊痫风、得风湿，还能叫人一天天枯下去、枯死；人家还说，她夜里骑着黑人出门逛，因为她不光是个巫女，还是个女巫。杜加尔老爷听说了佩姬大娘（Aun’ Peggy）的种种手段，就盘算起能不能请她帮把手，把黑人从葡萄藤跟前挡开。一年春天，老夫人装起一篮子鸡肉、磅蛋糕，又添上一瓶斯卡珀农葡萄酒，杜加尔老爷把它搁上轻便马车，赶车去了佩姬大娘的小屋。他提着篮子进了门，跟佩姬大娘谈了老半天。

===Original===
“De nex’ day Aun’ Peggy come up ter de vimya’d. De niggers seed her slippin’ ’roun’, en dey soon foun’ out what she ’uz doin’ dere. Mars Dugal’ had hi’ed her ter goopher de grapevimes. She sa’ntered ’roun’ ’mongs’ de vimes, en tuk a leaf fum dis one, en a grape-hull fum dat one, en a grape-seed fum anudder one; en den a little twig fum here, en a little pinch er dirt fum dere﻿—en put it all in a big black bottle, wid a snake’s toof en a speckle’ hen’s gall en some ha’rs fum a black cat’s tail, en den fill’ de bottle wid scuppernon’ wine. W’en she got de goopher all ready en fix’, she tuk ’n went out in de woods en buried it under de root uv a red oak tree, en den come back en tole one er de niggers she done goopher de grapevimes, en a’er a nigger w’at eat dem grapes ’ud be sho ter die inside’n twel’ mont’s.

===Chinese===
「第二天，佩姬大娘就到葡萄园来了。黑人们瞧见她在这儿悄悄转悠，不多时就弄清了她来做什么——杜加尔老爷雇了她，来给葡萄藤下咒。她在藤子中间溜溜达达，从这一棵上摘片叶子，从那一棵上捡个葡萄壳，再从另一棵上抠粒葡萄籽；又从这儿撅一小根嫩枝，从那儿捏一撮土——把这一切统统装进一只黑色大瓶子，再搁进去一颗蛇牙、一副花斑母鸡的胆、几根黑猫尾巴上的毛，然后把瓶子灌满斯卡珀农葡萄酒。等这咒都备办停当，她便拿着它走进树林，埋在一棵红橡树根底下，回来告诉一个黑人，说她已经在葡萄藤上下好了咒，哪个黑人吃了那些葡萄，保险活不过十二个月。

===Original===
“Atter dat de niggers let de scuppernon’s ’lone, en Mars Dugal’ didn’ hab no ’casion ter fine no mo’ fault; en de season wuz mos’ gone, w’en a strange gemman stop at de plantation one night ter see Mars Dugal’ on some business; en his coachman, seein’ de scuppernon’s growin’ so nice en sweet, slip ’roun’ behine de smoke-house, en et all de scuppernon’s he could hole. Nobody didn’ notice it at de time, but dat night, on de way home, de gemman’s hoss runned away en kill’ de coachman. W’en we hearn de noos, Aun’ Lucy, de cook, she up ’n say she seed de strange nigger eat’n’ er de scuppernon’s behine de smoke-house; en den we knowed de goopher had b’en er wukkin’. Den one er de nigger chilluns runned away fum de quarters one day, en got in de scuppernon’s, en died de nex’ week. W’ite folks say he die’ er de fevuh, but de niggers knowed it wuz de goopher. So you k’n be sho de darkies didn’ hab much ter do wid dem scuppernon’ vimes.

===Chinese===
「打那以后，黑人就不沾斯卡珀农了，杜加尔老爷也再没由头挑剔什么。季节都快过尽了，一天夜里，来了位外乡先生，为着一些事务到种植园上见杜加尔老爷；他的车夫见斯卡珀农长得又好又甜，就溜到熏肉房后头，把肚皮能装下的葡萄吃了个净光。当时谁也没留神，可当天夜里回家的路上，那位先生的马惊了，把车夫摔死了。我们一听见信儿，厨子露西大娘（Aun’ Lucy）就站出来说，她瞧见那个外乡黑人躲在熏肉房后头吃斯卡珀农；这下大伙儿明白了，是那咒显了灵。后来又有一个黑人娃子有一天从住区里跑出去，钻进斯卡珀农藤里，过了一个礼拜就死了。白人说他是害热病死的，可黑人都心里有数，那是咒闹的。所以您尽管放心，打那往后，黑人跟那些斯卡珀农藤就搭不上什么交道了。

===Original===
“W’en de scuppernon’ season ’uz ober fer dat year, Mars Dugal’ foun’ he had made fifteen hund’ed gallon er wine; en one er de niggers hearn him laffin’ wid de oberseah fit ter kill, en sayin’ dem fifteen hund’ed gallon er wine wuz monst’us good intrus’ on de ten dollars he laid out on de vimya’d. So I ’low ez he paid Aun’ Peggy ten dollars fer to goopher de grapevimes.

===Chinese===
「那年斯卡珀农的季节过完，杜加尔老爷一算，酿成了一千五百加仑酒；有个黑人听见他跟监工笑得要死，说那一千五百加仑酒，是他撂在葡萄园上那十块钱生出的一笔好大利息。所以俺琢磨着，他是付了佩姬大娘十块钱，雇她给葡萄藤下的咒。

===Original===
“De goopher didn’ wuk no mo’ tel de nex’ summer, w’en ’long to’ds de middle er de season one er de fiel’ han’s died; en ez dat lef’ Mars Dugal’ sho’t er han’s, he went off ter town fer ter buy anudder. He fotch de noo nigger home wid ’im. He wuz er ole nigger, er de color er a gingy-cake, en ball ez a hoss-apple on de top er his head. He wuz a peart ole nigger, do’, en could do a big day’s wuk.

===Chinese===
「那咒一直没再发作，直到第二年夏天，快到季中的时候，一个田里的劳力死了；杜加尔老爷手上短了人，就进城另买了一个。他把那新黑奴带回了家。那是个老黑奴，肤色跟块姜饼一个样，头顶秃得像颗马苹果。不过话说回来，他是个精神头十足的老黑人，一大天的重活都拿得下来。

===Original===
“Now it happen dat one er de niggers on de nex’ plantation, one er ole Mars Henry Brayboy’s niggers, had runned away de day befo’, en tuk ter de swamp, en ole Mars Dugal’ en some er de yuther nabor w’ite folks had gone out wid dere guns en dere dogs fer ter he’p ’em hunt fer de nigger; en de han’s on our own plantation wuz all so flusterated dat we fuhgot ter tell de noo han’ ’bout de goopher on de scuppernon’ vimes. Co’se he smell de grapes en see de vimes, an atter dahk de fus’ thing he done wuz ter slip off ter de grapevimes ’dout sayin’ nuffin ter nobody. Nex’ mawnin’ he tole some er de niggers ’bout de fine bait er scuppernon’ he et de night befo’.

===Chinese===
「赶巧不巧，头一天，隔壁种植园——老亨利·布雷博伊老爷家的一个黑奴跑了，一头扎进沼泽地里，老杜加尔老爷和左近另几位白人老爷都带着枪、牵着狗，出去帮着追那黑奴；我们自家种植园上的人手全慌了神儿，忘了把斯卡珀农藤上有咒这茬儿告诉那个新人。他自然是闻见了葡萄、瞧见了藤子，天一擦黑，头一件事就是一声不吭地溜到葡萄藤那儿去了。第二天早上，他还跟几个黑人念叨，头天夜里他吃了一顿多美的斯卡珀农呢。

===Original===
“W’en dey tole ’im ’bout de goopher on de grapevimes, he ’uz dat tarrified dat he turn pale, en look des like he gwine ter die right in his tracks. De oberseah come up en axed w’at ’uz de matter; en w’en dey tole ’im Henry be’n eatin’ er de scuppernon’s, en got de goopher on ’im, he gin Henry a big drink er w’iskey, en ’low dat de nex’ rainy day he take ’im ober ter Aun’ Peggy’s, en see ef she wouldn’ take de goopher off’n him, seein’ ez he didn’ know nuffin erbout it tel he done et de grapes.

===Chinese===
「等大伙儿把葡萄藤上有咒的事告诉他，他吓得脸都变了色，那模样就像当场要断气似的。监工走过来问出了什么事；听说亨利（Henry）吃了斯卡珀农、身上带着咒，他就给亨利灌了满满一大口威士忌，说等下一个雨天，他带他过佩姬大娘家去，瞧瞧她肯不肯把咒从他身上解了——毕竟他先前一点不知道，直等到把葡萄吃下了肚。

===Original===
“Sho nuff, it rain de nex’ day, en de oberseah went ober ter Aun’ Peggy’s wid Henry. En Aun’ Peggy say dat bein’ ez Henry didn’ know ’bout de goopher, en et de grapes in ign’ance er de conseq’ences, she reckon she mought be able fer ter take de goopher off’n him. So she fotch out er bottle wid some cunjuh medicine in it, en po’d some out in a go’d fer Henry ter drink. He manage ter git it down; he say it tas’e like whiskey wid sump’n bitter in it. She ’lowed dat ’ud keep de goopher off’n him tel de spring; but w’en de sap begin ter rise in de grapevimes he ha’ ter come en see her ag’in, en she tell him w’at ’e’s ter do.

===Chinese===
「果不其然，第二天就落雨了，监工便带着亨利去了佩姬大娘家。佩姬大娘说，既然亨利不知道咒的事，是不明就里吃的葡萄，她寻思她兴许有本事把咒从他身上解掉。于是她取出一瓶盛着巫药的瓶子，倒了一些在葫芦瓢里给亨利喝。他好歹咽了下去；他说那味道像掺了苦味的威士忌。她说，这药能压住那咒、保他到开春；可等葡萄藤里的浆汁往上冒的时候，他还得再来见她一回，到那时她自会告诉他该当如何。

===Original===
“Nex’ spring, w’en de sap commence’ ter rise in de scuppernon’ vime, Henry tuk a ham one night. Whar’d he git de ham? *I* doan know; dey wa’n’t no hams on de plantation ’cep’n’ w’at ’uz in de smoke-house, but *I* never see Henry ’bout de smoke-house. But ez I wuz a-sayin’, he tuk de ham ober ter Aun’ Peggy’s; en Aun’ Peggy tole ’im dat w’en Mars Dugal’ begin ter prune de grapevimes, he mus’ go en take ’n scrape off de sap whar it ooze out’n de cut een’s er de vimes, en ’n’int his ball head wid it; en ef he do dat once’t a year de goopher wouldn’ wuk agin ’im long ez he done it. En bein’ ez he fotch her de ham, she fix’ it so he kin eat all de scuppernon’ he want.

===Chinese===
「第二年开春，斯卡珀农藤里的浆汁往上冒的时候，亨利有天夜里弄到了一只火腿。火腿打哪儿弄来的？俺可不知道；种植园上除了熏肉房里挂着的，别处没有火腿，可俺压根儿没瞧见亨利在熏肉房跟前露过面。闲话少说，反正他把火腿拎到了佩姬大娘家；佩姬大娘告诉他，等杜加尔老爷动手修剪葡萄藤，他就得去把藤子断口上渗出的浆汁刮下来，抹上他那颗光脑袋；一年只要这么抹上一回，只要他年年不落，那咒就再不会作践他。再者，看在他捎来火腿的份上，她行了个方便，往后他爱吃多少斯卡珀农，只管吃。

===Original===
“So Henry ’n’int his head wid de sap out’n de big grapevime des ha’f way ’twix’ de quarters en de big house, en de goopher nebber wuk agin him dat summer. But de beatenes’ thing you eber see happen ter Henry. Up ter dat time he wuz ez ball ez a sweeten’ ’tater, but des ez soon ez de young leaves begun ter come out on de grapevimes, de ha’r begun ter grow out on Henry’s head, en by de middle er de summer he had de bigges’ head er ha’r on de plantation. Befo’ dat, Henry had tol’able good ha’r ’roun’ de aidges, but soon ez de young grapes begun ter come, Henry’s ha’r begun to quirl all up in little balls, des like dis yer reg’lar grapy ha’r, en by de time de grapes got ripe his head look des like a bunch er grapes. Combin’ it didn’ do no good; he wuk at it ha’f de night wid er Jim Crow,1 en think he git it straighten’ out, but in de mawnin’ de grapes ’ud be dere des de same. So he gin it up, en tried ter keep de grapes down by havin’ his ha’r cut sho’t.

===Chinese===
「于是亨利就从黑奴住处和大宅正中间那棵大葡萄藤上刮了浆汁，抹了脑袋，那年夏天，那咒就再没找过他的麻烦。可是啊，落在亨利身上的事，真真是你没见过的头一桩稀奇。在那以前，他脑袋秃得像块红薯，可葡萄藤上的嫩叶刚一冒尖，亨利头上就开始冒头发了，到仲夏时节，他顶着全种植园最茂盛的一脑袋头发。先前，亨利脑袋四边原还有一圈挺像样的头发，可小葡萄一挂果，亨利的头发就开始卷成一个个小球，就跟俺们这种天生葡萄粒似的卷发一模一样，等葡萄熟透的当口，他那颗脑袋瞧上去活脱脱一大串葡萄。梳是不中用的；他拿着一把硬齿梳（Jim Crow）[1] 半宿半宿地折腾，自以为梳直了，可第二天一早，那串『葡萄』照旧一颗不少地盘在头上。他只得撒手随它去，把头发剪得短短的，好压住那一头葡萄。

===Original===
“But dat wa’n’t de quares’ thing ’bout de goopher. When Henry come ter de plantation, he wuz gittin’ a little ole an stiff in de j’ints. But dat summer he got des ez spry en libely ez any young nigger on de plantation; fac’, he got so biggity dat Mars Jackson, de oberseah, ha’ ter th’eaten ter whip ’im, ef he didn’ stop cuttin’ up his didos en behave hisse’f. But de mos’ cur’ouses’ thing happen’ in de fall, when de sap begin ter go down in de grapevimes. Fus’, when de grapes ’uz gethered, de knots begun ter straighten out’n Henry’s ha’r; en w’en de leaves begin ter fall, Henry’s ha’r ’mence’ ter drap out; en when de vimes ’uz bar’, Henry’s head wuz baller ’n it wuz in de spring, en he begin ter git ole en stiff in de j’ints ag’in, en paid no mo’ ’tention ter de gals dyoin’ er de whole winter. En nex’ spring, w’en he rub de sap on ag’in, he got young ag’in, en so soopl en libely dat none er de young niggers on de plantation couldn’ jump, ner dance, ner hoe ez much cotton ez Henry. But in de fall er de year his grapes ’mence’ ter straighten out, en his j’ints ter git stiff, en his ha’r drap off, en de rheumatiz begin ter wrastle wid ’im.

===Chinese===
「可这还不是那咒最古怪的门道。亨利刚到种植园的时候，上了点年纪，关节也有些发僵。可那年夏天，他出落得跟园里随便哪个年轻黑人一样轻快活泛；说实在的，他都张狂得没边儿了，监工杰克逊老爷（Mars Jackson）只得吓唬他，再胡闹就要挨鞭子，叫他安分些、懂点规矩。可顶出奇的事出在秋天，葡萄藤里的浆汁往下退的时候。先是葡萄一收完，亨利头发里的小卷卷就开始抻直；跟着叶子一落，亨利的头发就开始往下掉；等藤子精光的时候，亨利的脑袋比开春那会儿还秃，他又见老、关节又僵，一冬里连姑娘们都不再搭理了。到第二年开春，他再把浆汁抹上去，就又年轻回来，那么软和、那么活泛，园里哪个年轻黑人都跳不过他、舞不过他，锄棉花也没他锄得多。可一到秋里，他那串葡萄又抻直了，关节又僵了，头发又掉了，风湿病也寻上来跟他摔跤了。

===Original===
“Now, ef you’d ’a’ knowed ole Mars Dugal’ McAdoo, you’d ’a’ knowed dat it ha’ ter be a mighty rainy day when he couldn’ fine sump’n fer his niggers ter do, en it ha’ ter be a mighty little hole he couldn’ crawl thoo, en ha’ ter be a monst’us cloudy night when a dollar git by him in de dahkness; en w’en he see how Henry git young in de spring en ole in de fall, he ’lowed ter hisse’f ez how he could make mo’ money out’n Henry dan by wukkin’ him in de cotton-fiel’. ’Long de nex’ spring, atter de sap ’mence’ ter rise, en Henry ’n’int ’is head en sta’ted fer ter git young en soopl, Mars Dugal’ up ’n tuk Henry ter town, en sole ’im fer fifteen hunder’ dollars. Co’se de man w’at bought Henry didn’ know nuffin ’bout de goopher, en Mars Dugal’ didn’ see no ’casion fer ter tell ’im. Long to’ds de fall, w’en de sap went down, Henry begin ter git ole ag’in same ez yuzhal, en his noo marster begin ter git skeered les’n he gwine ter lose his fifteen-hunder’-dollar nigger. He sent fer a mighty fine doctor, but de med’cine didn’ ’pear ter do no good; de goopher had a good holt. Henry tole de doctor ’bout de goopher, but de doctor des laff at ’im.

===Chinese===
「唉，你们要是识得老杜加尔·麦卡杜老爷的为人，就知道：除非是瓢泼大雨的天，他不会寻不出活儿给黑人干；除非是针眼大的窟窿，他没有钻不过去的；除非是伸手不见五指的黑夜，没有一块钱能打他眼皮底下溜过去。他瞧出亨利春天变年轻、秋天变老这套来路，就自个儿掂量开了：使唤亨利挣钱，比押着他在棉花地里卖苦力进项大得多。第二年开春，浆汁往上冒，亨利抹了脑袋、正往年轻里变、往活泛里变的时候，杜加尔老爷冷不丁把亨利带进城，一千五百块钱把他卖了。买主自然对咒的事一无所知，杜加尔老爷也觉得没必要多说。快入秋、浆汁往下退的时候，亨利照例又见老了，新主人心里发起毛来，生怕他那花一千五百块买来的黑奴要折在手里。他请了一位顶有名的大夫，可药吃下去不见起色；那咒抓得他死死的。亨利把咒的事说给大夫听，大夫只顾笑话他。

===Original===
“One day in de winter Mars Dugal’ went ter town, en wuz santerin’ ’long de Main Street, when who should he meet but Henry’s noo marster. Dey said ‘Hoddy,’ en Mars Dugal’ ax ’im ter hab a seegyar; en atter dey run on awhile ’bout de craps en de weather, Mars Dugal’ ax ’im, sorter keerless, like ez ef he des thought of it﻿—

===Chinese===
「冬天里有一天，杜加尔老爷进城，正在主街上闲溜达，迎面碰上的不是别人，正是亨利的新主人。两人道了声『好啊』，杜加尔老爷请他抽雪茄；闲扯了一阵收成和天气，杜加尔老爷才装作随口一问，那架势倒像刚想起来似的——

===Original===
“ ‘How you like de nigger I sole you las’ spring?’

“Henry’s marster shuck his head en knock de ashes off’n his seegyar.

===Chinese===
「『去年开春我卖给你的那个黑鬼，你使唤着可还趁手？』」

「亨利的主人摇了摇头，磕掉雪茄上的烟灰。」

===Original===
“ ‘ ’Spec’ I made a bad bahgin when I bought dat nigger. Henry done good wuk all de summer, but sence de fall set in he ’pears ter be sorter pinin’ away. Dey ain’ nuffin pertickler de matter wid ’im﻿—leastways de doctor say so﻿—’cep’n’ a tech er de rheumatiz; but his ha’r is all fell out, en ef he don’t pick up his strenk mighty soon, I spec’ I’m gwine ter lose ’im.’

===Chinese===
「『我买那个黑鬼，怕是做了笔蚀本生意。整个夏天，亨利活儿干得不赖，可自打入秋，他就蔫蔫地一天不如一天。他倒没什么了不得的毛病——至少大夫这么说——就是沾了点儿风湿；可他的头发全掉光了，他要是不能赶紧把气力攒起来，我怕是要把他赔在手里喽。』」

===Original===
“Dey smoked on awhile, en bimeby ole mars say, ‘Well, a bahgin’s a bahgin, but you en me is good fren’s, en I doan wan’ ter see you lose all de money you paid fer dat nigger; en ef w’at you say is so, en I ain’t ’sputin’ it, he ain’t wuf much now. I ’spec’s you wukked him too ha’d dis summer, er e’se de swamps down here don’t agree wid de san’-hill nigger. So you des lemme know, en ef he gits any wusser I’ll be willin’ ter gib yer five hund’ed dollars fer ’im, en take my chances on his livin’.’

===Chinese===
「两人又抽了一阵烟，末了，杜加尔老爷开腔道：『唉，买卖归买卖，可你跟我是要好的朋友，我不忍看你买那黑鬼的钱全打了水漂；你讲的若是实情——我也犯不上争辩——他眼下值不了几个钱了。我寻思，是你今年夏天把他使狠了；再不然，就是这一带的沼泽地跟俺们沙岗地带的黑奴犯冲。你只管给我个信儿，他要是再往坏里去，我愿意出五百块把他接过来，他死他活，我认这个风险。』」

===Original===
“Sho ’nuff, when Henry begun ter draw up wid de rheumatiz en it look like he gwine ter die fer sho, his noo marster sen’ fer Mars Dugal’, en Mars Dugal’ gin him what he promus, en brung Henry home ag’in. He tuk good keer uv ’im dyoin’ er de winter﻿—give ’im w’iskey ter rub his rheumatiz, en terbacker ter smoke, en all he want ter eat﻿—’caze a nigger w’at he could make a thousan’ dollars a year off’n didn’ grow on eve’y huckleberry bush.

===Chinese===
「果不其然，等亨利叫风湿拘挛得缩成一团、眼瞅着真要不济的时候，新主人打发人去请杜加尔老爷，杜加尔老爷便依着许下的价给了钱，把亨利又领回家来。整个冬天，他把亨利照看得妥妥帖帖——给他威士忌搓风湿，给他烟叶抽，想吃什么都管够——因为一个一年能从他身上挣出一千块的黑奴，可不是随便哪丛越橘上都长得出来的。

===Original===
“Nex’ spring, w’en de sap ris en Henry’s ha’r commence’ ter sprout, Mars Dugal’ sole ’im ag’in, down in Robeson County dis time; en he kep’ dat sellin’ business up fer five year er mo’. Henry nebber say nuffin ’bout de goopher ter his noo marsters, ’caze he know he gwine ter be tuk good keer uv de nex’ winter, w’en Mars Dugal’ buy him back. En Mars Dugal’ made ’nuff money off’n Henry ter buy anudder plantation ober on Beaver Crick.

===Chinese===
「第二年开春，浆汁上来、亨利的头发刚冒芽，杜加尔老爷又把他卖了，这一回卖到了罗伯逊县（Robeson County）；这桩卖来卖去的买卖，他前后做了五年还不止。亨利从没跟那些新主人吐露过咒的半个字，因为他知道，等到入冬杜加尔老爷把他买回去，自会好吃好喝地养着他。靠从亨利身上挣下的钱，杜加尔老爷都够上比弗溪那边再置一座种植园了。

===Original===
“But ’long ’bout de een’ er dat five year dey come a stranger ter stop at de plantation. De fus’ day he ’uz dere he went out wid Mars Dugal’ en spent all de mawnin’ lookin’ ober de vimya’d, en atter dinner dey spent all de evenin’ playin’ kya’ds. De niggers soon ’skiver’ dat he wuz a Yankee, en dat he come down ter Norf C’lina fer ter l’arn de w’ite folks how to raise grapes en make wine. He promus Mars Dugal’ he c’d make de grapevimes b’ar twice’t ez many grapes, en dat de noo winepress he wuz a-sellin’ would make mo’ d’n twice’t ez many gallons er wine. En ole Mars Dugal’ des drunk it all in, des ’peared ter be bewitch’ wid dat Yankee. W’en de darkies see dat Yankee runnin’ ’roun’ de vimya’d en diggin’ under de grapevimes, dey shuk dere heads, en ’lowed dat dey feared Mars Dugal’ losin’ his min’. Mars Dugal’ had all de dirt dug away fum under de roots er all de scuppernon’ vimes, an’ let ’em stan’ dat away fer a week er mo’. Den dat Yankee made de niggers fix up a mixtry er lime en ashes en manyo, en po’ it ’roun’ de roots er de grapevimes. Den he ’vise Mars Dugal’ fer ter trim de vimes close’t, en Mars Dugal’ tuck ’n done eve’ything de Yankee tole him ter do. Dyoin’ all er dis time, mind yer, dis yer Yankee wuz libbin’ off’n de fat er de lan’, at de big house, en playin’ kya’ds wid Mars Dugal’ eve’y night; en dey say Mars Dugal’ los’ mo’n a thousan’ dollars dyoin’ er de week dat Yankee wuz a-ruinin’ de grapevimes.

===Chinese===
「可就在那五年将尽的当口，种植园来了个寄住的生人。头一天，他就跟着杜加尔老爷出去，一整个上午都耗在查看葡萄园上；饭后，两人又把一整个傍晚耗在打牌上。黑人们不多时就看出，他是个北方佬（Yankee），是下到北卡罗来纳来，教白人老爷们怎么种葡萄、酿葡萄酒的。他向杜加尔老爷担保，他能叫葡萄藤结出两倍的葡萄，还说他正推销的那种新式榨酒机，能榨出不止两倍的酒。老杜加尔老爷把这些话囫囵全咽了下去，简直像叫那北方佬迷了心窍。黑人瞧那北方佬在园子里跑来跑去、在葡萄藤根底下刨挖，都直摇头，说只怕杜加尔老爷脑子糊涂了。杜加尔老爷叫人把斯卡珀农藤根底下的土统统挖开，由着藤子那么敞根晾了一个多礼拜。随后，那北方佬叫黑人配了一料石灰、草木灰加粪肥的混合物，围着葡萄根浇下去。接着他又劝杜加尔老爷把藤子往死里剪，杜加尔老爷便北方佬说啥他做啥。这期间你可记着，这位北方佬住在大宅里，吃着这地界最肥的出产，夜夜陪杜加尔老爷打牌；都说就在那北方佬糟蹋葡萄园的那个礼拜里，杜加尔老爷输给了他一千多块。

===Original===
“W’en de sap ris nex’ spring, ole Henry ’n’inted his head ez yuzhal, en his ha’r ’mence’ ter grow des de same ez it done eve’y year. De scuppernon’ vimes growed monst’s fas’, en de leaves wuz greener en thicker dan dey eber be’n dyoin’ my rememb’ance; en Henry’s ha’r growed out thicker dan eber, en he ’peared ter git younger ’n younger, en soopler ’n soopler; en seein’ ez he wuz sho’t er han’s dat spring, havin’ tuk in consid’able noo groun’, Mars Dugal’ ’cluded he wouldn’ sell Henry ’tel he git de crap in en de cotton chop’. So he kep’ Henry on de plantation.

===Chinese===
「第二年开春浆汁上来，老亨利照旧抹了脑袋，头发也照旧一年一茬地长。斯卡珀农藤长得飞快，叶子比俺记事儿以来哪一年都绿、都厚；亨利的头发也长得比哪年都旺，人瞧着一年比一年后生，一天比一天活泛。那年春天杜加尔老爷手头正缺人，因为新开了好些生地，他打定主意，等庄稼进了仓、棉花锄完末一遍苗，再卖亨利不迟。就这样，亨利留在了种植园上。

===Original===
“But ’long ’bout time fer de grapes ter come on de scuppernon’ vimes, dey ’peared ter come a change ober ’em; de leaves withered en swivel’ up, en de young grapes turn’ yaller, en bimeby eve’ybody on de plantation could see dat de whole vimya’d wuz dyin’. Mars Dugal’ tuk’n water de vimes en done all he could, but ’t wa’n’ no use: dat Yankee had done bus’ de watermillyum. One time de vimes picked up a bit, en Mars Dugal’ ’lowed dey wuz gwine ter come out ag’in; but dat Yankee done dug too close under de roots, en prune de branches too close ter de vime, en all dat lime en ashes done burn’ de life out’n de vimes, en dey des kep’ a-with’in’ en a-swivelin’.

===Chinese===
「可到了斯卡珀农该挂果的时候，藤子上像是起了变故；叶子枯了、蔫了、缩了，小葡萄发了黄，不多时，种植园上人人都看得分明：整座葡萄园都在死去。杜加尔老爷又是浇水，又把能想的辙都想了，可全是白搭：那北方佬早把整颗西瓜砸破了。有一阵子藤子稍稍支棱起来一点，杜加尔老爷还当它们能缓过来；可那北方佬在根底下挖得太近，枝又剪得贴藤太狠，那些石灰和草木灰又把藤子里的生机烧了个干净，它们就只好一天天枯下去、缩下去。

===Original===
“All dis time de goopher wuz a-wukkin’. When de vimes sta’ted ter wither, Henry ’mence’ ter complain er his rheumatiz; en when de leaves begin ter dry up, his ha’r ’mence’ ter drap out. When de vimes fresh’ up a bit, Henry’d git peart ag’in, en when de vimes wither’ ag’in, Henry’d git ole ag’in, en des kep’ gittin’ mo’ en mo’ fitten fer nuffin; he des pined away, en pined away, en fine’ly tuk ter his cabin; en when de big vime whar he got de sap ter ’n’int his head withered en turned yaller en died, Henry died too﻿—des went out sorter like a cannel. Dey didn’t ’pear ter be nuffin de matter wid ’im, ’cep’n’ de rheumatiz, but his strenk des dwinel’ away ’tel he didn’ hab ernuff lef’ ter draw his bref. De goopher had got de under holt, en th’owed Henry dat time fer good en all.

===Chinese===
「这工夫，那咒一直在暗地里使劲儿。藤子一开始枯，亨利就嚷嚷风湿犯了；叶子一开始发干，他的头发就开始往下掉。藤子稍稍缓过一口气，亨利就又精神起来；藤子再蔫下去，他就又见老，一天比一天更不中用；他就那么一日一日地枯损下去，末了躺倒在自家小屋里起不来；等到他取浆汁抹头的那棵大藤也枯了、黄了、死了，亨利也跟着去了——就像一支蜡烛，慢慢烧到了头，悄悄灭了。他瞧着也没什么病，不就是风湿，可那股气力一天天耗尽，到后来连喘口气的劲儿都不剩了。那一回，咒抢得了下把位，把亨利结结实实摔了出去，这一摔，就再没让他爬起来。

===Original===
“Mars Dugal’ tuk on might’ly ’bout losin’ his vimes en his nigger in de same year; en he swo’ dat ef he could git holt er dat Yankee he’d wear ’im ter a frazzle, en den chaw up de frazzle; en he’d done it, too, for Mars Dugal’ ’uz a monst’us brash man w’en he once git started. He sot de vimya’d out ober ag’in, but it wuz th’ee er fo’ year befo’ de vimes got ter b’arin’ any scuppernon’s.

===Chinese===
「一年里头，葡萄园和黑奴先后都没了，杜加尔老爷那个痛心哟；他赌咒发誓，说只要让他逮着那北方佬，他要把他搓成一根烂布条，再把烂布条嚼个粉碎；他真做得出来，杜加尔老爷一旦开了头，是个天不怕地不怕的火性子。他把葡萄园重新栽了一遍，可足足过了三四年，藤子才又结出斯卡珀农来。

===Original===
“W’en de wah broke out, Mars Dugal’ raise’ a comp’ny, en went off ter fight de Yankees. He say he wuz mighty glad dat wah come, en he des want ter kill a Yankee fer eve’y dollar he los’ ’long er dat grape-raisin’ Yankee. En I ’spec’ he would ’a’ done it, too, ef de Yankees hadn’ s’picioned sump’n, en killed him fus’. Atter de s’render ole miss move’ ter town, de niggers all scattered ’way fum de plantation, en de vimya’d ain’ be’n cultervated sence.”

“Is that story true?” asked Annie doubtfully, but seriously, as the old man concluded his narrative.

“It’s des ez true ez I’m a-settin’ here, miss. Dey’s a easy way ter prove it: I kin lead de way right ter Henry’s grave ober yander in de plantation buryin’-groun’. En I tell yer w’at, marster, I wouldn’ ’vise you to buy dis yer ole vimya’d, ’caze de goopher’s on it yit, en dey ain’ no tellin’ w’en it’s gwine ter crap out.”

===Chinese===
「后来打起了仗，杜加尔老爷自己招了一队人马，开拔去打北方佬。他说，这仗打起来他高兴得很，他正巴望着，那坑走他钱的种葡萄北方佬每坑他一块钱，他就要宰一个北方仔回本。俺看他真干得出来——要不是北方佬起了疑心，先把他结果了。投降之后，老夫人搬进了城，黑人们都从种植园四散走了，这葡萄园打那以后就再没人侍弄过。」

「这故事是真的吗？」老人讲完这段叙述，安妮将信将疑，却又神情郑重地问。

「俺坐在这儿有多真，这故事就有多真，太太。有个现成的法子能证明：俺能领着道儿，一直走到那边种植园墓地里亨利的坟头跟前。俺再跟您说一句，老爷，俺劝您别买这个老葡萄园，因为那咒到如今还附着在上头，谁说得准它什么时候又要发作。」

===Original===
“But I thought you said all the old vines died.”

“Dey did ’pear ter die, but a few un ’em come out ag’in, en is mixed in ’mongs’ de yuthers. I ain’ skeered ter eat de grapes, ’caze I knows de old vimes fum de noo ones; but wid strangers dey ain’ no tellin’ w’at mought happen. I wouldn’ ’vise yer ter buy dis vimya’d.”

===Chinese===
「可我记得您方才说，老藤全都死光了呀。」

「它们当年瞧着是死了，可有几棵又缓过来了，如今混在新藤里头。俺自家吃葡萄不怕，因为俺认得出哪些是老藤、哪些是新藤；可换了生人，天知道会出什么事。俺劝您，这葡萄园别买。」

===Original===
I bought the vineyard, nevertheless, and it has been for a long time in a thriving condition, and is often referred to by the local press as a striking illustration of the opportunities open to Northern capital in the development of Southern industries. The luscious scuppernong holds first rank among our grapes, though we cultivate a great many other varieties, and our income from grapes packed and shipped to the Northern markets is quite considerable. I have not noticed any developments of the goopher in the vineyard, although I have a mild suspicion that our colored assistants do not suffer from want of grapes during the season.

===Chinese===
尽管如此，我还是把葡萄园买了下来。这些年来，它一直经营得欣欣向荣，当地报纸还常常拿它作例子，说它是北方资本南下、开发南方实业的一个惹眼的样板。甘美多汁的斯卡珀农，在我们的葡萄里稳坐头一把交椅——虽说我们也栽种着许多别的品种——装箱运往北方市场售卖的葡萄，也给我们带来相当可观的进项。园子里，我倒不曾见那咒发作过什么迹象，只是隐隐有点疑心：每逢季节一到，我们雇的黑人帮工，好像从来不曾短过葡萄吃。

===Original===
I found, when I bought the vineyard, that Uncle Julius had occupied a cabin on the place for many years, and derived a respectable revenue from the product of the neglected grapevines. This, doubtless, accounted for his advice to me not to buy the vineyard, though whether it inspired the goopher story I am unable to state. I believe, however, that the wages I paid him for his services as coachman, for I gave him employment in that capacity, were more than an equivalent for anything he lost by the sale of the vineyard.

===Chinese===
买下葡萄园之后我才知道，朱利叶斯大叔在园里那间小屋已经住了好些年，一直靠这些无人经管的葡萄藤上的出产，稳稳挣着一份可观的进项。这多半就解释了他为什么劝我别买这园子；至于那份咒的故事是不是也打这儿来的，我就无从说起了。不过我相信，后来我雇他当了车夫，为此付给他的工钱，抵偿他因葡萄园易手而损失的一切，绰绰有余。

## Po’ Sandy / 可怜的桑迪

===Original===
On the northeast corner of my vineyard in central North Carolina, and fronting on the Lumberton plank-road, there stood a small frame house, of the simplest construction. It was built of pine lumber, and contained but one room, to which one window gave light and one door admission. Its weather-beaten sides revealed a virgin innocence of paint. Against one end of the house, and occupying half its width, there stood a huge brick chimney: the crumbling mortar had left large cracks between the bricks; the bricks themselves had begun to scale off in large flakes, leaving the chimney sprinkled with unsightly blotches. These evidences of decay were but partially concealed by a creeping vine, which extended its slender branches hither and thither in an ambitious but futile attempt to cover the whole chimney. The wooden shutter, which had once protected the unglazed window, had fallen from its hinges, and lay rotting in the rank grass and jimson-weeds beneath. This building, I learned when I bought the place, had been used as a schoolhouse for several years prior to the breaking out of the war, since which time it had remained unoccupied, save when some stray cow or vagrant hog had sought shelter within its walls from the chill rains and nipping winds of winter.

===Chinese===
在我那处位于北卡罗来纳州中部的葡萄园的东北角，临着伦伯顿木板路，立着一座构造极为简易的小木屋。它是松木盖的，只有一间屋子，一扇窗采光，一道门出入。饱经风雨的四壁，还显出一副从未沾过油漆的清白本色。屋子一头贴墙立着一座巨大的砖烟囱，占了屋端宽度的一半：酥裂的灰泥在砖缝间留下一道道大口子；砖块本身也开始成片剥落，烟囱上遍布难看的斑痕。这些朽坏的迹象，只有一部分被一株攀援的藤蔓遮住；它把纤细的枝条东一根、西一根地探出去，雄心勃勃却全然徒劳，想把整座烟囱都罩起来。那扇曾经护住无玻璃窗的木百叶早已脱了铰链，朽烂在窗下疯长的野草和曼陀罗草丛里。买下这处产业时我才得知，这栋房子在南北战争爆发前曾当过几年校舍，此后便一直空着，只有走失的母牛或游荡的猪，在冬日冷雨砭骨、寒风割面时，钻进它的四壁之间躲避。

===Original===
One day my wife requested me to build her a new kitchen. The house erected by us, when we first came to live upon the vineyard, contained a very conveniently arranged kitchen; but for some occult reason my wife wanted a kitchen in the back yard, apart from the dwelling-house, after the usual Southern fashion. Of course I had to build it.

To save expense, I decided to tear down the old schoolhouse, and use the lumber, which was in a good state of preservation, in the construction of the new kitchen. Before demolishing the old house, however, I made an estimate of the amount of material contained in it, and found that I would have to buy several hundred feet of lumber additional, in order to build the new kitchen according to my wife’s plan.

===Chinese===
一天，我的妻子要我给她盖一间新厨房。我们初来葡萄园定居时盖的那栋房子里，本有一间布置得十分便利的厨房；可不知出于什么说不清道不明的缘由，我妻子想要一间设在后院、与正屋分开的厨房，照南方通行的样式。当然，这厨房我只好盖。

为了节省开支，我决定拆掉那座旧校舍，用那些保存尚且完好的木料来盖新厨房。不过在动手拆旧屋之前，我估算了一下它含有的用料，发现要按我妻子的图样把新厨房盖起来，还得再买几百英尺木料才行。

===Original===
One morning old Julius McAdoo, our colored coachman, harnessed the gray mare to the rockaway, and drove my wife and me over to the sawmill from which I meant to order the new lumber. We drove down the long lane which led from our house to the plank-road; following the plank-road for about a mile, we turned into a road running through the forest and across the swamp to the sawmill beyond. Our carriage jolted over the half-rotted corduroy road which traversed the swamp, and then climbed the long hill leading to the sawmill. When we reached the mill, the foreman had gone over to a neighboring farmhouse, probably to smoke or gossip, and we were compelled to await his return before we could transact our business. We remained seated in the carriage, a few rods from the mill, and watched the leisurely movements of the mill-hands. We had not waited long before a huge pine log was placed in position, the machinery of the mill was set in motion, and the circular saw began to eat its way through the log, with a loud whir which resounded throughout the vicinity of the mill. The sound rose and fell in a sort of rhythmic cadence, which, heard from where we sat, was not unpleasing, and not loud enough to prevent conversation. When the saw started on its second journey through the log, Julius observed, in a lugubrious tone, and with a perceptible shudder:—

===Chinese===
一天早晨，我家的黑人车夫老朱利叶斯·麦卡杜（Julius McAdoo）给灰母马套上四轮敞篷马车，载着我妻子和我，去我打算订购新木料的那家锯木厂。马车驶下从我们家通往木板路的长巷；沿木板路走了约一英里，我们拐上一条穿过森林、越过沼泽、直抵远处锯木厂的大路。车身在横贯沼泽、半已朽烂的圆木路上一路颠簸，随后爬上通向锯木厂的长坡。到得厂里，工头到邻近的农舍去了，多半是抽烟闲磕牙，我们只得等他回来才能办事。我们坐在离厂房几竿远的马车上，看锯木工们慢条斯理地忙活。没等多久，一根巨大的松木被架上工位，厂里的机器开动起来，圆锯开始啃进木头，响亮的嗡鸣传遍厂房四周。那声音一起一落，带着一种节奏，坐在我们那儿听来并不难听，也不至于响得妨碍说话。圆锯第二趟咬进木头时，朱利叶斯用一种悲戚的腔调、带着清晰可辨的战栗说道——

===Original===
“Ugh! but dat des do cuddle my blood!”

“What’s the matter, Uncle Julius?” inquired my wife, who is of a very sympathetic turn of mind. “Does the noise affect your nerves?”

===Chinese===
「哎哟！这动静真是叫俺血都凝住了！」

「怎么啦，朱利叶斯大叔？」我妻子问道，她是个天生富于同情心的人。「是这噪声搅了你的神经吗？」

===Original===
“No, Mis’ Annie,” replied the old man, with emotion, “I ain’ narvous; but dat saw, a-cuttin’ en grindin’ thoo dat stick er timber, en moanin’, en groanin’, en sweekin’, kyars my ’memb’ance back ter ole times, en ’min’s me er po’ Sandy.” The pathetic intonation with which he lengthened out the “po’ Sandy” touched a responsive chord in our own hearts.

===Chinese===
「不是的，安妮太太，」老人动了感情，答道，「俺不是神经不安；是那把锯子，在那根木头上又割又磨，一声声呻吟、哀号、惨叫，把俺的记性捎回了老日子，让俺想起可怜的桑迪。」他把「可怜的桑迪」几个字拖得长长的，那凄婉的声调，也在我们心里拨响了一根应和的弦。

===Original===
“And who was poor Sandy?” asked my wife, who takes a deep interest in the stories of plantation life which she hears from the lips of the older colored people. Some of these stories are quaintly humorous; others wildly extravagant, revealing the Oriental cast of the negro’s imagination; while others, poured freely into the sympathetic ear of a Northern-bred woman, disclose many a tragic incident of the darker side of slavery.

===Chinese===
「可怜的桑迪是谁呀？」我妻子问。她对从年长有色人种口中听来的种植园生活故事，怀有浓厚的兴趣。这些故事，有的古趣盎然，逗人发笑；有的狂诞离奇，显出黑人想象力的东方气质；还有一些，趁着一双北方长大、肯听人倾诉的女人的耳朵尽情倾吐，道出奴隶制阴暗面里多少桩惨事。

===Original===
“Sandy,” said Julius, in reply to my wife’s question, “was a nigger w’at useter b’long ter ole Mars Marrabo McSwayne. Mars Marrabo’s place wuz on de yuther side’n de swamp, right nex’ ter yo’ place. Sandy wuz a monst’us good nigger, en could do so many things erbout a plantation, en alluz ’ten’ ter his wuk so well, dat w’en Mars Marrabo’s chilluns growed up en married off, dey all un ’em wanted dey daddy fer ter gin ’em Sandy fer a weddin’ present. But Mars Marrabo knowed de res’ wouldn’ be satisfied ef he gin Sandy ter a’er one un ’em; so w’en dey wuz all done married, he fix it by ’lowin’ one er his chilluns ter take Sandy fer a mont’ er so, en den ernudder for a mont’ er so, en so on dat erway tel dey had all had ’im de same lenk er time; en den dey would all take him roun’ ag’in, ’cep’n’ oncet in a w’ile w’en Mars Marrabo would len’ ’im ter some er his yuther kinfolks ’roun’ de country, w’en dey wuz short er han’s; tel bimeby it got so Sandy didn’ hardly knowed whar he wuz gwine ter stay fum one week’s een’ ter de yuther.

===Chinese===
「桑迪呀，」朱利叶斯回答我妻子的问题说，「是个黑人，从前属于老马拉博·麦克斯韦恩（Marrabo McSwayne）老爷。马拉博老爷的庄子在沼泽那一头，紧挨着您这块地。桑迪是个顶顶好的黑人，种植园里样样活计都拿得起，做事又总是那么经心，所以马拉博老爷的儿女们长大成亲时，一个个都缠着爹爹，要他把桑迪当贺礼送给自己。可马拉博老爷心里明白，桑迪给了哪一个，其余的都不会痛快；于是等儿女全都成了亲，他想出个法子：让一个孩子留下桑迪使唤个把月，再换另一个使唤个把月，这么挨个轮下去，直到每个孩子使唤他的日子都一般长；然后再从头轮起。只是隔三差五，赶上远近亲戚家缺人手，马拉博老爷也会把桑迪借出去；到后来，弄得桑迪从这个礼拜的末尾到下个礼拜的末尾，几乎不知道自己接着要住到谁家去。

===Original===
“One time w’en Sandy wuz lent out ez yushal, a spekilater come erlong wid a lot er niggers, en Mars Marrabo swap’ Sandy’s wife off fer a noo ’oman. W’en Sandy come back, Mars Marrabo gin ’im a dollar, en ’lowed he wuz monst’us sorry fer ter break up de fambly, but de spekilater had gin ’im big boot, en times wuz hard en money skase, en so he wuz bleedst ter make de trade. Sandy tuk on some ’bout losin’ his wife, but he soon seed dey want no use cryin’ ober spilt merlasses; en bein’ ez he lacked de looks er de noo ’oman, he tuk up wid her atter she’d be’n on de plantation a mont’ er so.

===Chinese===
「有一回，桑迪照旧被借了出去，一个人贩子（spekilater）押着一伙黑人打这儿过，马拉博老爷便拿桑迪的媳妇换走，换回一个女人。桑迪回来后，马拉博老爷给了他一块钱，说拆散人家的骨肉，他心里实在过意不去，可那贩子肯倒贴一大笔找头，如今世道艰难、现钱又紧，他没法子，只得做这笔买卖。桑迪为丢了媳妇难过了一阵，可他很快就明白，泼洒在地上的糖蜜，哭也哭不回来；加上他瞧着新来的女人模样中意，等她在庄园里住了个把月，两人便好上了。

===Original===
“Sandy en his noo wife got on mighty well tergedder, en de niggers all ’mence’ ter talk about how lovin’ dey wuz. W’en Tenie wuz tuk sick oncet, Sandy useter set up all night wid ’er, en den go ter wuk in de mawnin’ des lack he had his reg’lar sleep; en Tenie would ’a’ done anythin’ in de worl’ for her Sandy.

===Chinese===
「桑迪跟新媳妇过得和和美美，黑人们都开始谈论他们俩多么恩爱。有一回蒂妮（Tenie）病倒了，桑迪就整宿整宿守着她，天一亮照旧下地干活，跟睡足了整觉似的；而蒂妮呢，为了她的桑迪，天底下的事没有不肯做的。

===Original===
“Sandy en Tenie hadn’ be’n libbin’ tergedder fer mo’ d’n two mont’s befo’ Mars Marrabo’s old uncle, w’at libbed down in Robeson County, sent up ter fin’ out ef Mars Marrabo couldn’ len’ ’im er hire ’im a good han’ fer a mont’ er so. Sandy’s marster wuz one er dese yer easy-gwine folks w’at wanter please eve’ybody, en he says yas, he could len’ ’im Sandy. En Mars Marrabo tol’ Sandy fer ter git ready ter go down ter Robeson nex’ day, fer ter stay a mont’ er so.

===Chinese===
「桑迪和蒂妮一块儿过日子还不到两个多月，马拉博老爷的一位老叔父——他住在罗伯逊县（Robeson County）——就捎信上来，问问马拉博老爷能不能借他、或是雇他一个好劳力，用上个把月。桑迪的主人是个好说话的性子，见谁都愿意给个方便，便说行，桑迪可以借给他。于是马拉博老爷吩咐桑迪收拾收拾，第二天就下罗伯逊去，住个把月光景。

===Original===
“It wuz monst’us hard on Sandy fer ter take ’im ’way fum Tenie. It wuz so fur down ter Robeson dat he didn’ hab no chance er comin’ back ter see her tel de time wuz up; he wouldn’ ’a’ mine comin’ ten er fifteen mile at night ter see Tenie, but Mars Marrabo’s uncle’s plantation wuz mo’ d’n forty mile off. Sandy wuz mighty sad en cas’ down atter w’at Mars Marrabo tol’ ’im, en he says ter Tenie, sezee:—

===Chinese===
「要把桑迪从蒂妮身边带走，这对他实在是难熬。罗伯逊远在下头，不到期满，他一点指望回去看她都没有；夜里走个十英里十五英里来看蒂妮，他倒不嫌，可马拉博老爷叔父的庄子隔着四十多英里地呢。听了马拉博老爷的话，桑迪愁眉苦脸、垂头丧气，他对蒂妮说道——

===Original===
“‘I’m gittin’ monst’us ti’ed er dish yer gwine roun’ so much. Here I is lent ter Mars Jeems dis mont’, en I got ter do so-en-so; en ter Mars Archie de nex’ mont’, en I got ter do so-en-so; den I got ter go ter Miss Jinnie’s: en hit’s Sandy dis en Sandy dat, en Sandy yer en Sandy dere, tel it ’pears ter me I ain’ got no home, ner no marster, ner no mistiss, ner no nuffin. I can’t eben keep a wife: my yuther ole ’oman wuz sol’ away widout my gittin’ a chance fer ter tell her good-by; en now I got ter go off en leab you, Tenie, en I dunno whe’r I’m eber gwine ter see you ag’in er no. I wisht I wuz a tree, er a stump, er a rock, er sump’n w’at could stay on de plantation fer a w’ile.’

===Chinese===
「『俺这么被支来支去，实在支得够了。这个月俺借在吉姆斯老爷（Mars Jeems）家，叫俺干这干那；下个月到阿奇老爷（Mars Archie）家，又叫俺干这干那；然后还得去金妮小姐（Miss Jinnie）家：成天桑迪长、桑迪短，桑迪在这儿、桑迪在那儿，闹得俺觉着自己没家、没主人、没主母，什么都没有。俺连个媳妇都留不住：先前那个老婆被人卖掉，俺连道声再会的工夫都没捞着；如今俺又要撇下你走了，蒂妮，这一去还不知能不能再见着你的面。俺巴不得自己是棵树、是个树墩、是块石头，随便什么能在这庄园上待一阵子的东西才好。』

===Original===
“Atter Sandy got thoo talkin’, Tenie didn’ say naer word, but des sot dere by de fier, studyin’ en studyin’. Bimeby she up ’n’ says:—

“‘Sandy, is I eber tol’ you I wuz a cunjuh ’oman?’

===Chinese===
「桑迪说完了，蒂妮一声没言语，只是坐在炉火边想了又想。过了半晌，她忽地开口道——

「『桑迪，俺可曾告诉过你，俺原本是个巫女？』

===Original===
“Co’se Sandy hadn’ nebver dremp’ er nuffin lack dat, en he made a great ’miration w’en he hear w’at Tenie say. Bimeby Tenie went on:—

“‘I ain’ goophered nobody, ner done no cunjuh wuk, fer fifteen year er mo’; en w’en I got religion I made up my mine I wouldn’ wuk no mo’ goopher. But dey is some things I doan b’lieve it’s no sin fer ter do; en ef you doan wanter be sent roun’ fum pillar ter pos’, en ef you doan wanter go down ter Robeson, I kin fix things so you won’t haf ter. Ef you’ll des say de word, I kin turn you ter w’ateber you wanter be, en you kin stay right whar you wanter, ez long ez you mineter.’

===Chinese===
「桑迪做梦也没梦见过这一层，听了蒂妮的话，惊讶得不得了。过了一会儿，蒂妮接着说——

「『俺有十五年还多，没给人下过咒（goopher）、没使过巫术了；自从信了教，俺就打定主意，再不弄咒术这营生。可有几样事，俺觉得做了也不算作孽；你要是不愿被人从东家支到西家，要是不想下罗伯逊去，俺有法子叫你去不成。你只要说句话，俺就能把你变成你想变的任何东西，你想待在哪儿就待在哪儿，想待多久就待多久。』

===Original===
“Sandy say he doan keer; he’s willin’ fer ter do anythin’ fer ter stay close ter Tenie. Den Tenie ax ’im ef he doan wanter be turnt inter a rabbit.

“Sandy say, ‘No, de dogs mought git atter me.’

“‘Shill I turn you ter a wolf?’ sez Tenie.

“‘No, eve’ybody’s skeered er a wolf, en I doan want nobody ter be skeered er me.’

“‘Shill I turn you ter a mawkin’-bird?’

“‘No, a hawk mought ketch me. I wanter be turnt inter sump’n w’at’ll stay in one place.’

“‘I kin turn you ter a tree,’ sez Tenie. ‘You won’t hab no mouf ner years, but I kin turn you back oncet in a w’ile, so you kin git sump’n ter eat, en hear w’at’s gwine on.’

===Chinese===
「桑迪说他不挑；只要能守在蒂妮身边，叫他变什么都情愿。蒂妮便问他，想不想变成一只兔子。

「桑迪说：『不成，狗会撵俺。』

「『那俺把你变成一只狼？』蒂妮说。

「『不成，谁见了狼都怕，俺不想叫谁怕俺。』

「『那俺把你变成一只反舌鸟（mockingbird）？』

「『不成，老鹰会逮着俺。俺想变成个待在原地不挪窝的东西。』

「『俺能把你变成一棵树，』蒂妮说。『变成树，你没嘴也没耳朵，可俺能隔一阵子把你变回来，让你弄点吃的，听听外头出了什么事。』

===Original===
“Well, Sandy say dat’ll do. En so Tenie tuk ’im down by de aidge er de swamp, not fur fum de quarters, en turnt ’im inter a big pine-tree, en sot ’im out ’mongs’ some yuther trees. En de nex’ mawnin’, ez some er de fiel’ han’s wuz gwine long dere, dey seed a tree w’at dey didn’ ’member er habbin’ seed befo’; it wuz monst’us quare, en dey wuz bleedst ter ’low dat dey hadn’ ’membered right, er e’se one er de saplin’s had be’n growin’ monst’us fas’.

===Chinese===
「嗯，桑迪说这样就好。于是蒂妮领他到沼泽边上、离黑人住处不远的地方，把他变成一棵大松树，栽在别的树木中间。第二天早晨，几个下地的帮工打那儿路过，瞧见一棵树，不记得从前见过；这事实在邪门，他们只好认定，要么是自己记岔了，要么就是哪棵小树苗长得出奇地快。

===Original===
“W’en Mars Marrabo ’skiver’ dat Sandy wuz gone, he ’lowed Sandy had runned away. He got de dogs out, but de las’ place dey could track Sandy ter wuz de foot er dat pine-tree. En dere de dogs stood en barked, en bayed, en pawed at de tree, en tried ter climb up on it; en w’en dey wuz tuk roun’ thoo de swamp ter look fer de scent, dey broke loose en made fer dat tree ag’in. It wuz de beatenis’ thing de w’ite folks eber hearn of, en Mars Marrabo ’lowed dat Sandy must ’a’ clim’ up on de tree en jump’ off on a mule er sump’n, en rid fur ernuff fer ter spile de scent. Mars Marrabo wanted ter ’cuse some er de yuther niggers er heppin’ Sandy off, but dey all ’nied it ter de las’; en eve’ybody knowed Tenie sot too much sto’ by Sandy fer ter he’p ’im run away whar she couldn’ nebber see ’im no mo’.

===Chinese===
「马拉博老爷发觉桑迪不见了，一口咬定他是逃跑了。他把狗放了出来，可狗能把桑迪追踪到的最后一处，正是那棵松树脚下。狗就站在那儿，冲着树汪汪叫、嗥嗥嚎，用爪子扒树干，还一个劲儿往树上爬；把它们牵到沼泽里四下找气味，它们挣脱开，又直奔那棵树去。这是白人们从来没听说过的一桩邪门事，马拉博老爷说，桑迪准是爬上那棵树，又跳到一头骡子什么的背上，跑出去老远，把气味搅散了。马拉博老爷想赖到别的黑人头上，说他们帮桑迪逃走，可他们到死也不认；再说谁都明白，蒂妮把桑迪看得多重，绝不会帮他逃到她这辈子再见不着面的地方去。

===Original===
“W’en Sandy had be’n gone long ernuff fer folks ter think he done got clean away, Tenie useter go down ter de woods at night en turn ’im back, en den dey’d slip up ter de cabin en set by de fire en talk. But dey ha’ ter be monst’us keerful, er e’se somebody would ’a’ seed ’em, en dat would ’a’ spile’ de whole thing; so Tenie alluz turnt Sandy back in de mawnin’ early, befo’ anybody wuz a-stirrin’.

===Chinese===
「等桑迪失踪得够久、人人都当他跑了个干净，蒂妮就常常夜里下林子把他变回来，两人再悄悄溜回小屋，坐在炉火边说话。可他们得千万加小心，不然叫人撞见，整个事儿就全砸了；所以蒂妮总是赶在一大早、人还没起床走动之前，先把桑迪变回去。

===Original===
“But Sandy didn’ git erlong widout his trials en tribberlations. One day a woodpecker come erlong en ’mence’ ter peck at de tree; en de nex’ time Sandy wuz turnt back he had a little roun’ hole in his arm, des lack a sharp stick be’n stuck in it. Atter dat Tenie sot a sparrer-hawk fer ter watch de tree; en w’en de woodpecker come erlong nex’ mawnin’ fer ter finish his nes’, he got gobble’ up mos’ ’fo’ he stuck his bill in de bark.

===Chinese===
「不过桑迪也有他的劫难。有一天，一只啄木鸟飞来，动嘴啄那棵树；下一回桑迪被变回来，胳膊上就多了个小圆洞，活像叫尖木棍扎过一下。打那以后，蒂妮就派一只雀鹰守着那棵树；第二天早晨啄木鸟再来接着做窝，差不多嘴还没插进树皮，就叫雀鹰一口吞了。

===Original===
“Nudder time, Mars Marrabo sent a nigger out in de woods fer ter chop tuppentime boxes. De man chop a box in dish yer tree, en hack’ de bark up two er th’ee feet, fer ter let de tuppentime run. De nex’ time Sandy wuz turnt back he had a big skyar on his lef’ leg, des lack it be’n skunt; en it tuk Tenie nigh ’bout all night fer ter fix a mixtry ter kyo it up. Atter dat, Tenie sot a hawnet fer ter watch de tree; en w’en de nigger come back ag’in fer ter cut ernudder box on de yuther side’n de tree, de hawnet stung ’im so hard dat de ax slip en cut his foot nigh ’bout off.

===Chinese===
「又有一回，马拉博老爷派一个黑人下林子去砍接松脂（turpentine）的槽子。那人在桑迪这棵树上砍出一个槽，又把树皮往上豁开两三英尺，好让松脂往外淌。下一回桑迪被变回来，左腿上落了一大块疤，像蹭掉了一层皮；蒂妮调了一副药，几乎费了一整夜才把它治好。打那以后，蒂妮又派一只大黄蜂守着树；等那黑人再来要在树另一面砍第二个槽时，大黄蜂狠狠蜇了他一家伙，蜇得他手一哆嗦，斧头滑了下去，差点把他一只脚砍下来。

===Original===
“W’en Tenie see so many things happenin’ ter de tree, she ’cluded she’d ha’ ter turn Sandy ter sump’n e’se; en atter studyin’ de matter ober, en talkin’ wid Sandy one ebenin’, she made up her mine fer ter fix up a goopher mixtry w’at would turn herse’f en Sandy ter foxes, er sump’n, so dey could run away en go some’rs whar dey could be free en lib lack w’ite folks.

===Chinese===
「蒂妮见这棵树接二连三地遭殃，便断定得把桑迪变成个别的东西了；她把这事前前后后掂量了一番，又在一个傍晚跟桑迪商量妥当，打定主意要配一副咒药（goopher mixtry），把她自己和桑迪都变成狐狸什么的，好双双逃出去，逃到一个能得自由、能像白人那样过日子的地方去。

===Original===
“But dey ain’ no tellin’ w’at’s gwine ter happen in dis worl’. Tenie had got de night sot fer her en Sandy ter run away, w’en dat ve’y day one er Mars Marrabo’s sons rid up ter de big house in his buggy, en say his wife wuz monst’us sick, en he want his mammy ter len’ ’im a ’oman fer ter nuss his wife. Tenie’s mistiss say sen’ Tenie; she wuz a good nuss. Young mars wuz in a tarrible hurry fer ter git back home. Tenie wuz washin’ at de big house dat day, en her mistiss say she should go right ’long wid her young marster. Tenie tried ter make some ’scuse fer ter git away en hide ’tel night, w’en she would have eve’ything fix’ up fer her en Sandy; she say she wanter go ter her cabin fer ter git her bonnet. Her mistiss say it doan matter ’bout de bonnet; her head-hankcher wuz good ernuff. Den Tenie say she wanter git her bes’ frock; her mistiss say no, she doan need no mo’ frock, en w’en dat one got dirty she could git a clean one whar she wuz gwine. So Tenie had ter git in de buggy en go ’long wid young Mars Dunkin ter his plantation, w’ich wuz mo’ d’n twenty mile away; en dey wa’n’t no chance er her seein’ Sandy no mo’ ’tel she come back home. De po’ gal felt monst’us bad ’bout de way things wuz gwine on, en she knowed Sandy mus’ be a wond’rin’ why she didn’ come en turn ’im back no mo’.

===Chinese===
「可这世上的事，谁也说不准下一刻出什么岔子。蒂妮已经定下了她和桑迪出逃的那个晚上，偏偏就在当天，马拉博老爷的一个儿子驾着轻便马车赶到大宅，说他的媳妇病得厉害，要他母亲借个女人给他，好伺候病人。蒂妮的主母说，派蒂妮去吧，她是个顶好的看护。少爷急得火烧火燎要往家赶。那天蒂妮正在大宅里洗衣裳，主母说她这就得跟着少爷一道走。蒂妮想编个由头脱开身，躲到天黑——到夜里，她和桑迪的一切就都拾掇停当了；她说要回小屋去取帽子。主母说，帽子不帽子的不碍事，头巾包着就挺好。蒂妮又说要去取她那条最好的裙子；主母说不用，她用不着多带裙子，身上这条脏了，到地方自会有的换。就这样，蒂妮只得上了马车，跟着小邓肯少爷（Mars Dunkin）往他家庄子去，那地方隔着二十多英里；她要想再见桑迪，就只剩回到家这一条路了。可怜的姑娘眼睁睁瞧着事情走到这一步，心里苦得没法说，她知道桑迪这会儿准在犯嘀咕：怎么她再也不来把他变回去了。

===Original===
“W’iles Tenie wuz away nussin’ young Mars Dunkin’s wife, Mars Marrabo tuk a notion fer ter buil’ ’im a noo kitchen; en bein’ ez he had lots er timber on his place, he begun ter look ’roun’ fer a tree ter hab de lumber sawed out’n. En I dunno how it come to be so, but he happen fer ter hit on de ve’y tree w’at Sandy wuz turnt inter. Tenie wuz gone, en dey wa’n’t nobody ner nuffin fer ter watch de tree.

===Chinese===
「蒂妮在外头给小邓肯少爷的媳妇当看护的那些日子，马拉博老爷忽然动念要盖间新厨房；他庄子上木材多的是，便开始四处物色一棵树，好锯成木料来用。也不知怎么就这样巧，他偏偏挑中了桑迪变成的那棵树。蒂妮不在，那棵树就没人、也没个什么替它照看了。

===Original===
“De two men w’at cut de tree down say dey nebber had sech a time wid a tree befo’: dey axes would glansh off, en didn’ ’pear ter make no progress thoo de wood; en of all de creakin’, en shakin’, en wobblin’ you eber see, dat tree done it w’en it commence’ ter fall. It wuz de beatenis’ thing!

===Chinese===
「砍倒那棵树的两个人说，他们这辈子没跟一棵树这么较过劲：斧头砍上去直往下滑，在木头里像是半点也进不去；等那棵树开始倒的时候，那份吱嘎乱响、摇摇晃晃、颤颤巍巍的架势，真是你没见过、我没见过的。真是邪门透顶！

===Original===
“W’en dey got de tree all trim’ up, dey chain it up ter a timber waggin, en start fer de sawmill. But dey had a hard time gittin’ de log dere: fus’ dey got stuck in de mud w’en dey wuz gwine crosst de swamp, en it wuz two er th’ee hours befo’ dey could git out. W’en dey start’ on ag’in, de chain kep’ a-comin’ loose, en dey had ter keep a-stoppin’ en a-stoppin’ fer ter hitch de log up ag’in. W’en dey commence’ ter climb de hill ter de sawmill, de log broke loose, en roll down de hill en in ’mongs’ de trees, en hit tuk nigh ’bout half a day mo’ ter git it haul’ up ter de sawmill.

===Chinese===
「他们把树的枝杈拾掇干净，用链子拴上运木车，就动身往锯木厂去。可要把这根木头运到那儿，真够他们受的：头一桩，过沼泽时车轮陷进泥里，折腾了两三个钟头才拔出来；再往前走，链子老是一松就脱，他们只得走走停停，停下重新把木头拴牢；等上锯木厂那道坡时，木头干脆挣脱链子，滚下坡去，钻进树林子里，差不多又搭进去半个白天，才把它拖上锯木厂。

===Original===
“De nex’ mawnin’ atter de day de tree wuz haul’ ter de sawmill, Tenie come home. W’en she got back ter her cabin, de fus’ thing she done wuz ter run down ter de woods en see how Sandy wuz gittin’ on. W’en she seed de stump standin’ dere, wid de sap runnin’ out’n it, en de limbs layin’ scattered roun’, she nigh ’bout went out’n her min’. She run ter her cabin, en got her goopher mixtry, en den follered de track er de timber waggin ter de sawmill. She knowed Sandy couldn’ lib mo’ d’n a minute er so ef she turnt him back, fer he wuz all chop’ up so he’d ’a’ be’n bleedst ter die. But she wanted ter turn ’im back long ernuff fer ter ’splain ter ’im dat she hadn’ went off a-purpose, en lef’ ’im ter be chop’ down en sawed up. She didn’ want Sandy ter die wid no hard feelin’s to’ds her.

===Chinese===
「木头运到锯木厂的第二天一早，蒂妮回来了。一回到小屋，她头一件事就是奔下林子，看看桑迪怎么样了。她一眼看见树桩立在那儿，树汁顺着桩口往外淌，枝桠七零八落撒了一地，登时急得几乎没了魂。她跑回小屋，取来她的咒药，顺着运木车的辙印一路追到锯木厂。她知道，把桑迪变回来，他也活不过一分钟，因为他已经被砍得七零八碎，变回来也只有死路一条。可她想把他变回来一小会儿，跟他说明白：她不是存心走开的，不是存心扔下他让人砍倒、让人锯碎的。她不愿桑迪到死还记恨着她。

===Original===
“De han’s at de sawmill had des got de big log on de kerridge, en wuz startin’ up de saw, w’en dey seed a ’oman runnin’ up de hill, all out er bref, cryin’ en gwine on des lack she wuz plumb ’stracted. It wuz Tenie; she come right inter de mill, en th’owed herse’f on de log, right in front er de saw, a-hollerin’ en cryin’ ter her Sandy ter fergib her, en not ter think hard er her, fer it wa’n’t no fault er hern. Den Tenie ’membered de tree didn’ hab no years, en she wuz gittin’ ready fer ter wuk her goopher mixtry so ez ter turn Sandy back, w’en de mill-hands kotch holt er her en tied her arms wid a rope, en fasten’ her to one er de posts in de sawmill; en den dey started de saw up ag’in, en cut de log up inter bo’ds en scantlin’s right befo’ her eyes. But it wuz mighty hard wuk; fer of all de sweekin’, en moanin’, en groanin’, dat log done it w’iles de saw wuz a-cuttin’ thoo it. De saw wuz one er dese yer ole-timey, up-en-down saws, en hit tuk longer dem days ter saw a log ’en it do now. Dey greased de saw, but dat didn’ stop de fuss; hit kep’ right on, tel fin’ly dey got de log all sawed up.

===Chinese===
「锯木厂的工人们刚把那根大木头架上运料车，正要开锯，就见一个女人奔上坡来，气都喘不匀，一路哭喊，那模样简直全疯了。那正是蒂妮；她径直冲进锯木厂，扑到木头上，正正拦在锯子跟前，喊她的桑迪，哭着求他原谅她，别记恨她，这事一点不是她的错。接着蒂妮想起树没有耳朵，正要施起咒药把桑迪变回来，锯木工们却一把揪住了她，拿绳子捆住她的两条胳膊，把她拴在锯木厂里的一根柱子上；然后重新开动锯子，当着她的面，把那根木头锯成了一块块木板、一根根小方料。可这活儿干得实在费劲；锯子在木头里行进的时候，那木头又吱吱、又呜呜、又哼哼，什么惨声都出尽了。那锯子是老式的上下往复锯，那年月锯一根木头，比如今费的工夫长得多。他们给锯子上了油，可那响动一点没停，一直闹到最后，整根木头锯完才算消停。

===Original===
“W’en de oberseah w’at run de sawmill come fum breakfas’, de han’s up en tell him ’bout de crazy ’oman—ez dey s’posed she wuz—w’at had come runnin’ in de sawmill, a-hollerin’ en gwine on, en tried ter th’ow herse’f befo’ de saw. En de oberseah sent two er th’ee er de han’s fer ter take Tenie back ter her marster’s plantation.

===Chinese===
「管锯木厂的监工吃罢早饭回来，工人们一五一十告诉他：有个疯女人——他们只当她疯了——跑进锯木厂，又喊又叫，还想往锯子跟前扑。监工便派了两三个工人，把蒂妮送回她主人的庄园去。

===Original===
“Tenie ’peared ter be out’n her min’ fer a long time, en her marster ha’ ter lock her up in de smoke-’ouse ’tel she got ober her spells. Mars Marrabo wuz monst’us mad, en hit would ’a’ made yo’ flesh crawl fer ter hear him cuss, ’caze he say de spekilater w’at he got Tenie fum had fooled ’im by wukkin’ a crazy ’oman off on him. Wiles Tenie wuz lock up in de smoke-’ouse, Mars Marrabo tuk ’n’ haul de lumber fum de sawmill, en put up his noo kitchen.

===Chinese===
「蒂妮好长日子都显得神志不清，她主人只得把她锁在熏肉房里，等她疯病过去。马拉博老爷气得不行，他那顿咒骂你要是听见，保管皮肉发麻，因为他说，把蒂妮贩给他的那个人贩子坑了他，拿个疯女人蒙混了他。蒂妮锁在熏肉房里的那阵子，马拉博老爷把木料从锯木厂运了回来，盖起了他的新厨房。

===Original===
“W’en Tenie got quiet’ down, so she could be ’lowed ter go ’roun’ de plantation, she up’n’ tole her marster all erbout Sandy en de pine-tree; en w’en Mars Marrabo hearn it, he ’lowed she wuz de wuss ’stracted nigger he eber hearn of. He didn’ know w’at ter do wid Tenie: fus’ he thought he’d put her in de po’house; but fin’ly, seein’ ez she didn’ do no harm ter nobody ner nuffin, but des went ’roun’ moanin’, en groanin’, en shakin’ her head, he ’cluded ter let her stay on de plantation en nuss de little nigger chilluns w’en dey mammies wuz ter wuk in de cotton-fiel’.

===Chinese===
「等蒂妮安静下来，可以放她在庄园里走动了，她便把桑迪和松树的事，一五一十说给了主人听；马拉博老爷一听，断定她是他这辈子所听说的疯得最厉害的黑鬼。他不知道该拿蒂妮怎么办：起先想把她送进济贫院；可后来见她既不伤人、也不害物，只是成天唉声叹气、哼哼唧唧、摇着个头，便决定留她住在庄园里，等娃娃们的娘下棉田干活时，叫她照看那些黑人小娃娃。

===Original===
“De noo kitchen Mars Marrabo buil’ wuzn’ much use, fer it hadn’ be’n put up long befo’ de niggers ’mence’ ter notice quare things erbout it. Dey could hear sump’n moanin’ en groanin’ ’bout de kitchen in de night-time, en w’en de win’ would blow dey could hear sump’n a-hollerin’ en sweekin’ lack it wuz in great pain en sufferin’. En it got so atter a w’ile dat it wuz all Mars Marrabo’s wife could do ter git a ’oman ter stay in de kitchen in the daytime long ernuff fer ter do de cookin’; en dey wa’n’t naer nigger on de plantation w’at wouldn’ rudder take forty dan ter go ’bout dat kitchen atter dark—dat is, ’cep’n’ Tenie; she didn’ ’pear ter min’ de ha’nts. She useter slip ’roun’ at night, en set on de kitchen steps, en lean up agin de do’-jamb, en run on ter herse’f wid some kine er foolishness w’at nobody couldn’ make out; fer Mars Marrabo had th’eaten’ ter sen’ her off’n de plantation ef she say anything ter any er de yuther niggers ’bout de pine-tree. But somehow er ’nudder de niggers foun’ out all erbout it, en dey all knowed de kitchen wuz ha’nted by Sandy’s sperrit. En bimeby hit got so Mars Marrabo’s wife herse’f wuz skeered ter go out in de yard atter dark.

===Chinese===
「马拉博老爷盖的那间新厨房没派上多大用场，因为落成没多久，黑人们就开始察觉它四下里邪气。夜里，他们听得见有什么东西围着厨房呜呜咽咽、哼哼唧唧；刮风的天气，还听得见什么东西在嚎、在尖叫，像是在受大苦楚。过了些时候，连白天里劝个女人在厨房待够了做完饭的工夫，都得马拉博老爷的太太好说歹说；庄园上没有一个黑人，宁肯挨四十鞭，也不肯天黑以后走近那厨房——只有蒂妮例外；她像是并不忌讳鬼魂（ha'nt）。她常常夜里悄悄溜到那儿，坐在厨房台阶上，倚着门框，自顾自絮叨些谁也听不明白的疯话；因为马拉博老爷放了话，她要是跟旁的黑人提起松树半个字，就把她撵出庄园去。可是不知怎么七拐八绕，黑人们还是把这事摸了个底儿掉，人人都知道那厨房是桑迪的鬼魂在作祟。到后来，连马拉博老爷的太太自己，天一擦黑也不敢到院子里去了。

===Original===
“W’en it come ter dat, Mars Marrabo tuk en to’ de kitchen down, en use’ de lumber fer ter buil’ dat ole school’ouse w’at you er talkin’ ’bout pullin’ down. De school’ouse wuzn’ use’ ’cep’n’ in de daytime, en on dark nights folks gwine ’long de road would hear quare soun’s en see quare things. Po’ ole Tenie useter go down dere at night, en wander ’roun’ de school’ouse; en de niggers all ’lowed she went fer ter talk wid Sandy’s sperrit. En one winter mawnin’, w’en one er de boys went ter school early fer ter start de fire, w’at should he fin’ but po’ ole Tenie, layin’ on de flo’, stiff, en col’, en dead. Dere didn’ ’pear ter be nuffin pertickler de matter wid her—she had des grieve’ herse’f ter def fer her Sandy. Mars Marrabo didn’ shed no tears. He thought Tenie wuz crazy, en dey wa’n’t no tellin’ w’at she mought do nex’; en dey ain’ much room in dis worl’ fer crazy w’ite folks, let ’lone a crazy nigger.

===Chinese===
「闹到这个地步，马拉博老爷索性把厨房拆了，用那些木料盖起了你们正念叨着要拆的那座旧校舍。校舍只有白天有人用用，可一到没有月光的黑夜里，过路的人就会听见怪声、瞧见怪影。可怜的老蒂妮常在夜里下到那儿，围着校舍转来转去；黑人们都说，她是去跟桑迪的鬼魂说话。一个冬天的早晨，有个孩子早早到校生炉子，你猜他瞧见了什么——可怜的老蒂妮，直挺挺、冷冰冰地躺在地上，死了。她身上瞧不出什么特别的病症——她只是为了她的桑迪，把自己活活愁死了。马拉博老爷一滴眼泪也没掉。他认定蒂妮是个疯子，谁知道她下一步还会做出什么来；这世上连疯了的白人都没多少立足的地方，更别提一个疯了的黑人。

===Original===
“Hit wa’n’t long atter dat befo’ Mars Marrabo sol’ a piece er his track er lan’ ter Mars Dugal’ McAdoo—*my* ole marster—en dat’s how de ole school’ouse happen to be on yo’ place. W’en de wah broke out, de school stop’, en de ole school’ouse be’n stannin’ empty ever sence—dat is, ’cep’n’ fer de ha’nts. En folks sez dat de ole school’ouse, er any yuther house w’at got any er dat lumber in it w’at wuz sawed out’n de tree w’at Sandy wuz turnt inter, is gwine ter be ha’nted tel de las’ piece er plank is rotted en crumble’ inter dus’.”

===Chinese===
「那以后没过多久，马拉博老爷就把他地里的一块卖给了杜加尔·麦卡杜（Dugal McAdoo）老爷——正是俺的老主人——你们那块地上的旧校舍就是这么来的。南北战争一打起来，学堂也散了，旧校舍从此一直空着——只除了里头的鬼魂。人们还说，那座旧校舍，或者随便哪座房子，只要用了那棵树——就是桑迪变成的那棵——上头锯出来的木料，就得一直闹鬼，闹到最后一块木板朽透、碎成灰土才算完。」

===Original===
Annie had listened to this gruesome narrative with strained attention.

“What a system it was,” she exclaimed, when Julius had finished, “under which such things were possible!”

“What things?” I asked, in amazement. “Are you seriously considering the possibility of a man’s being turned into a tree?”

“Oh, no,” she replied quickly, “not that;” and then she murmured absently, and with a dim look in her fine eyes, “Poor Tenie!”

===Chinese===
安妮屏息凝神，听完了这个令人毛骨悚然的故事。

「那是怎样的一种制度啊，」朱利叶斯讲完时，她感叹道，「这种事情居然会在它底下发生！」

「什么事情？」我诧异地问，「你总不会当真在考虑一个人变成树的可能性吧？」

「哦，不，」她赶紧答道，「不是说那个；」随后她心不在焉地喃喃道，那双好看的眼睛里蒙了一层幽幽的光，「可怜的蒂妮！」

===Original===
We ordered the lumber, and returned home. That night, after we had gone to bed, and my wife had to all appearances been sound asleep for half an hour, she startled me out of an incipient doze by exclaiming suddenly—

===Chinese===
我们订好木料，回了家。当天夜里，我们上床之后，我妻子看样子已经酣睡了半个钟头，忽然一声惊呼，把我从将睡未睡的迷糊里吓了出来——

===Original===
“John, I don’t believe I want my new kitchen built out of the lumber in that old schoolhouse.”

“You wouldn’t for a moment allow yourself,” I replied, with some asperity, “to be influenced by that absurdly impossible yarn which Julius was spinning today?”

“I know the story is absurd,” she replied dreamily, “and I am not so silly as to believe it. But I don’t think I should ever be able to take any pleasure in that kitchen if it were built out of that lumber. Besides, I think the kitchen would look better and last longer if the lumber were all new.”

===Chinese===
「约翰，我想我还是不要用那座旧校舍的木料来盖我的新厨房了。」

「你总不至于，」我有些恼火地答道，「让朱利叶斯今天编的那套荒诞得没边儿的故事给说动了吧？」

「我知道这故事荒唐，」她恍恍惚惚地答道，「我也没那么傻，不至于信它。可要是厨房是用那批木料盖的，我想我永远也没法在那里面寻一点乐趣。再说，木料要全是新的，厨房会更好看，也更耐用。」

===Original===
Of course she had her way. I bought the new lumber, though not without grumbling. A week or two later I was called away from home on business. On my return, after an absence of several days, my wife remarked to me—

===Chinese===
当然，最后依了她。我买下了新木料，尽管不是没有嘟囔。一两个星期后，我因事出门。离家几天回来时，我妻子对我说——

===Original===
“John, there has been a split in the Sandy Run Colored Baptist Church, on the temperance question. About half the members have come out from the main body, and set up for themselves. Uncle Julius is one of the seceders, and he came to me yesterday and asked if they might not hold their meetings in the old schoolhouse for the present.”

===Chinese===
「约翰，桑迪溪有色人种浸信会（Sandy Run Colored Baptist Church）在禁酒问题上闹分裂了。大约一半教友从主体里退了出来，自立了门户。朱利叶斯大叔就是分裂出去的那一拨里的，他昨天来找我，问他们眼下能不能就在那座旧校舍里聚会。」

===Original===
“I hope you didn’t let the old rascal have it,” I returned, with some warmth. I had just received a bill for the new lumber I had bought.

===Chinese===
「我希望你没把房子给那个老滑头，」我语气很冲地回道。我刚收到新买木料的账单。

===Original===
“Well,” she replied, “I couldn’t refuse him the use of the house for so good a purpose.”

“And I’ll venture to say,” I continued, “that you subscribed something toward the support of the new church?”

===Chinese===
「唉，」她答道，「为了这么正经的用途，我总不好不借他房子吧。」

「而且我敢说，」我接着道，「你还捐了钱贴补那个新教堂？」

===Original===
She did not attempt to deny it.

“What are they going to do about the ghost?” I asked, somewhat curious to know how Julius would get around this obstacle.

===Chinese===
她没有想否认。

「那他们打算拿那个鬼怎么办？」我问道，心里颇为好奇，想知道朱利叶斯会怎么绕过这道坎。

===Original===
“Oh,” replied Annie, “Uncle Julius says that ghosts never disturb religious worship, but that if Sandy’s spirit *should* happen to stray into meeting by mistake, no doubt the preaching would do it good.”

===Chinese===
「哦，」安妮答道，「朱利叶斯大叔说，鬼魂从不搅扰敬拜；不过，桑迪的魂儿要是*碰巧*走岔了门、误进了聚会，那讲道对它一定有好处。」

## Mars Jeems’s Nightmare / 詹姆斯老爷的噩梦

===Original===
We found old Julius very useful when we moved to our new residence. He had a thorough knowledge of the neighborhood, was familiar with the roads and the watercourses, knew the qualities of the various soils and what they would produce, and where the best hunting and fishing were to be had. He was a marvelous hand in the management of horses and dogs, with whose mental processes he manifested a greater familiarity than mere use would seem to account for, though it was doubtless due to the simplicity of a life that had kept him close to nature. Toward my tract of land and the things that were on it﻿—the creeks, the swamps, the hills, the meadows, the stones, the trees﻿—he maintained a peculiar personal attitude, that might be called predial rather than proprietary. He had been accustomed, until long after middle life, to look upon himself as the property of another. When this relation was no longer possible, owing to the war, and to his master’s death and the dispersion of the family, he had been unable to break off entirely the mental habits of a lifetime, but had attached himself to the old plantation, of which he seemed to consider himself an appurtenance. We found him useful in many ways and entertaining in others, and my wife and I took quite a fancy to him.

Shortly after we became established in our home on the sand-hills, Julius brought up to the house one day a colored boy of about seventeen, whom he introduced as his grandson, and for whom he solicited employment. I was not favorably impressed by the youth’s appearance﻿—quite the contrary, in fact; but mainly to please the old man I hired Tom﻿—his name was Tom﻿—to help about the stables, weed the garden, cut wood and bring water, and in general to make himself useful about the outdoor work of the household.

My first impression of Tom proved to be correct. He turned out to be very trifling, and I was much annoyed by his laziness, his carelessness, and his apparent lack of any sense of responsibility. I kept him longer than I should, on Julius’s account, hoping that he might improve; but he seemed to grow worse instead of better, and when I finally reached the limit of my patience, I discharged him.

===Chinese===
我们搬到新居之后，发现老朱利叶斯十分有用。他对左近一带了如指掌，熟识各条道路和水路，知道各种土壤的品性、能长什么，也知道哪里能猎到最好的野物、钓到最好的鱼。他打理起马匹和狗来是把好手，对牲口心思的了解，似乎远非单凭使唤所能解释——不过这无疑要归功于他那亲近自然、简朴一生的生涯。对于我那片地产和地上的一切——溪流、沼泽、山丘、草场、石头、树木——他抱着一种奇特的态度，与其说是主人的气派，不如说是随庄地附属之物的做派。直到中年过后很久，他都习惯把自己看作别人的财产。后来因为战争、主人的去世和主家的离散，这层关系再也无法维系，他却终究改不掉一辈子养成的心理积习，依旧依附着那座老种植园，仿佛自认为是庄园的一件附属物。我们在许多事情上觉得他有用，在别的事情上又觉得他有趣，我和妻子都相当喜欢他。

我们在沙丘上的新家安顿下来后不久，有一天朱利叶斯领了个十七岁上下的有色男孩到宅子里来，介绍说这是他的孙子，想给他讨份差事。这小伙子的相貌没给我留下什么好印象——事实上恰恰相反；但主要为了让老人高兴，我还是雇下了汤姆（Tom）——他名叫汤姆——叫他在马棚帮工、给菜园除草、劈柴挑水，总之在家里的户外活计上样样搭把手。

我对汤姆的第一印象果然没有错。他这人是真吊儿郎当，他的懒惰、马虎，还有那副显然毫无责任心的样子，都叫我大为恼火。看在朱利叶斯的份上，我留他留得比应该的更久，指望他能长进；可他非但不见好，反倒越发糟糕，等我的耐性终于到了头，便把他辞退了。

===Original===
“I am sorry, Julius,” I said to the old man; “I should have liked to oblige you by keeping him; but I can’t stand Tom any longer. He is absolutely untrustworthy.”

“Yas, suh,” replied Julius, with a deep sigh and a long shake of the head, “I knows he ain’ much account, en dey ain’ much ’pen’ence ter be put on ’im. But I wuz hopin’ dat you mought make some ’lowance fuh a’ ign’ant young nigger, suh, en gib ’im one mo’ chance.”

But I had hardened my heart. I had always been too easily imposed upon, and had suffered too much from this weakness. I determined to be firm as a rock in this instance.

“No, Julius,” I rejoined decidedly, “it is impossible. I gave him more than a fair trial, and he simply won’t do.”

===Chinese===
“对不起，朱利叶斯，”我对老人说，“我本想把他留下来，全你的情面；可我实在受不了汤姆了。他根本就靠不住。”

“是，先生，”朱利叶斯答道，深深叹了口气，还摇了半天头，“俺晓得他不中用，也指望不上他。可俺原想着，您老兴许能担待担待，看在一个没见识的年轻黑奴份上，先生，再赏他一回机会。”

可我已经横下心了。我一向太好说话，为这毛病吃的亏也够多了。这一回我打定主意，要硬得像块石头。

“不行，朱利叶斯，”我断然答道，“没商量了。我给他的考验早已宽出了格，他就是不成器。”

===Original===
When my wife and I set out for our drive in the cool of the evening﻿—afternoon is “evening” in Southern parlance﻿—one of the servants put into the rockaway two large earthenware jugs. Our drive was to be down through the swamp to the mineral spring at the foot of the sand-hills beyond. The water of this spring was strongly impregnated with sulphur and iron, and, while not particularly agreeable of smell or taste, was used by us, in moderation, for sanitary reasons.

When we reached the spring, we found a man engaged in cleaning it out. In answer to an inquiry he said that if we would wait five or ten minutes, his task would be finished and the spring in such condition that we could fill our jugs. We might have driven on, and come back by way of the spring, but there was a bad stretch of road beyond, and we concluded to remain where we were until the spring should be ready. We were in a cool and shady place. It was often necessary to wait awhile in North Carolina; and our Northern energy had not been entirely proof against the influences of climate and local custom.

===Chinese===
傍晚凉爽时分，我和妻子驱车出游——按南方人的说法，下午便算“傍晚”——一个仆人往四轮敞篷马车里放了两只大陶罐。我们这趟车是要穿过沼泽地，到那边沙丘脚下的矿泉去。这泉水的硫磺味和铁腥味都很重，气味滋味都谈不上可口，但为了保健的缘故，我们还是适量饮用。

到了泉边，只见一个人正在清理泉眼。他应答我们的询问说，只要等上五到十分钟，他的活儿就完了，泉眼也能收拾得让我们灌水。我们本可以驱车先走，回头再绕到泉边来，但前头有一段路不好走，我们便决定原地等着，等泉眼收拾停当。我们所在的地方清凉背阴。在北卡罗来纳，等上一等是常有的事；就连我们北方人的一股锐气，也未能完全顶得住气候与地方风气的浸染。

===Original===
While we sat there, a man came suddenly around a turn of the road ahead of us. I recognized in him a neighbor with whom I had exchanged formal calls. He was driving a horse, apparently a high-spirited creature, possessing, so far as I could see at a glance, the marks of good temper and good breeding; the gentleman, I had heard it suggested, was slightly deficient in both. The horse was rearing and plunging, and the man was beating him furiously with a buggy-whip. When he saw us, he flushed a fiery red, and, as he passed, held the reins with one hand, at some risk to his safety, lifted his hat, and bowed somewhat constrainedly as the horse darted by us, still panting and snorting with fear.

===Chinese===
我们坐在那儿，忽见前方路上一个人影从弯道后头转了出来。我认出那是位邻居，我们彼此正式拜会过。他正驾着一匹马，那显然是匹烈性子的牲口，据我一眼所见，颇具好脾性和良种血统的气象；至于这位绅士，我听人议论过，这两样他都稍欠了些。那马又是人立又是暴跳，那人抡着马车鞭没命地抽它。一见我们，他腾地涨红了脸，经过时单手挽缰——颇有些不顾自身的安危——举起帽子，颇为局促地欠了欠身，那马打我们身边一窜而过，兀自吓得直喘直打响鼻。

===Original===
“He looks as though he were ashamed of himself,” I observed.

“I’m sure he ought to be,” exclaimed my wife indignantly. “I think there is no worse sin and no more disgraceful thing than cruelty.”

“I quite agree with you,” I assented.

“A man w’at ’buses his hoss is gwine ter be ha’d on de folks w’at wuks fer ’im,” remarked Julius. “Ef young Mistah McLean doan min’, he’ll hab a bad dream one er dese days, des lack ’is grandaddy had way back yander, long yeahs befo’ de wah.”

===Chinese===
“他看上去倒像是自知理亏。”我说。

“他确实该臊得慌，”我妻子愤然道，“依我看，再没有比残忍更重的罪、更丢脸的事了。”

“我完全同意你的看法。”我附和道。

“虐待马儿的人，对替他做工的人也狠得下心，”朱利叶斯搭话说，“小麦克莱恩先生（McLean）要是不当心，早晚也要摊上一个噩梦，就跟他爷爷当年做的一样——那是老早老早以前的事喽，南北战争前好多年哩。”

===Original===
“What was it about Mr. McLean’s dream, Julius?” I asked. The man had not yet finished cleaning the spring, and we might as well put in time listening to Julius as in any other way. We had found some of his plantation tales quite interesting.

===Chinese===
“麦克莱恩先生的梦是怎么回事，朱利叶斯？”我问道。那人还没有清理完泉眼，与其干耗着，不如听听朱利叶斯讲，我们倒也觉得他的一些种植园故事挺有意思。

===Original===
“Mars Jeems McLean,” said Julius, “wuz de grandaddy er dis yer gent’eman w’at is des gone by us beatin’ his hoss. He had a big plantation en a heap er niggers. Mars Jeems wuz a ha’d man, en monst’us stric’ wid his han’s. Eber sence he growed up he nebber ’peared ter hab no feelin’ fer nobody. W’en his daddy, ole Mars John McLean, died, de plantation en all de niggers fell ter young Mars Jeems. He had be’n bad ’nuff befo’, but it wa’n’t long atterwa’ds ’tel he got so dey wuz no use in libbin’ at all ef you ha’ ter lib roun’ Mars Jeems. His niggers wuz bleedzd ter slabe fum daylight ter da’k, w’iles yuther folks’s didn’ hafter wuk ’cep’n’ fum sun ter sun; en dey didn’ git no mo’ ter eat dan dey oughter, en dat de coa’ses’ kin’.
