「彭伯顿上校挠着头，盘算上哪儿去张罗这一千块钱；他一时想不出辙，因为凭他拿得出的抵押，能借的钱他早已借了个尽。可他就是一门心思要定那匹马，于是说道——

===Original===
“ ‘I’ll gib you my note fer’ ’leven hund’ed dollahs fer dat hoss.’

“De yuther man shuck ’is head, en sezee:﻿—

“ ‘Yo’ note, suh, is better ’n gol’, I doan doubt; but I is made it a rule in my bizness not ter take no notes fum nobody. Howsomeber, suh, ef you is kinder sho’t er fun’s, mos’ lackly we kin make some kin’ er bahg’in. En w’iles we is talkin’, I mought ’s well say dat I needs ernudder good nigger down on my place. Ef you is got a good one ter spar’, I mought trade wid you.’

===Chinese===
「『我给你打张一千一百块的本票，换那匹马。』

「那另一位摇了摇头，说道——

「『您的本票，先生，比金子还靠得住，这我不怀疑；可我做买卖立下过规矩，谁的票据也不收。不过呢，先生，您要是手头有些紧，咱们多半还能想出个两便的买卖。趁这会儿谈着，我不妨顺便直说：我庄园里还缺一个好黑奴。您要是有富余的好手，咱们兴许能换一换。』

===Original===
“Now, Kunnel Pen’leton didn’ r’ally hab no niggers fer ter spar’, but he ’lowed ter hisse’f he wuz des bleedzd ter hab dat hoss, en so he sez, sezee:﻿—

“ ‘Well, I doan lack ter, but I reckon I’ll haf ter. You come out ter my plantation ter-morrer en look ober my niggers, en pick out de one you wants.’

===Chinese===
「其实呢，彭伯顿上校手上哪有富余的黑人，可他心里想，自己横竖是非有那匹马不可，便说道——

「『唉，我本不愿意，可看样子也只好如此。你明儿到我庄园上来，把我的黑人们都过过眼，挑一个你中意的。』

===Original===
“So sho’ ’nuff nex’ day dis yer man come out ter Kunnel Pen’leton’s place en rid roun’ de plantation en glanshed at de niggers, en who sh’d he pick out fum ’em all but Sis’ Becky.

“ ‘I needs a noo nigger ’oman down ter my place,’ sezee, ‘fer ter cook en wash, en so on; en dat young ’oman’ll des fill de bill. You gimme her, en you kin hab Lightnin’ Bug.’ ”

===Chinese===
「果不其然，第二天这人来到彭伯顿上校的庄园，骑着马在庄子四下里转，把黑人们扫了一眼；那么些人里他不挑别人，偏偏挑中了贝琪大姐。

「『我庄园里正缺个新来的黑人妇女，』他说，『烧饭啦、洗衣啦这些活计；这个年轻女人正合适。你把她给我，闪电虫就归你。』

===Original===
“Now, Kunnel Pen’leton didn’ lack ter trade Sis’ Becky, ’ca’se she wuz nigh ’bout de bes’ fiel’-han’ he had; en ’sides, Mars Kunnel didn’ keer ter take de mammies ’way fum dey chillun w’iles de chillun wuz little. But dis man say he want Becky, er e’se Kunnel Pen’leton couldn’ hab de race hoss.

“ ‘Well,’ sez de kunnel, ‘you kin hab de ’oman. But I doan lack ter sen’ her ’way fum her baby. W’at’ll you gimme fer dat nigger baby?’

===Chinese===
「彭伯顿上校本不想拿贝琪大姐做这笔交易，因为她差不多是他手上最好的田里劳力；再说，上校老爷向来不肯在孩子还小的时候，把当娘的从孩子身边拉走。可这人说他就要贝琪，不然彭伯顿上校就休想得到那匹赛马。

「『好吧，』上校说，『这个女人你带走。可我不大乐意叫她离开她的娃娃。这个黑人娃娃，你肯出个什么价？』

===Original===
“ ‘I doan want de baby,’ sez de yuther man. ‘I ain’ got no use fer de baby.’

“ ‘I tell yer w’at I’ll do,’ ’lows Kunnel Pen’leton, ‘I’ll th’ow dat pickaninny in fer good measure.’

“But de yuther man shuck his head. ‘No,’ sezee, ‘I’s much erbleedzd, but I doan raise niggers; I raises hosses, en I doan wanter be both’rin’ wid no nigger babies. Nemmine de baby. I’ll keep dat ’oman so busy she’ll fergit de baby; fer niggers is made ter wuk, en dey ain’ got no time fer no sich foolis’ness ez babies.’

===Chinese===
「『娃娃我不要，』那人说。『娃娃对我没有用处。』

「『那这么办，』彭伯顿上校说，『我把那黑娃娃饶上，算个添头。』

「可那人直摇头。『不，』他说，『多谢您的好意，可我不养黑奴；我养的是马，我可不想让黑人娃娃来纠缠。娃娃就免了。我会叫那女人忙得把娃娃都忘掉；黑奴天生就是干活的，可没工夫在娃娃这种蠢事上费神。』

===Original===
“Kunnel Pen’leton didn’ wanter hu’t Becky’s feelin’s﻿—fer Kunnel Pen’leton wuz a kin’-hea’ted man, en nebber lack’ ter make no trouble fer nobody﻿—en so he tol’ Becky he wuz gwine sen’ her down ter Robeson County fer a day er so, ter he’p out his son-in-law in his wuk; en bein’ ez dis yuther man wuz gwine dat way, he had ax’ ’im ter take her ’long in his buggy.

===Chinese===
「彭伯顿上校不愿伤贝琪的心——因为上校是个心慈面软的人，从不愿叫任何人为难——于是他告诉贝琪，说要打发她去罗伯逊县待上一天半天，帮他女婿做些活计；又正好这人也往那边去，他便求他顺路用轻便马车把她捎上。

===Original===
“ ‘Kin I kyar little Mose wid me, marster?’ ax’ Sis’ Becky.

“ ‘N-o,’ sez de kunnel, ez ef he wuz studyin’ whuther ter let her take ’im er no; ‘I reckon you better let Aun’ Nancy look atter yo’ baby fer de day er two you’ll be gone, en she’ll see dat he gits ernuff ter eat ’tel you gits back.’

===Chinese===
「『俺能把小摩西带在身边一起去吗，老爷？』贝琪大姐问。

「『呃——不行，』上校说，那口气像是正掂量着让不让她带；『我看你走的这一两天，娃娃还是交给南希大娘照看吧，她会留神让他吃得饱饱的，一直撑到你回来。』

===Original===
“So Sis’ Becky hug’ en kiss’ little Mose, en tol’ ’im ter be a good little pickaninny, en take keer er hisse’f, en not fergit his mammy w’iles she wuz gone. En little Mose put his arms roun’ his mammy en lafft en crowed des lack it wuz monst’us fine fun fer his mammy ter go ’way en leabe ’im.

===Chinese===
「于是贝琪大姐搂住小摩西亲了又亲，嘱咐他做个乖乖的黑娃娃，自己照看自己，娘不在家的日子里可别把娘给忘了。小摩西张开小胳膊搂住他娘，又是笑又是欢叫，倒好像娘要出门撇下他，是天底下顶好玩的乐事似的。

===Original===
“Well, dis yer hoss trader sta’ted out wid Becky, en bimeby, atter dey’d gone down de Lumbe’ton Road fer a few miles er so, dis man tu’nt roun’ in a diffe’nt d’rection, en kep’ goin’ dat erway, ’tel bimeby Sis’ Becky up ’n ax’ ’im ef he wuz gwine’ ter Robeson County by a noo road.

===Chinese===
「就这样，这马贩子带上贝琪上了路。走着走着，顺着伦伯顿路下去了几英里，这人却拐上另一个方向，只管照那么走下去；到后来贝琪大姐忍不住开了口，问他去罗伯逊县是不是改走了一条新道。

===Original===
“ ‘No, nigger,’ sezee, ‘I ain’ gwine ter Robeson County at all. I’s gwine ter Bladen County, whar my plantation is, en whar I raises all my hosses.’

“ ‘But how is I gwine ter git ter Mis’ Laura’s plantation down in Robeson County?’ sez Becky, wid her hea’t in her mouf, fer she ’mence’ ter git skeered all er a sudden.

===Chinese===
「『不，黑鬼，』他说，『我根本不是去罗伯逊县。我是去布莱登县（Bladen County），我的庄子在那儿，我养的马也全在那儿。』

「『那俺怎么去罗伯逊县底下劳拉太太（Mis' Laura）的庄子呀？』贝琪说，心一下子提到了嗓子眼，因为她猛然害怕起来。

===Original===
“ ‘You ain’ gwine ter git dere at all,’ sez de man. ‘You b’longs ter me now, fer I done traded my bes’ race hoss fer you, wid yo’ ole marster. Ef you is a good gal, I’ll treat you right, en ef you doan behabe yo’se’f﻿—w’y, w’at e’se happens’ll be yo’ own fault.’

===Chinese===
「『你根本就去不成那儿，』那人说。『你如今是我的人了，我拿我最好的赛马，跟你旧主人把你换了来。你要是个乖觉的女人，我自会好好待你；你要是不安分——哼，往后落个什么下场，可全是你自找的。』

===Original===
“Co’se Sis’ Becky cried en went on ’bout her pickaninny, but co’se it didn’ do no good, en bimeby dey got down ter dis yer man’s place, en he put Sis’ Becky ter wuk, en fergot all ’bout her habin’ a pickaninny.

===Chinese===
「贝琪大姐自是为她的黑娃娃哭了一场、闹了一场，可哭闹自然全不管用。不多时他们到了这人的庄子，他把贝琪大姐打发去干活，把她说有娃娃这回事忘了个一干二净。

===Original===
“Meanw’iles, w’en ebenin’ come, de day Sis’ Becky wuz tuk ’way, little Mose mence’ ter git res’less, en bimeby, w’en his mammy didn’ come, he sta’ted ter cry fer ’er. Aun’ Nancy fed ’im en rocked ’im en rocked ’im, en fin’lly he des cried en cried ’tel he cried hisse’f ter sleep.

===Chinese===
「这头呢，贝琪大姐被带走的当天，天一擦黑，小摩西就烦躁起来；等来等去不见他娘回来，他就扯开嗓子哭着要娘。南希大娘喂他、摇他，摇了又摇，到末了他还是哭啊哭啊，一直哭到自己睡着了才罢。

===Original===
“De nex’ day he didn’ ’pear ter be as peart ez yushal, en w’en night come he fretted en went on wuss ’n he did de night befo’. De nex’ day his little eyes ’mence’ ter lose dey shine, en he wouldn’ eat nuffin, en he ’mence’ ter look so peaked dat Aun’ Nancy tuk ’n kyared ’im up ter de big house, en showed ’im ter her ole missis, en her ole missis gun her some med’cine fer ’im, en ’lowed ef he didn’ git no better she sh’d fetch ’im up ter de big house ag’in, en dey’d hab a doctor, en nuss little Mose up dere. Fer Aun’ Nancy’s ole missis ’lowed he wuz a lackly little nigger en wu’th raisin’.

===Chinese===
「第二天，他看着就不如往常欢实；到了夜里，又哭又闹，比头一晚闹得还凶。再过一天，他那双小眼睛开始失了光亮，东西也不肯吃了，眼见着瘦损下去，憔悴得不像样。南希大娘便抱着他上大宅去，抱给他旧主母看。旧主母给了她一些药，还说要是他不见好，就把他再抱上大宅来，请个医生，把小摩西放在上头好生看护将养。因为南希大娘的旧主母说，这黑人小娃儿有活气，是养得起来的。

===Original===
“But Aun’ Nancy had l’arn’ ter lack little Mose, en she didn’ wanter hab ’im tuk up ter de big house. En so w’en he didn’ git no better, she gethered a mess er green peas, and tuk de peas en de baby, en went ter see ole Aun’ Peggy, de cunjuh ’oman down by de Wim’l’ton Road. She gun Aun’ Peggy de mess er peas, en tol’ her all ’bout Sis’ Becky en little Mose.

===Chinese===
「可南希大娘早已打心眼里喜欢上小摩西，不愿意让他给抱上大宅去。于是眼看他还不见好，她就摘了一捧青豌豆，一手豌豆、一手娃娃，去见住在威明顿大道边上的那位老巫女佩姬大娘。她把那捧豌豆奉给佩姬大娘，把贝琪大姐和小摩西的事，原原本本讲给她听。

===Original===
“ ‘Dat is a monst’us small mess er peas you is fotch’ me,’ sez Aun’ Peggy, sez she.

“ ‘Yas, I knows,’ ’lowed Aun’ Nancy, ‘but dis yere is a monst’us small pickaninny.’

“ ‘You’ll hafter fetch me sump’n mo’,’ sez Aun’ Peggy, ‘fer you can’t ’spec’ me ter was’e my time diggin’ roots en wukkin’ cunj’ation fer nuffin.’

“ ‘All right,’ sez Aun’ Nancy, ‘I’ll fetch you sump’n mo’ nex’ time.’

“ ‘You bettah,’ sez Aun’ Peggy, ‘er e’se dey’ll be trouble. Wat dis yer little pickaninny needs is ter see his mammy. You leabe ’im heah ’tel ebenin’ en I’ll show ’im his mammy.’

===Chinese===
「『你给我捎来的这捧豌豆，可真够小的，』佩姬大娘说。

「『是啊，俺知道，』南希大娘应道，『可这儿这个黑娃娃，也真够小的呀。』

「『下回你得再给我捎点儿别的来，』佩姬大娘说，『你不能指望俺白挖草根、白施咒作法，糟蹋俺的工夫。』

「『行，』南希大娘说，『下回俺再给您捎点儿别的来。』

「『那你最好记着点儿，』佩姬大娘说，『不然可有你麻烦的。这黑娃娃缺的是见一见他娘。你把他撂在这儿待到天黑，俺就叫他见着他娘。』

===Original===
“So w’en Aun’ Nancy had gone ’way, Aun’ Peggy tuk ’n wukked her roots, en tu’nt little Mose ter a hummin’-bird, en sont ’im off fer ter fin’ his mammy.

===Chinese===
「南希大娘走了之后，佩姬大娘便作起法来，摆弄她的草根，把小摩西变成一只蜂鸟，放他飞去寻他娘。

===Original===
“So little Mose flewed, en flewed, en flewed away, ’tel bimeby he got ter de place whar Sis’ Becky b’longed. He seed his mammy wukkin’ roun’ de ya’d, en he could tell fum lookin’ at her dat she wuz trouble’ in her min’ ’bout sump’n, en feelin’ kin’ er po’ly. Sis’ Becky heared sump’n hummin’ roun’ en roun’ her, sweet en low. Fus’ she ’lowed it wuz a hummin’-bird; den she thought it sounded lack her little Mose croonin’ on her breas’ way back yander on de ole plantation. En she des ’magine’ it wuz her little Mose, en it made her feel bettah, en she went on ’bout her wuk pearter ’n she’d done sence she’d be’n down dere. Little Mose stayed roun’ ’tel late in de ebenin’, en den flewed back ez hard ez he could ter Aun’ Peggy. Ez fer Sis’ Becky, she dremp all dat night dat she wuz holdin’ her pickaninny in her arms, en kissin’ him, en nussin’ him, des lack she useter do back on de ole plantation whar he wuz bawn. En fer th’ee er fo’ days Sis’ Becky went ’bout her wuk wid mo’ sperrit dan she’d showed sence she’d be’n down dere ter dis man’s plantation.

===Chinese===
「小摩西飞呀，飞呀，一个劲儿往远里飞，飞到后来，到了贝琪大姐落脚的地方。他看见他娘在院子里做活，一看那样子就知道，她心里正为什么事犯愁，人也恹恹的没精神。贝琪大姐听见有什么东西围着她一圈一圈地嗡嗡，声音又轻又甜。起初她只当是只蜂鸟；后来又觉得，那声音像极了老远老远以前，在老庄园上，她的小摩西伏在她胸口咿咿呀呀。她索性认定那就是她的小摩西，心里便好受了些，做起活来，也比自打到了这地方之后利落欢快。小摩西在近旁一直待到天色向晚，才拼尽全力飞回佩姬大娘那里去。至于贝琪大姐，那一整夜她都梦见自己把黑娃娃搂在怀里，亲他、哺他，就同从前在老庄园上——他出生的那个地方——一模一样。接连三四天，贝琪大姐做起活来都比她到这人的庄园以后更有些生气。

===Original===
“De nex’ day atter he come back, little Mose wuz mo’ pearter en better ’n he had be’n fer a long time. But to’ds de een’ er de week he ’mence’ ter git res’less ag’in, en stop’ eatin’, en Aun’ Nancy kyared ’im down ter Aun’ Peggy once mo’, en she tu’nt ’im ter a mawkin’-bird dis time, en sont ’im off ter see his mammy ag’in.

===Chinese===
「回来后的第二天，小摩西比好一阵子以来都欢实、都精神。可是挨到这个礼拜的末尾，他又烦躁起来，又不肯吃东西了。南希大娘再一次把他抱到佩姬大娘那里；这一回她把他变成一只反舌鸟，又放他去见他娘。

===Original===
“It didn’ take him long fer ter git dere, en w’en he did, he seed his mammy standin’ in de kitchen, lookin’ back in de d’rection little Mose wuz comin’ fum. En dey wuz tears in her eyes, en she look’ mo’ po’ly en peaked ’n she had w’en he wuz down dere befo’. So little Mose sot on a tree in de ya’d en sung, en sung, en sung, des fittin’ ter split his th’oat. Fus’ Sis’ Becky didn’ notice ’im much, but dis mawkin’-bird kep’ stayin’ roun’ de house all day, en bimeby Sis’ Becky des ’magine’ dat mawkin’-bird wuz her little Mose crowin’ en crowin’, des lack he useter do w’en his mammy would come home at night fum de cotton-fiel’. De mawkin’-bird stayed roun’ dere ’mos’ all day, en w’en Sis’ Becky went out in de ya’d one time, dis yer mawkin’-bird lit on her shoulder en peck’ at de piece er bread she wuz eatin’, en fluttered his wings so dey rub’ up agin de side er her head. En w’en he flewed away ’long late in de ebenin’, des ’fo’ sundown, Sis’ Becky felt mo’ better ’n she had sence she had heared dat hummin’-bird a week er so pas’. En dat night she dremp ’bout ole times ag’in, des lack she did befo’.

===Chinese===
「没多大工夫他就到了。到那儿一看，他见他娘正站在厨房里，朝着小摩西飞来的那个方向望过来。她眼里噙着泪，人比他上回来的时候更显憔悴瘦损。于是小摩西落在院里一棵树上，唱啊，唱啊，唱啊，简直要把嗓子唱裂了才肯罢休。起初贝琪大姐没怎么留意他，可这反舌鸟整天整天地在屋子四周盘桓不走，到后来贝琪大姐便认定，这反舌鸟就是她的小摩西在欢叫，一声连一声，就像从前每当他娘夜里从棉花地收工回家时那样。那反舌鸟在那儿待了几乎一整天；有一回贝琪大姐走到院子里，这反舌鸟落在她肩头上，啄她手里正吃着的那块面包，扑扇着翅膀，翅尖直蹭着她脸侧。等天色向晚、日头将落，他飞走了，贝琪大姐觉得，自打一个来礼拜前听见那只蜂鸟的声音以来，数这一刻心里最松快。那天夜里，她又梦见了老日子，跟先前一回一回做的一样。

===Original===
“But dis yer totin’ little Mose down ter ole Aun’ Peggy, en dis yer gittin’ things fer ter pay de cunjuh ’oman, use’ up a lot er Aun’ Nancy’s time, en she begun ter git kinder ti’ed. ’Sides dat, w’en Sis’ Becky had be’n on de plantation, she had useter he’p Aun’ Nancy wid de young uns ebenin’s en Sundays; en Aun’ Nancy ’mence’ ter miss ’er monst’us, ’speshly sence she got a tech er de rheumatiz herse’f, en so she ’lows ter ole Aun’ Peggy one day:﻿—

===Chinese===
「可这么一趟一趟把小摩西往老佩姬大娘那儿抱，再一样一样弄东西来酬谢巫女，费去了南希大娘不少工夫，她渐渐有些乏了。再说，从前贝琪大姐还在庄园上的时候，晚上和礼拜天总要帮南希大娘照应娃娃们；如今南希大娘想她想得不行，尤其她自己又沾上了点风湿毛病。于是有一天她对老佩姬大娘说道——

===Original===
“ ‘Aun’ Peggy, ain’ dey no way you kin fetch Sis’ Becky back home?’

“ ‘Huh!’ sez Aun’ Peggy, ‘I dunno ’bout dat. I’ll hafter wuk my roots en fin’ out whuther I kin er no. But it’ll take a monst’us heap er wuk, en I can’t was’e my time fer nuffin. Ef you’ll fetch me sump’n ter pay me fer my trouble, I reckon we kin fix it.’

===Chinese===
「『佩姬大娘，就没个法子，能把贝琪大姐接回家来吗？』

「『哼！』佩姬大娘说，『这我可说不好。俺得先摆弄摆弄俺的草根，看看成不成。可这得费老鼻子的工夫，俺总不能白糟蹋。你要是能捎些东西来，酬谢俺这一番辛苦，俺琢磨着，这事能办成。』

===Original===
“So nex’ day Aun’ Nancy went down ter see Aun’ Peggy ag’in.

“ ‘Aun’ Peggy,’ sez she, ‘I is fotch’ you my bes’ Sunday head-hankercher. Will dat do?’

“Aun’ Peggy look’ at de head-hankercher, en run her han’ ober it, en sez she:﻿—

===Chinese===
「于是第二天，南希大娘又下去见佩姬大娘。

「『佩姬大娘，』她说，『俺把俺最好的礼拜头巾给您捎来了。这个使得吗？』

「佩姬大娘看了看那头巾，又用手在上面摩挲了一遍，说道——

===Original===
“ ‘Yas, dat’ll do fus’-rate. I’s be’n wukkin’ my roots sence you be’n gone, en I ’lows mos’ lackly I kin git Sis’ Becky back, but it’s gwine take fig’rin’ en studyin’ ez well ez cunj’in’. De fus’ thing ter do’ll be ter stop fetchin’ dat pickaninny down heah, en not sen’ ’im ter see his mammy no mo’. Ef he gits too po’ly, you lemme know, en I’ll gib you some kin’ er mixtry fer ter make ’im fergit Sis’ Becky fer a week er so. So ’less’n you comes fer dat, you neenter come back ter see me no mo’ ’tel I sen’s fer you.’

===Chinese===
「『行，这个顶好。你走了这一阵，俺一直在摆弄俺的草根，俺琢磨着，多半能把贝琪大姐弄回来；可这不光要施咒，还得多方盘算、细细思量。头一件要紧事，是别再把那黑娃娃往这儿抱了，也别再送他去见他娘。他要是病得凶了，你捎话给俺，俺给你配一副咒药，叫他把贝琪忘上个把礼拜。所以——除了来取那药，在俺打发人叫你之前，你不必再来看俺了。』

===Original===
“So Aun’ Peggy sont Aun’ Nancy erway, en de fus’ thing she done wuz ter call a hawnet fum a nes’ unner her eaves.

“ ‘You go up ter Kunnel Pen’leton’s stable, hawnet,’ sez she, ‘en sting de knees er de race hoss name’ Lightnin’ Bug. Be sho’ en git de right one.’

===Chinese===
「佩姬大娘打发走南希大娘，回头头一件事，就是把一只大黄蜂从自家屋檐底下的窝里唤了出来。

「『你飞到彭伯顿上校的马厩去，黄蜂，』她说，『去蜇那匹叫闪电虫的赛马的膝盖。可千万认准了，别蜇错一匹。』

===Original===
“So de hawnet flewed up ter Kunnel Pen’leton’s stable en stung Lightnin’ Bug roun’ de laigs, en de nex’ mawnin’ Lightnin’ Bug’s knees wuz all swoll’ up, twice’t ez big ez dey oughter be. W’en Kunnel Pen’leton went out ter de stable en see de hoss’s laigs, hit would ’a’ des made you trimble lack a leaf fer ter heah him cuss dat hoss trader. Howsomeber, he cool’ off bimeby en tol’ de stable boy fer ter rub Lightnin’ Bug’s laigs wid some linimum. De boy done ez his marster tol’ ’im, en by de nex’ day de swellin’ had gone down consid’able. Aun’ Peggy had sont a sparrer, w’at had a nes’ in one er de trees close ter her cabin, fer ter watch w’at wuz gwine on ’roun’ de big house, en w’en dis yer sparrer tol’ ’er de hoss wuz gittin’ ober de swellin’, she sont de hawnet back fer ter sting ’is knees some mo’, en de nex’ mawnin’ Lightnin’ Bug’s laigs wuz swoll’ up wuss ’n befo’.

===Chinese===
「大黄蜂飞到彭伯顿上校的马厩，围着闪电虫的腿蜇了一通。第二天早上，闪电虫的膝盖全肿了起来，比本来该有的尺寸粗了一倍。彭伯顿上校到马厩里一眼看见马腿——他咒骂那马贩子的那股凶劲儿，听着的人真会像风里的树叶似的打颤。不过他渐渐消了气，吩咐马童拿擦药给闪电虫的腿揉擦。马童照主人的吩咐做了，到第二天，肿就消去了大半。佩姬大娘早先放出一只麻雀——它在离她小屋不远的一棵树上搭着窝——去盯着大宅那边的动静；等这麻雀回话说那匹马快消肿了，她再把大黄蜂派回去，把马的膝盖又蜇了一通。于是第二天早上，闪电虫的腿肿得比先前更凶了。

===Original===
“Well, dis time Kunnel Pen’leton wuz mad th’oo en th’oo, en all de way ’roun’, en he cusst dat hoss trader up en down, fum *A* ter *Izzard*. He cusst so ha’d dat de stable boy got mos’ skeered ter def, en went off en hid hisse’f in de hay.

===Chinese===
「这一回，彭伯顿上校可是彻头彻尾、里里外外气了个透，把那马贩子上上下下骂了个遍，从 *A* 一直骂到 *Izzard*（从头骂到尾）。他骂得那样凶，马童差一点儿给吓死，躲到干草堆里藏了起来。

===Original===
“Ez fer Kunnel Pen’leton, he went right up ter de house en got out his pen en ink, en tuk off his coat en roll’ up his sleeves, en writ a letter ter dis yer hoss trader, en sezee:﻿—

“ ‘You is sol’ me a hoss w’at is got a ringbone er a spavin er sump’n, en w’at I paid you fer wuz a soun’ hoss. I wants you ter sen’ my nigger ’oman back en take yo’ ole hoss, er e’se I’ll sue you, sho ’s you bawn.’

===Chinese===
「彭伯顿上校呢，径直回宅子里去，取出笔和墨水，脱了外衣，挽起袖子，给这马贩子写了一封信，说道——

「『你卖给我的这匹马，不是生了环骨瘤（ringbone），就是得了飞节内肿（spavin），总归是腿里有了毛病；可我付钱买的，是一匹没病的马。我要你把我的黑奴女人送回来，把你这匹老马领回去；不然我就要告你，这话板上钉钉。』

===Original===
“But dis yer man wa’n’t skeered a bit, en he writ back ter Kunnel Pen’leton dat a bahg’in wuz a bahg’in; dat Lightnin’ Bug wuz soun’ w’en he sol’ ’im, en ef Kunnel Pen’leton didn’ knowed ernuff ’bout hosses ter take keer er a fine racer, dat wuz his own fune’al. En he say Kunnel Pen’leton kin sue en be cusst fer all he keer, but he ain’ gwine ter gib up de nigger he bought en paid fer.

===Chinese===
「可这人一点儿也不怕，回信给彭伯顿上校说：买卖就是买卖；闪电虫出手的时候好好的没毛病；彭伯顿上校要是没那份相马养马的本事，伺候不好一匹好赛马，那后果只好他自己担待。他还说，彭伯顿上校要告尽管告、要骂尽管骂，他才不放在心上；可他买下、付了钱的那个黑奴，他是绝不放手的。

===Original===
“W’en Kunnel Pen’leton got dis letter he wuz madder ’n he wuz befo’, ’speshly ’ca’se dis man ’lowed he didn’ know how ter take keer er fine hosses. But he couldn’ do nuffin but fetch a lawsuit, en he knowed, by his own ’spe’ience, dat lawsuits wuz slow ez de seben-yeah eetch and cos’ mo’ d’n dey come ter, en he ’lowed he better go slow en wait awhile.

===Chinese===
「彭伯顿上校接到这封信，比先前更恼，尤其气这人说他不懂得照管好马。可他除了打官司别无他法，而他凭自己的经验深知：官司这东西，慢得像七年疥疮，花销还常常比讨回来的多。他想来想去，还是稳着些好，先缓一缓，等上一阵子再说。

===Original===
“Aun’ Peggy knowed w’at wuz gwine on all dis time, en she fix’ up a little bag wid some roots en one thing en ernudder in it, en gun it ter dis sparrer er her’n, en tol’ ’im ter take it ’way down yander whar Sis’ Becky wuz, en drap it right befo’ de do’ er her cabin, so she’d be sho’ en fin’ it de fus’ time she come out’n de do’.

===Chinese===
「这前前后后的一切，佩姬大娘都了然于心。她备下一个小布袋，里头装了些草根和这个那个的东西，交给她那只麻雀，吩咐他把袋子衔到下头贝琪大姐待的地方，丢在她小屋门口的正前方，好叫她一出屋门，头一眼准能拾着。

===Original===
“One night Sis’ Becky dremp’ her pickaninny wuz dead, en de nex’ day she wuz mo’nin’ en groanin’ all day. She dremp’ de same dream th’ee nights runnin’, en den, de nex’ mawnin’ atter de las’ night, she foun’ dis yer little bag de sparrer had drap’ in front her do’; en she ’lowed she’d be’n cunju’d, en wuz gwine ter die, en ez long ez her pickaninny wuz dead dey wa’n’t no use tryin’ ter do nuffin nohow. En so she tuk ’n went ter bed, en tol’ her marster she’d be’n cunju’d en wuz gwine ter die.

===Chinese===
「一天夜里，贝琪大姐梦见她的黑娃娃死了，第二天她便哭了一天、呻吟了一天。同样的梦，她一连做了三夜。末了一夜过后的第二天早上，她拾到了麻雀丢在她门前的那只小布袋；她认定自己是中了咒，活不成了，再说黑娃娃已经死了，再挣扎也没有一点用处。于是她索性躺倒在床上，告诉主人，说她中了咒，就要死了。

===Original===
“Her marster lafft at her, en argyed wid her, en tried ter ’suade her out’n dis yer fool notion, ez he called it﻿—fer he wuz one er dese yer w’ite folks w’at purten’ dey doan b’liebe in cunj’in’﻿—but hit wa’n’t no use. Sis’ Becky kep’ gittin’ wusser en wusser, ’tel fin’lly dis yer man ’lowed Sis’ Becky wuz gwine ter die, sho’ ’nuff. En ez he knowed dey hadn’ be’n nuffin de matter wid Lightnin’ Bug w’en he traded ’im, he ’lowed mebbe he could kyo’ ’im en fetch ’im roun’ all right, leas’ways good ’nuff ter sell ag’in. En anyhow, a lame hoss wuz better ’n a dead nigger. So he sot down en writ Kunnel Pen’leton a letter.

===Chinese===
「她的主人笑话她，跟她讲道理，想劝她打消这个——照他的说法——愚蠢的念头；因为他是那种嘴上装作不信施咒的白人。可全然无用。贝琪大姐一天沉似一天，到末了这人只得认了：贝琪大姐怕是真要死了。他心里明白，闪电虫出手时本没病没灾，便琢磨着，兴许还能把他治好、将养过来，至少养到能再卖出去的地步。再说了，一匹瘸马总比一个死黑奴值钱。于是他坐下来，给彭伯顿上校写了一封信。

===Original===
“ ‘My conscience,’ sezee, ‘has be’n troublin’ me ’bout dat ringbone’ hoss I sol’ you. Some folks ’lows a hoss trader ain’ got no conscience, but dey doan know me, fer dat is my weak spot, en de reason I ain’ made no mo’ money hoss tradin’. Fac’ is,’ sezee, ‘I is got so I can’t sleep nights fum studyin’ ’bout dat spavin’ hoss; en I is made up my min’ dat, w’iles a bahg’in is a bahg’in, en you seed Lightnin’ Bug befo’ you traded fer ’im, principle is wuth mo’ d’n money er hosses er niggers. So ef you’ll sen’ Lightnin’ Bug down heah, I’ll sen’ yo’ nigger ’oman back, en we’ll call de trade off, en be ez good frien’s ez we eber wuz, en no ha’d feelin’s.’

===Chinese===
「『我的良心，』他说，『这些日子一直为那匹卖给你的环骨瘤马不得安生。有人说马贩子没有良心，那是他们不认得我——良心正是我的软处，也是我贩马没多挣下钱的原因。实不相瞒，』他说，『我为那匹飞节内肿的马思前想后，夜里都睡不着觉了；我已经拿定主意：买卖固然是买卖，你换马之前也亲眼见过闪电虫，可道义比金钱、比马匹、比黑奴都金贵。所以，你只要把闪电虫送回来，我就把你的黑奴女人送回去，这笔买卖一笔勾销，咱们还像从前一样做好朋友，谁也不记谁的仇。』

===Original===
“So sho’ ’nuff, Kunnel Pen’leton sont de hoss back. En w’en de man w’at come ter bring Lightnin’ Bug tol’ Sis’ Becky her pickaninny wa’n’t dead, Sis’ Becky wuz so glad dat she ’lowed she wuz gwine ter try ter lib ’tel she got back whar she could see little Mose once mo’. En w’en she retch’ de ole plantation en seed her baby kickin’ en crowin’ en holdin’ out his little arms to’ds her, she wush’ she wuzn’ cunju’d en didn’ hafter die. En w’en Aun’ Nancy tol’ ’er all ’bout Aun’ Peggy, Sis’ Becky went down ter see de cunjuh ’oman, en Aun’ Peggy tol’ her she had cunju’d her. En den Aun’ Peggy tuk de goopher off’n her, en she got well, en stayed on de plantation, en raise’ her pickaninny. En w’en little Mose growed up, he could sing en whistle des lack a mawkin’-bird, so dat de w’ite folks useter hab ’im come up ter de big house at night, en whistle en sing fer ’em, en dey useter gib ’im money en vittles en one thing er ernudder, w’ich he alluz tuk home ter his mammy; fer he knowed all ’bout w’at she had gone th’oo. He tu’nt out ter be a sma’t man, en l’arnt de blacksmif trade; en Kunnel Pen’leton let ’im hire his time. En bimeby he bought his mammy en sot her free, en den he bought hisse’f, en tuk keer er Sis’ Becky ez long ez dey bofe libbed.”

===Chinese===
「果不其然，彭伯顿上校把马送了回去。送闪电虫来的人告诉贝琪大姐，她的黑娃娃没有死，贝琪大姐欢喜得不得了，说她要打起精神撑着活，活到回到能再见小摩西一面的地方。等她回到老庄园，看见她的娃娃又蹬又笑、张着小胳膊朝她扑过来，她只巴不得自己没中过咒、不必去死。南希大娘把佩姬大娘的事一五一十讲给她听之后，贝琪大姐便下去见那位巫女；佩姬大娘对她明说，咒是她下的。随后佩姬大娘解了她身上下的咒（goopher），她的病就好了，从此留在庄园上，把黑娃娃拉扯成人。小摩西长大以后，唱起歌、打起口哨来活像一只反舌鸟，白人们便常叫他夜里上大宅去，给他们吹口哨、唱歌，常赏他钱啊、吃食啊这些那些，他总是一概带回家里给他娘；因为他全知道他娘吃过的苦。他后来成了个有出息的人，学了打铁的手艺；彭伯顿上校准他自雇时间——按期向主人缴一笔钱，挣下的归自己。再后来，他先出钱买下他娘、放她自由，又买下他自己，一直照料着贝琪大姐，直到他们俩都离开人世。」

===Original===
My wife had listened to this story with greater interest than she had manifested in any subject for several days. I had watched her furtively from time to time during the recital, and had observed the play of her countenance. It had expressed in turn sympathy, indignation, pity, and at the end lively satisfaction.

===Chinese===
我妻子听着这个故事，表现出的兴致比多日来对任何事情的都高。讲述之间，我不时悄悄看她，留意她脸上的神情。那神情先后流露出同情、愤慨、怜惜，到末了，则是掩不住的欢喜。

===Original===
“That is a very ingenious fairy tale, Julius,” I said, “and we are much obliged to you.”

“Why, John!” said my wife severely, “the story bears the stamp of truth, if ever a story did.”

===Chinese===
「这可真是个编得巧妙的神话，朱利叶斯，」我说，「多谢你啦。」

「嗳，约翰！」我妻子正色道，「要说有故事带着真实的印记，这故事就是。」

===Original===
“Yes,” I replied, “especially the hummingbird episode, and the mockingbird digression, to say nothing of the doings of the hornet and the sparrow.”

“Oh, well, I don’t care,” she rejoined, with delightful animation; “those are mere ornamental details and not at all essential. The story is true to nature, and might have happened half a hundred times, and no doubt did happen, in those horrid days before the war.”

===Chinese===
「是啊，」我答道，「尤其是蜂鸟那一段，还有反舌鸟那段插曲，更别提大黄蜂和麻雀的种种作为了。」

「哦，那我不在乎，」她兴冲冲地回嘴道，「那些不过是添彩的枝节，全不要紧。这故事合乎人情事理，没准儿发生过五十来回；在南北战争前那些可怕的岁月里，毫无疑问就是发生过。」

===Original===
“By the way, Julius,” I remarked, “your story doesn’t establish what you started out to prove﻿—that a rabbit’s foot brings good luck.”

“Hit’s plain ’nuff ter me, suh,” replied Julius. “I bet young missis dere kin ’splain it herse’f.”

===Chinese===
「对了，朱利叶斯，」我说，「你这个故事，可没能证成你起初要证明的事——兔子脚带得来好运。」

「俺看明白得很，先生，」朱利叶斯答道，「俺敢说，这位年轻的太太自己就能说明白。」

===Original===
“I rather suspect,” replied my wife promptly, “that Sis’ Becky had no rabbit’s foot.”

“You is hit de bull’s-eye de fus’ fire, ma’m,” assented Julius. “Ef Sis’ Becky had had a rabbit foot, she nebber would ’a’ went th’oo all dis trouble.”

===Chinese===
「我倒疑心，」我妻子立刻答道，「贝琪大姐压根儿就没有兔子脚。」

「您头一枪就打中了靶心，太太，」朱利叶斯应道，「贝琪大姐要是有一只兔子脚，哪里还会遭这一场劫难。」

===Original===
I went into the house for some purpose, and left Julius talking to my wife. When I came back a moment later, he was gone.

===Chinese===
我进屋去有点事，便留下朱利叶斯陪我妻子说话。片刻之后我出来，他已经走了。

===Original===
My wife’s condition took a turn for the better from this very day, and she was soon on the way to ultimate recovery. Several weeks later, after she had resumed her afternoon drives, which had been interrupted by her illness, Julius brought the rockaway round to the front door one day, and I assisted my wife into the carriage.

===Chinese===
恰恰从这一天起，我妻子的病情好转了，不久便走上了彻底康复的路。几个星期后，她恢复了因病中断的午后驾车出游。一天，朱利叶斯把四轮敞篷马车赶到前门口，我搀着妻子上了车。

===Original===
“John,” she said, before I had taken my seat, “I wish you would look in my room, and bring me my handkerchief. You will find it in the pocket of my blue dress.”

===Chinese===
「约翰，」她在我落座之前说，「劳驾你去我房里瞧一眼，把我的手帕拿来。就在我那件蓝衣服的口袋里。」

===Original===
I went to execute the commission. When I pulled the handkerchief out of her pocket, something else came with it and fell on the floor. I picked up the object and looked at it. It was Julius’s rabbit’s foot.

===Chinese===
我去办这桩差事。当我从她口袋里抽出手帕时，另一样东西跟着带了出来，掉在地上。我拾起那东西一看——正是朱利叶斯那只兔子脚。

## The Gray Wolf’s Ha’nt / 灰狼的鬼魂

===Original===
It was a rainy day at the vineyard. The morning had dawned bright and clear. But the sky had soon clouded, and by nine o’clock there was a light shower, followed by others at brief intervals. By noon the rain had settled into a dull, steady downpour. The clouds hung low, and seemed to grow denser instead of lighter as they discharged their watery burden, and there was now and then a muttering of distant thunder. Outdoor work was suspended, and I spent most of the day at the house, looking over my accounts and bringing up some arrears of correspondence.

===Chinese===
葡萄园里这一天有雨。清晨破晓时分明明晴朗清爽，可天色很快便阴了上来，到九点钟落了一阵小雨，隔不多久又是一阵。到晌午，雨已经下成了一场沉闷的、连绵不绝的倾泻。乌云低低压着，卸下满腹的水，非但不显得轻了，反而愈发浓重，远处还时不时滚过一阵低沉的闷雷。户外的活计全停了，这一天我大半待在屋里，翻检账目，又补写了几封拖欠已久的信。

===Original===
Towards four o’clock I went out on the piazza, which was broad and dry, and less gloomy than the interior of the house, and composed myself for a quiet smoke. I had lit my cigar and opened the volume I was reading at that time, when my wife, whom I had left dozing on a lounge, came out and took a rocking-chair near me.

===Chinese===
将近四点钟，我踱到游廊上。游廊宽敞干爽，不似屋子里面那么阴沉；我安顿下来，准备安安静静抽口烟。刚点上雪茄、翻开我近日在读的那部书，我妻子——方才还把她留在躺椅上打盹——走了出来，在我近旁一张摇椅里坐下。

===Original===
“I wish you would talk to me, or read to me—or something,” she exclaimed petulantly. “It’s awfully dull here today.”

“I’ll read to you with pleasure,” I replied, and began at the point where I had found my bookmark:—

===Chinese===
「我倒巴不得你跟我说说话，或者念点什么给我听——随便什么都行，」她不耐烦地嚷道，「今天这儿可闷死人了。」

「我乐意念给你听，」我答道，便从夹书签的地方念起——

===Original===
“ ‘The difficulty of dealing with transformations so many-sided as those which all existences have undergone, or are undergoing, is such as to make a complete and deductive interpretation almost hopeless. So to grasp the total process of redistribution of matter and motion as to see simultaneously its several necessary results in their actual interdependence is scarcely possible. There is, however, a mode of rendering the process as a whole tolerably comprehensible. Though the genesis of the rearrangement of every evolving aggregate is in itself one, it presents to our intelligence’ ”—

===Chinese===
「『要应对一切存在物所已经历、或正在经历的这许多面的转化，困难之大，几乎使一种完整而演绎的解释成为无望。要把握物质与运动再分配的全过程，从而同时看清其若干必然结果在实际相互依存中的面貌，几乎是不可能的。不过，倒有一种办法，可以把这一过程作为整体弄得大体可以理解。虽然每一个演化中的聚合体，其重排的起源本身是同一的，它呈现于我们智能面前的却是——』」

===Original===
“John,” interrupted my wife, “I wish you would stop reading that nonsense and see who that is coming up the lane.”

I closed my book with a sigh. I had never been able to interest my wife in the study of philosophy, even when presented in the simplest and most lucid form.

===Chinese===
「约翰，」我妻子打断道，「别念那劳什子了，去看看巷子那头来的又是谁。」

我叹了口气，合上书。哪怕摆成最浅显明白的样子，我也从来没能让我妻子对哲学研究生出半点兴致。

===Original===
Someone was coming up the lane; at least, a huge faded cotton umbrella was making progress toward the house, and beneath it a pair of nether extremities in trousers was discernible. Any doubt in my mind as to whose they were was soon resolved when Julius reached the steps and, putting the umbrella down, got a good dash of the rain as he stepped up on the porch.

===Chinese===
巷子里确实有人过来；至少，一把褪了色的大棉布伞正朝屋子这边挪，伞底下隐约辨得出一双装在裤管里的下肢。那双腿是谁的，我本还有几分疑惑；及至朱利叶斯走到台阶跟前，把伞往地上一放，跨步上廊时结结实实兜了一头雨，这疑惑顿时烟消云散。

===Original===
“Why in the world, Julius,” I asked, “didn’t you keep the umbrella up until you got under cover?”

“It’s bad luck, suh, ter raise a’ umbrella in de house, en w’iles I dunno whuther it’s bad luck ter kyar one inter de piazzer er no, I ’lows it’s alluz bes’ ter be on de safe side. I didn’ s’pose you en young missis ’u’d be gwine on yo’ dribe ter-day, but bein’ ez it’s my pa’t ter take you ef you does, I ’lowed I’d repo’t fer dooty, en let you say whuther er no you wants ter go.”

===Chinese===
「你究竟是怎么想的，朱利叶斯，」我问，「怎么不把伞撑到廊子底下再收？」

「在屋里撑伞不吉利，先生。把伞拿上游廊晦气不晦气，俺说不上来；可俺想啊，凡事总以稳当为上。俺原估摸着，今儿个这样的天，您和少太太不会驾车出门了；不过您要真出门，伺候的差事终究落在俺头上，俺想着还是来报个到，听您示下，说去还是不去。」

===Original===
“I’m glad you came, Julius,” I responded. “We don’t want to go driving, of course, in the rain, but I should like to consult you about another matter. I’m thinking of taking in a piece of new ground. What do you imagine it would cost to have that neck of woods down by the swamp cleared up?”

===Chinese===
「你来了正好，朱利叶斯，」我应道，「雨天地里当然出不成门，不过我另有一件事想同你商量。我打算开一片新地。依你看，把沼泽边那片林子清理出来，得费多少钱？」

===Original===
The old man’s countenance assumed an expression of unwonted seriousness, and he shook his head doubtfully.

===Chinese===
老人脸上浮起一层少有的郑重，迟疑地摇了摇头。

===Original===
“I dunno ’bout dat, suh. It mought cos’ mo’, en it mought cos’ less, ez fuh ez money is consarned. I ain’ denyin’ you could cl’ar up dat trac’ er lan’ fer a hund’ed er a couple er hund’ed dollahs—ef you wants ter cl’ar it up. But ef dat ’uz my trac’ er lan’, I wouldn’ ’sturb it, no, suh, I wouldn’; sho ’s you bawn, I wouldn’.”

===Chinese===
「这个俺说不好，先生。单论钱，兴许要多，兴许要少。俺不否认，那块地您花上一百两百块美金，是清理得出来的——只要您当真想清理。可那块地要是俺的，俺说什么也不去动它；不动，先生，说什么也不；老天爷在上，决不。」

===Original===
“But why not?” I asked.

“It ain’ fittin’ fer grapes, fer noo groun’ nebber is.”

===Chinese===
「可这是为什么？」我问。

「那地种不得葡萄；生荒地从来种不得。」

===Original===
“I know it, but”—

“It ain’ no yeathly good fer cotton, ’ca’se it’s too low.”

===Chinese===
「这我知道，可是」——

「种棉花也压根儿不成，那地地势太洼。」

===Original===
“Perhaps so; but it will raise splendid corn.”

“I dunno,” rejoined Julius deprecatorily. “It’s so nigh de swamp dat de ’coons’ll eat up all de cawn.”

===Chinese===
「兴许如此；不过种玉米，那地可是再好不过。」

「难说，」朱利叶斯不以为然地应道，「那地紧挨着沼泽，浣熊要把玉米吃个精光。」

===Original===
“I think I’ll risk it,” I answered.

“Well, suh,” said Julius, “I wushes you much joy er yo’ job. Ef you has bad luck er sickness er trouble er any kin’, doan blame *me*. You can’t say ole Julius didn’ wa’n you.”

===Chinese===
「我想，这点险我还是冒得起的，」我答道。

「那好啊，先生，」朱利叶斯说，「这桩活计，俺先祝您多多受用。往后您要是走了背运、害了病、遭了灾，不管哪一样，可别怪*俺*。到时候您可不能说，老朱利叶斯没提醒过您。」

===Original===
“Warn him of what, Uncle Julius?” asked my wife.

“Er de bad luck w’at follers folks w’at ’sturbs dat trac’ er lan’. Dey is snakes en sco’pions in dem woods. En ef you manages ter ’scape de p’isen animals, you is des boun’ ter hab a ha’nt ter settle wid—ef you doan hab two.”

===Chinese===
「提醒他什么呀，朱利叶斯大叔？」我妻子问。

「提醒他，动了那块地的人，背后跟着的是什么样的背运。那片林子里有蛇，有蝎子。就算您躲开了那些带毒的畜生，也注定躲不开要同一个作祟的鬼魂（ha'nt）算账——弄不好是两个。」

===Original===
“Whose haunt?” my wife demanded, with growing interest.

“De gray wolf’s ha’nt, some folks calls it—but I knows better.”

“Tell us about it, Uncle Julius,” said my wife. “A story will be a godsend today.”

===Chinese===
「谁的鬼魂？」我妻子追问，兴致越来越浓。

「有人管它叫灰狼的鬼魂——可俺心里比他们清楚。」

「给我们讲讲吧，朱利叶斯大叔，」我妻子说，「今天有个故事听，真是老天赏赐。」

===Original===
It was not difficult to induce the old man to tell a story, if he were in a reminiscent mood. Of tales of the old slavery days he seemed indeed to possess an exhaustless store—some weirdly grotesque, some broadly humorous; some bearing the stamp of truth, faint, perhaps, but still discernible; others palpable inventions, whether his own or not we never knew, though his fancy doubtless embellished them. But even the wildest was not without an element of pathos—the tragedy, it might be, of the story itself; the shadow, never absent, of slavery and of ignorance; the sadness, always, of life as seen by the fading light of an old man’s memory.

===Chinese===
只要老人来了追忆往事的兴致，要引他讲个故事并不难。那些旧奴隶年月的故事，他肚子里仿佛真藏着一口取之不竭的井——有的诡谲怪诞，有的粗放逗笑；有的带着真实的印痕，纵然淡薄，却还辨得出来；另一些则一望便知是编造，究竟出自他本人还是别人之口，我们始终无从知晓，虽说十有八九经过了想象的润饰。但就连最荒诞不经的，也不缺一缕悲怆——也许是故事本身的惨烈；是奴隶制与蒙昧投下的、从不缺席的阴影；是一位老人就着记忆中渐暗的余光望出去，人生所固有的那份哀愁。

===Original===
“Way back yander befo’ de wah,” began Julius, “ole Mars Dugal’ McAdoo useter own a nigger name’ Dan. Dan wuz big en strong en hearty en peaceable en good-nachu’d most er de time, but dange’ous ter aggervate. He alluz done his task, en nebber had no trouble wid de w’ite folks, but woe be unter de nigger w’at ’lowed he c’d fool wid Dan, fer he wuz mos’ sho’ ter git a good lammin’. Soon ez eve’ybody foun’ Dan out, dey didn’ many un ’em ’temp’ ter ’sturb ’im. De one dat did would ’a’ wush’ he hadn’, ef he could ’a’ libbed long ernuff ter do any wushin’.

===Chinese===
「老早以前，还没打仗那阵子，」朱利叶斯开了腔，「老杜加尔·麦卡杜（Dugal' McAdoo）老爷手底下有个黑人，名叫丹（Dan）。丹生得高大、结实、壮健；性子平日里和和平平、好脾气，可要把他招恼了，就危险了。他份内的活计从来做得妥妥帖帖，跟白人也从没闹过别扭；可是哪个黑人要是自以为能作弄丹，那他可要遭殃，八成逃不脱一顿好揍。等大伙儿都摸清了丹的底细，就没几个人想去撩他了。真去撩的那一个，只要还能活得够久、来得及懊悔，准会懊悔自己多这一手。」

===Original===
“It all happen’ dis erway. Dey wuz a cunjuh man w’at libbed ober t’ other side er de Lumbe’ton Road. He had be’n de only cunjuh doctor in de naberhood fer lo! dese many yeahs, ’tel ole Aun’ Peggy sot up in de bizness down by de Wim’l’ton Road. Dis cunjuh man had a son w’at libbed wid ’im, en it wuz dis yer son w’at got mix’ up wid Dan—en all ’bout a ’oman.

===Chinese===
「事情就是这么起来的。伦伯顿大道那头，住着一个巫师（conjure man）。多年来，呶，好些个年头了，这一带的巫医一直是他独一份的营生；直到后来佩姬大娘（Aun' Peggy）在威尔明顿大道（Wim'l'ton Road）边上也支起了这门生意。这巫师有个儿子，跟他一块儿过活；偏偏就是这个儿子，跟丹缠到了一处——说到底，全是为着一个女人。」

===Original===
“Dey wuz a gal on de plantation name’ Mahaly. She wuz a monst’us lackly gal—tall en soopl’, wid big eyes, en a small foot, en a lively tongue, en w’en Dan tuk ter gwine wid ’er eve’ybody ’lowed dey wuz well match’, en none er de yuther nigger men on de plantation das’ ter go nigh her, fer dey wuz all feared er Dan.

===Chinese===
「庄园上有个姑娘，名叫马哈利（Mahaly）。她是个顶俊俏的姑娘——高挑个儿，身段柔软，一双大眼睛，一对小脚，口齿又伶俐。丹跟她相好上以后，人人夸这俩人般配；庄园上旁的黑人男人，没一个敢往她跟前凑，因为他们全都怕丹。」

===Original===
“Now, it happen’ dat dis yer cunjuh man’s son wuz gwine ’long de road one day, w’en who sh’d come pas’ but Mahaly. En de minute dis man sot eyes on Mahaly, he ’lowed he wuz gwine ter hab her fer hisse’f. He come up side er her en ’mence’ ter talk ter her; but she didn’ paid no ’tention ter ’im, fer she wuz studyin’ ’bout Dan, en she didn’ lack dis nigger’s looks nohow. So w’en she got ter whar she wuz gwine, dis yer man wa’n’t no fu’ther ’long dan he wuz w’en he sta’ted.

===Chinese===
「偏巧有一天，这巫师的儿子正顺着大路走，迎面过来的不是别人，正是马哈利。这人的眼睛一搭上马哈利，当下就打定主意，要把她弄到手归自己。他凑到她旁边，跟她搭起话来；可她理也不理，因为她心里惦记着丹，再说这黑人的模样，她横竖看不顺眼。等她走到要去的地方，这人还是一步没挪，跟刚开腔时一个样。」

===Original===
“Co’se, atter he had made up his min’ fer ter git Mahaly, he ’mence’ ter ’quire ’roun’, en soon foun’ out all ’bout Dan, en w’at a dange’ous nigger he wuz. But dis man ’lowed his daddy wuz a cunjuh man, en so he’d come out all right in de een’; en he kep’ right on atter Mahaly. Meanw’iles Dan’s marster had said dey could git married ef dey wanter, en so Dan en Mahaly had tuk up wid one ernudder, en wuz libbin’ in a cabin by deyse’ves, en wuz des wrop’ up in one ernudder.

===Chinese===
「当然喽，他既存心要弄到马哈利，便四处打听起来，很快把丹的底细摸了个透，知道那是个惹不得的黑人。可这人一想，自家老子是巫师，末了自有法子保他逢凶化吉；便照旧缠住马哈利不放。这当口，丹的主人发了话，说他们要愿意就许他们成婚；于是丹和马哈利两个正正经经搭伙过起日子，单住一间小屋，成天你缠着我、我缠着你。」

===Original===
“But dis yer cunjuh man’s son didn’ ’pear ter min’ Dan’s takin’ up wid Mahaly, en he kep’ on hangin’ ’roun’ des de same, ’tel fin’lly one day Mahaly sez ter Dan, sez she:—

“ ‘I wush you’d do sump’n ter stop dat free nigger man fum follerin’ me ’roun’. I doan lack him nohow, en I ain’ got no time fer ter was’e wid no man but you.’

===Chinese===
「可这巫师的儿子，丹把马哈利娶到了手，他面上竟像全不在意，照旧在跟前转悠。到后来有一天，马哈利对丹说道——

「『你想法子叫那个自由黑人别再跟着俺转悠了行不行。俺横竖看不上他；俺的工夫，除了你，谁也不配消受。』」

===Original===
“Co’se Dan got mad w’en he heared ’bout dis man pest’rin’ Mahaly, en de nex’ night, w’en he seed dis nigger comin’ ’long de road, he up en ax’ ’im w’at he mean by hangin’ ’roun’ his ’oman. De man didn’ ’spon’ ter suit Dan, en one wo’d led ter ernudder, ’tel bimeby dis cunjuh man’s son pull’ out a knife en sta’ted ter stick it in Dan; but befo’ he could git it drawed good, Dan haul’ off en hit ’im in de head so ha’d dat he nebber got up. Dan ’lowed he’d come to atter a w’ile en go ’long ’bout his bizness, so he went off en lef’ ’im layin’ dere on de groun’.

===Chinese===
「丹听说这人纠缠马哈利，自然火从心起。第二天夜里，他瞧见这黑人顺着大路过来，便迎上去问他：老围着他女人打转，是什么意思。那人的回话不中丹的意，一句赶一句，到后来，这巫师的儿子抽出刀来，就要往丹身上捅；还没等他把刀抽顺当，丹抡圆了胳膊，照他脑袋上一拳砸下去——这一拳，那人再没起来。丹心想，过一会儿他缓过劲儿来，自会爬起来各走各的路，便自顾自去了，撂他直挺挺躺在地上。」

===Original===
“De nex’ mawnin’ de man wuz foun’ dead. Dey wuz a great ’miration made ’bout it, but Dan didn’ say nuffin, en none er de yuther niggers hadn’ seed de fight, so dey wa’n’t no way ter tell who done de killin’. En bein’ ez it wuz a free nigger, en dey wa’n’t no w’ite folks ’speshly int’rusted, dey wa’n’t nuffin done ’bout it, en de cunjuh man come en tuk his son en kyared ’im ’way en buried ’im.

===Chinese===
「第二天一早，那人被发现死了。这事儿惊动了一大片，可丹一个字不吐，旁的黑人又都没瞧见那场打斗，就没了法子断定是谁下的手。再说死的又是个自由黑人，没有哪个白人特别上心，这事也就不了了之；巫师来把儿子领走，驮回去埋了。」

===Original===
“Now, Dan hadn’ meant ter kill dis nigger, en w’iles he knowed de man hadn’ got no mo’ d’n he desarved, Dan ’mence’ ter worry mo’ er less. Fer he knowed dis man’s daddy would wuk his roots en prob’ly fin’ out who had killt ’is son, en make all de trouble fer ’im he could. En Dan kep’ on studyin’ ’bout dis ’tel he got so he didn’ ha’dly das’ ter eat er drink fer fear dis cunjuh man had p’isen’ de vittles er de water. Fin’lly he ’lowed he’d go ter see Aun’ Peggy, de noo cunjuh ’oman w’at had moved down by de Wim’l’ton Road, en ax her fer ter do sump’n ter pertec’ ’im fum dis cunjuh man. So he tuk a peck er ’taters en went down ter her cabin one night.

===Chinese===
「丹原本并没打算要这黑人的命；他也明白，这人挨的不过是自作自受。话虽如此，丹心里还是多多少少犯起了嘀咕。他知道这人的老子会使根茎的法术，十有八九能查出是谁打死了他儿子，然后能给他添多少堵就添多少堵。丹翻来覆去琢磨这件事，琢磨到后来，简直不敢吃、不敢喝，生怕那巫师在饭食里、水里下了毒。末了他打定主意，去见见佩姬大娘——就是搬到威尔明顿大道边上去的那个新来的巫女——求她想个法子，保他不受这巫师的暗算。于是一天夜里，他拎上一配克红薯，下到她的小屋去了。」

===Original===
“Aun’ Peggy heared his tale, en den sez she:—

“ ‘Dat cunjuh man is mo’ d’n twice’t ez ole ez I is, en he kin make monst’us powe’ful goopher. W’at you needs is a life-cha’m, en I’ll make you one ter-morrer; it’s de on’y thing w’at’ll do you any good. You leabe me a couple er ha’rs fum yo’ head, en fetch me a pig ter-morrer night fer ter roas’, en w’en you come I’ll hab de cha’m all ready fer you.’

===Chinese===
「佩姬大娘听他讲完来龙去脉，说道——

「『那巫师的岁数比俺大一倍还多，下得一手顶厉害的咒（goopher）。你缺的是一件护身符（life-cha'm），明天俺给你做一件；也只有这东西顶得了事。你给俺留下两根你头上的头发，明儿夜里再捎一口小猪来，俺好烤着吃；等你来时，俺就把护身符给你备得妥妥帖帖。』」

===Original===
“So Dan went down ter Aun’ Peggy de nex’ night—wid a young shote—en Aun’ Peggy gun ’im de cha’m. She had tuk de ha’rs Dan had lef’ wid ’er, en a piece er red flannin, en some roots en yarbs, en had put ’em in a little bag made out’n ’coon-skin.

===Chinese===
「于是第二天夜里，丹又下了佩姬大娘那儿——拎着一只小猪崽——佩姬大娘便把护身符给了他。她拿丹留下的那两根头发，配上一块红法兰绒、几样根茎和草药，一起装进一只小口袋；口袋是拿浣熊皮做的。」

===Original===
“ ‘You take dis cha’m,’ sez she, ‘en put it in a bottle er a tin box, en bury it deep unner de root er a live-oak tree, en ez long ez it stays dere safe en soun’, dey ain’ no p’isen kin p’isen you, dey ain’ no rattlesnake kin bite you, dey ain’ no sco’pion kin sting you. Dis yere cunjuh man mought do one thing er ’nudder ter you, but he can’t kill you. So you neenter be at all skeered, but go ’long ’bout yo’ bizness en doan bother yo’ min’.’

===Chinese===
「『你把这护身符，』她说，『装进一只瓶子或者铁皮盒，埋到一棵活橡树根底下，埋深些。只要它在那儿安安稳稳、完好无损，就没有毒药毒得着你，没有响尾蛇咬得着你，没有蝎子蜇得着你。那巫师兴许还能对你使这样那样的手段，可他要不了你的命。所以你一点儿也不必怕，只管照常干你的活计，别往心里去。』」

===Original===
“So Dan went down by de ribber, en ’way up on de bank he buried de cha’m deep unner de root er a live-oak tree, en kivered it up en stomp’ de dirt down en scattered leaves ober de spot, en den went home wid his min’ easy.

===Chinese===
「于是丹下到河边，在高高的河岸上，把护身符深深埋进一棵活橡树的根底下，盖上土，踩瓷实，又撒了些树叶把那处地面遮住，这才安安心心回了家。」

===Original===
“Sho’ ’nuff, dis yer cunjuh man wukked his roots, des ez Dan had ’spected he would, en soon l’arn’ who killt his son. En co’se he made up his min’ fer ter git eben wid Dan. So he sont a rattlesnake fer ter sting ’im, but de rattlesnake say de nigger’s heel wuz so ha’d he couldn’ git his sting in. Den he sont his jay-bird fer ter put p’isen in Dan’s vittles, but de p’isen didn’ wuk. Den de cunjuh man ’low’ he’d double Dan all up wid de rheumatiz, so he couldn’ git ’is han’ ter his mouf ter eat, en would hafter sta’ve ter def; but Dan went ter Aun’ Peggy, en she gun ’im a’ ’intment ter kyo de rheumatiz. Den de cunjuh man ’lowed he’d bu’n Dan up wid a fever, but Aun’ Peggy tol’ ’im how ter make some yarb tea fer dat. Nuffin dis man tried would kill Dan, so fin’lly de cunjuh man ’lowed Dan mus’ hab a life-cha’m.

===Chinese===
「果不其然，那巫师使开了根茎的法术，正如丹料的那样，没过多久就查出是谁打死了他儿子。他自然打定主意要同丹清账。他先打发一条响尾蛇去咬丹，可响尾蛇回话说，那黑人的脚后跟硬得很，毒牙扎不进去。他又派他的松鸦（jay-bird）去往丹的饭食里下毒，可那毒不灵。巫师又想拿风湿把丹整个人折起来，叫他抬不起手、够不着嘴吃不上饭，活活饿死；可丹去找佩姬大娘，她给了他一贴治风湿的药膏。巫师又想叫一场热病把丹烧倒，佩姬大娘又教了他熬草药茶治这个。这人试来试去，什么都弄不死丹；到头来巫师断定：丹身上必是藏了护身符。」

===Original===
“Now, dis yer jay-bird de cunjuh man had wuz a monst’us sma’t creeter—fac’, de niggers ’lowed he wuz de ole Debbil hisse’f, des settin’ roun’ waitin’ ter kyar dis ole man erway w’en he’d retch’ de een’ er his rope. De cunjuh man sont dis jay-bird fer ter watch Dan en fin’ out whar he kep’ his cha’m. De jay-bird hung roun’ Dan fer a week er so, en one day he seed Dan go down by de ribber en look at a live-oak tree; en den de jay-bird went back ter his marster, en tol’ ’im he ’spec’ de nigger kep’ his life-cha’m under dat tree.

===Chinese===
「再说巫师养的这只松鸦，是个精明透顶的家伙——说实在的，黑人们私下都说，它就是老魔鬼本尊，成天蹲在边上守着，专等老头子那条命绳到了头，好把他驮走。巫师便派这只松鸦去盯丹的梢，看他护身符收在哪儿。松鸦围着丹转了一个来礼拜；有一天，它瞧见丹下到河边，对着一棵活橡树张望；松鸦便回去禀报主人，说它估摸着，那黑人把护身符收在那棵树底下。」

===Original===
“De cunjuh man lafft en lafft, en he put on his bigges’ pot, en fill’ it wid his stronges’ roots, en b’iled it en b’iled it, ’tel bimeby de win’ blowed en blowed, ’tel it blowed down de live-oak tree. Den he stirred some more roots in de pot, en it rained en rained ’tel de water run down de ribber bank en wash’ Dan’s life-cha’m inter de ribber, en de bottle went bobbin’ down de current des ez onconsarned ez ef it wa’n’t takin’ po’ Dan’s chances all ’long wid it. En den de cunjuh man lafft some mo’, en ’lowed ter hisse’f dat he wuz gwine ter fix Dan now, sho’ ’nuff; he wa’n’t gwine ter kill ’im des yet, fer he could do sump’n ter ’im w’at would hu’t wusser ’n killin’.

===Chinese===
「巫师听了，笑了又笑；他支起最大的一口锅，装满他顶厉害的根茎，煮啊煮啊，煮到后来，风刮起来了，越刮越凶，把那棵活橡树刮倒了。他又往锅里搅进几味根茎，雨便下起来，越下越大，雨水顺着河岸往下淌，把丹的护身符冲进了河里；那只瓶子顺流而下，一路颠簸打转，那副若无其事的架势，倒像全没捎带上可怜丹的活路。巫师又笑了一阵，心里盘算：这回可真要把丹收拾定了；眼下还不忙着叫他死——他有的是比死更叫人难受的手段。」

===Original===
“So dis cunjuh man ’mence’ by gwine up ter Dan’s cabin eve’y night, en takin’ Dan out in his sleep en ridin’ ’im roun’ de roads en fiel’s ober de rough groun’. In de mawnin’ Dan would be ez ti’ed ez ef he hadn’ be’n ter sleep. Dis kin’ er thing kep’ up fer a week er so, en Dan had des ’bout made up his min’ fer ter go en see Aun’ Peggy ag’in, w’en who sh’d he come across, gwine ’long de road one day, to’ds sundown, but dis yer cunjuh man. Dan felt kinder skeered at fus’; but den he ’membered ’bout his life-cha’m, w’ich he hadn’ be’n ter see fer a week er so, en ’lowed wuz safe en soun’ unner de live-oak tree, en so he hilt up ’is head en walk’ ’long, des lack he didn’ keer nuffin ’bout dis man no mo’ d’n any yuther nigger. W’en he got close ter de cunjuh man, dis cunjuh man sez, sezee:—

===Chinese===
「于是这巫师夜夜到丹的小屋去，趁他睡熟，把他牵出来，骑着他在大路上、田野里，专拣顶坎坷的地面跑。到早上，丹总累得跟一宿没合眼似的。这么折腾了一个来礼拜，丹正打定主意再去找一趟佩姬大娘，偏偏有一天，傍晚日头快落的时候，他在路上迎面撞见的不是别人，正是这巫师。丹起先有点发怵；可随即想起自己的护身符——有一个礼拜没去照看了，只道它还安安稳稳埋在活橡树底下——便昂起头，照直往前走，只当不把这人放在心上，同不把随便哪个旁的黑人放在心上一样。待他走到巫师近前，那巫师开口道——」

===Original===
“ ‘Hoddy, Brer Dan? I hopes you er well?’

“W’en Dan seed de cunjuh man wuz in a good humor en didn’ ’pear ter bear no malice, Dan ’lowed mebbe de cunjuh man hadn’ foun’ out who killt his son, en so he ’termine’ fer ter let on lack he didn’ know nuffin, en so sezee:—

===Chinese===
「『好啊，丹兄弟？身子骨还好吧？』

「丹见巫师一团和气，不像记仇的样子，心想必是还没查出谁打死了他儿子；于是拿定主意，只装什么都不知道，便应道——」

===Original===
“ ‘Hoddy, Unk’ Jube?’—dis ole cunjuh man’s name wuz Jube. ‘I’s p’utty well, I thank you. How is you feelin’ dis mawnin’?’

“ ‘I’s feelin’ ez well ez a’ ole nigger could feel w’at had los’ his only son, en his main ’pen’ence in ’is ole age.

===Chinese===
「『好啊，朱伯大叔！』——这老巫师名字叫朱伯（Jube）。『托您的福，俺还算康健。您今儿早上身上可好？』

「『俺觉着呀，就像一个没了独生子、老来断了指望的黑人老头儿能觉着的那种好。』」

===Original===
“ ‘But den my son wuz a bad boy,’ sezee, ‘en I couldn’ ’spec’ nuffin e’se. I tried ter l’arn him de arrer er his ways en make him go ter chu’ch en pra’r-meetin’; but it wa’n’t no use. I dunno who killt ’im, en I doan wanter know, fer I’d be mos’ sho’ ter fin’ out dat my boy had sta’ted de fuss. Ef I’d ’a’ had a son lack you, Brer Dan, I’d ’a’ be’n a proud nigger; oh, yas, I would, sho’s you bawn. But you ain’ lookin’ ez well ez you oughter, Brer Dan. Dey’s sump’n de matter wid you, en w’at’s mo’, I ’spec’ you dunno w’at it is.’

===Chinese===
「『不过话说回来，俺那儿子本来就是个劣货，』他说，『俺也不能指望别的。俺想教他明白他走的错路，押他去教堂、去祷告会；可全不中用。他是谁打死的，俺不知道，也不想知道；真要一查呀，十拿九稳是俺那孩子先挑的头。俺要是有你这么个儿子，丹兄弟，俺这张老脸多有光；哦，是啊，真会有的，老天在上。不过你气色可不似该有的那么好，丹兄弟。你身上有毛病；不但如此，俺看你自个儿还不知道毛病在哪儿呢。』」

===Original===
“Now, dis yer kin’ er talk nach’ly th’owed Dan off’n his gya’d, en fus’ thing he knowed he wuz talkin’ ter dis ole cunjuh man des lack he wuz one er his bes’ frien’s. He tol’ ’im all ’bout not feelin’ well in de mawnin’, en ax’ ’im ef he could tell w’at wuz de matter wid ’im.

===Chinese===
「这一套话，自然而然把丹的提防卸了个干净；等他醒过神来，他已经同这老巫师攀谈上了，亲热得像对了他顶要好的朋友。他把清早起来浑身不自在的事一五一十说了，又问巫师，能不能给瞧瞧他到底哪儿不对。」

===Original===
“ ‘Yas,’ sez de cunjuh man. ‘Dey is a witch be’n ridin’ you right ’long. I kin see de marks er de bridle on yo’ mouf. En I’ll des bet yo’ back is raw whar she’s be’n beatin’ you.’

===Chinese===
「『瞧出来了，』巫师说。『有个巫婆一直在骑着你不撒手。你嘴上勒出的笼头印子，俺都看得见。俺还敢跟你打赌，你背上叫她抽得准是稀烂。』」

===Original===
“ ‘Yas,’ ’spon’ Dan, ‘so it is.’ He hadn’ notice it befo’, but now he felt des lack de hide had be’n tuk off’n ’im.

“ ‘En yo’ thighs is des raw whar de spurrers has be’n driv’ in you,’ sez de cunjuh man. ‘You can’t see de raw spots, but you kin feel ’em.’

===Chinese===
「『可不是，』丹应道，『是烂着呢。』先前他没留神，这会儿却真觉得像叫人活剥了一层皮。

「『你大腿上，也叫马刺扎得稀烂，』巫师说，『那些烂处你瞧不见，可你摸得出、觉得着。』」

===Original===
“ ‘Oh, yas,’ ’lows Dan, ‘dey does hu’t pow’ful bad.’

“ ‘En w’at’s mo’,’ sez de cunjuh man, comin’ up close ter Dan en whusp’in’ in his yeah, ‘I knows who it is be’n ridin’ you.’

===Chinese===
「『哦，可不是，』丹说，『疼得厉害着呢。』

「『还有呢，』巫师凑到丹跟前，贴着他耳朵悄声说，『一直骑你的到底是谁，俺知道。』」

===Original===
“ ‘Who is it?’ ax’ Dan. ‘Tell me who it is.’

“ ‘It’s a’ ole nigger ’oman down by Rockfish Crick. She had a pet rabbit, en you cotch’ ’im one day, en she’s been squarin’ up wid you eber sence. But you better stop her, er e’se you’ll be rid ter def in a mont’ er so.’

===Chinese===
「『是谁？』丹问。『快告诉俺是谁。』

「『是罗克菲什溪（Rockfish Crick）边上一个老黑人婆子。她养过一只心爱的兔子，有一天叫你逮了去，打那以后，她就一直跟你清这笔账。你顶好趁早治住她；要不然，不出个把月，你就得叫她活活骑死。』」

===Original===
“ ‘No,’ sez Dan, ‘she can’t kill me, sho’.’

“ ‘I dunno how dat is,’ said de cunjuh man, ‘but she kin make yo’ life mighty mis’able. Ef I wuz in yo’ place, I’d stop her right off.’

===Chinese===
「『不能，』丹说，『她要不了俺的命，这错不了。』

「『那可说不准，』巫师道，『可她能叫你这日子过得苦不堪言。俺要是换了你，立马就把她治住。』」

===Original===
“ ‘But how is I gwine ter stop her?’ ax’ Dan. ‘I dunno nuffin ’bout stoppin’ witches.’

===Chinese===
「『可俺拿什么治住她呢？』丹问。『对付巫婆的门道，俺一窍不通。』」

===Original===
“ ‘Look a heah, Dan,’ sez de yuther; ‘you is a good young man. I lacks you monst’us well. Fac’, I feels lack some er dese days I mought buy you fum yo’ marster, ef I could eber make money ernuff at my bizness dese hard times, en ’dop’ you fer my son. I lacks you so well dat I’m gwine ter he’p you git rid er dis yer witch fer good en all; fer des ez long ez she libs, you is sho’ ter hab trouble, en trouble, en mo’ trouble.’

===Chinese===
「『你听俺说，丹，』那一位道，『你是个好后生，俺打心眼里喜欢你。说实在的，俺常想，这年头要是俺这营生能多挣出几个钱来，兴许哪天就把你从你主人手里买过来，收你当儿子。俺这么喜欢你，索性帮你把这巫婆一劳永逸地除了；因为只要她活着一天，你就免不了招灾，招灾，再招灾。』」

===Original===
“ ‘You is de bes’ frien’ I got, Unk’ Jube,’ sez Dan, ‘en I’ll ’member yo’ kin’ness ter my dyin’ day. Tell me how I kin git rid er dis yer ole witch w’at’s be’n ridin’ me so ha’d.’

===Chinese===
「『您是俺顶好的朋友，朱伯大叔，』丹说，『您这份情，俺到死都记着。快告诉俺，怎么才能除掉这个死咬着俺骑个不停的老巫婆。』」

===Original===
“ ‘In de fus’ place,’ sez de cunjuh man, ‘dis ole witch nebber comes in her own shape, but eve’y night, at ten o’clock, she tu’ns herse’f inter a black cat, en runs down ter yo’ cabin en bridles you, en mounts you, en dribes you out th’oo de chimbly, en rides you ober de roughes’ places she kin fin’. All you got ter do is ter set fer her in de bushes ’side er yo’ cabin, en hit her in de head wid a rock er a lighterd-knot w’en she goes pas’.’

===Chinese===
「『头一件，』巫师说，『这老巫婆从不拿本相来。每天夜里十点钟，她就把自己变成一只黑猫，跑到你小屋跟前，给你套上笼头，骑上你，把你从烟囱里赶出去，专拣顶崎岖的地方骑。你要做的，不过是埋伏在你小屋旁的树丛里，等她经过，拿块石头、或一截松明疙瘩（lighterd-knot），照她头上砸下去。』」

===Original===
“ ‘But,’ sez Dan, ‘how kin I see her in de da’k? En s’posen I hits at her en misses her? Er s’posen I des woun’s her, en she gits erway—w’at she gwine do ter me den?’

===Chinese===
「『可是，』丹说，『黑灯瞎火的，俺拿什么看见她？万一俺一砸砸偏了呢？再万一，俺只砸伤了她，叫她跑了——往后她要怎么整治俺？』」

===Original===
“ ‘I is done studied ’bout all dem things,’ sez de cunjuh man, ‘en it ’pears ter me de bes’ plan fer you ter foller is ter lemme tu’n you ter some creetur w’at kin see in de da’k, en w’at kin run des ez fas’ ez a cat, en w’at kin bite, en bite fer ter kill; en den you won’t hafter hab no trouble atter de job is done. I dunno whuther you’d lack dat er no, but dat is de sho’es’ way.’

===Chinese===
「『这些个，俺都替你盘算周全了，』巫师说，『依俺看，你顶稳当的一条路，是让俺把你变成一样黑夜看得见、跑起来同猫一样快、又长着牙、一口下去就要命的东西；这么着，事办完了也省得留后患。愿意不愿意，俺不知道；可这是顶十拿九稳的路。』」

===Original===
“ ‘I doan keer,’ ’spon’ Dan. ‘I’d des ez lief be anything fer a’ hour er so, ef I kin kill dat ole witch. You kin do des w’at you er mineter.’

===Chinese===
「『俺不挑，』丹应道，『只要能弄死那老巫婆，变上个把钟头，什么都成。您只管看着办。』」

===Original===
“ ‘All right, den,’ sez de cunjuh man, ‘you come down ter my cabin at half-past nine o’clock ter-night, en I’ll fix you up.’

“Now, dis cunjuh man, w’en he had got th’oo talkin’ wid Dan, kep’ on down de road ’long de side er de plantation, ’tel he met Mahaly comin’ home fum wuk des atter sundown.

===Chinese===
「『那就这么定了，』巫师说，『今儿夜里九点半，你下到俺小屋来，俺把你拾掇停当。』

「这巫师同丹把话说完，便顺着庄园边上的大路接着往前走，走到日头刚落，迎面正碰上收工回家的马哈利。」

===Original===
“ ‘Hoddy do, ma’m,’ sezee; ‘is yo’ name Sis’ Mahaly, w’at b’longs ter Mars Dugal’ McAdoo?’

“ ‘Yas,’ ’spon’ Mahaly, ‘dat’s my name, en I b’longs ter Mars Dugal’.’

===Chinese===
「『您好啊，大妹子。您就是马哈利妹子吧，杜加尔·麦卡杜老爷家里的？』

「『是啊，』马哈利应道，『俺就叫马哈利，俺是杜加尔老爷家的人。』」

===Original===
“ ‘Well,’ sezee, ‘yo’ husban’ Dan wuz down by my cabin dis ebenin’, en he got bit by a spider er sump’n, en his foot is swoll’ up so he can’t walk. En he ax’ me fer ter fin’ you en fetch you down dere ter he’p ’im home.’

“Co’se Mahaly wanter see w’at had happen’ ter Dan, en so she sta’ted down de road wid de cunjuh man. Ez soon ez he got her inter his cabin, he shet de do’, en sprinkle’ some goopher mixtry on her, en tu’nt her ter a black cat. Den he tuk ’n put her in a bairl, en put a bo’d on de bairl, en a rock on de bo’d, en lef’ her dere ’tel he got good en ready fer ter use her.

===Chinese===
「『是这样，』他说，『您家男人丹，今儿傍晚打俺小屋这边过，叫蜘蛛还是什么咬了一口，脚肿得走不动道儿了。他托俺寻着您，请您下去搭把手，搀他回家。』

「马哈利自然急着去看丹出了什么事，便跟着巫师顺路下去了。刚把她让进小屋，巫师就闩上门，往她身上撒了一把咒药（goopher mixtry），把她变成一只黑猫。随后他把她塞进一只木桶，桶口搭上木板，木板上压块石头，把她搁在那儿，等他哪天预备停当，好拿她派用场。」

===Original===
“ ’Long ’bout half-pas’ nine o’clock Dan come down ter de cunjuh man’s cabin. It wuz a wa’m night, en de do’ wuz stan’in’ open. De cunjuh man ’vited Dan ter come in, en pass’ de time er day wid ’im. Ez soon ez Dan ’mence’ talkin’, he heared a cat miauin’ en scratchin’ en gwine on at a tarrable rate.

===Chinese===
「九点半前后，丹下到了巫师的小屋。那是个暖和的夜晚，屋门敞着。巫师请丹进屋，同他寒暄了两句。丹刚一开腔，就听见一只猫又是喵喵叫、又是抓挠，闹腾得没了样子。」

===Original===
“ ‘Wat’s all dat fuss ’bout?’ ax’ Dan.

“ ‘Oh, dat ain’ nuffin but my ole gray tomcat,’ sez de cunjuh man. ‘I has ter shet ’im up sometimes fer ter keep ’im in nights, en co’se he doan lack it.

===Chinese===
「『那儿闹腾什么呢？』丹问。

「『哦，不碍事，是俺那只大灰雄猫，』巫师说，『有时夜里得把他关起来，省得他往外跑；他自然不乐意。』」

===Original===
“ ‘Now,’ ’lows de cunjuh man, ‘lemme tell you des w’at you is got ter do. W’en you ketches dis witch, you mus’ take her right by de th’oat en bite her right th’oo de neck. Be sho’ yo’ teef goes th’oo at de fus’ bite, en den you won’t nebber be bothe’d no mo’ by dat witch. En w’en you git done, come back heah en I’ll tu’n you ter yo’se’f ag’in, so you kin go home en git yo’ night’s res’.’

===Chinese===
「『听好，』巫师说，『你该怎么行事，俺一条一条说给你。等你逮住这巫婆，你要一把拿住她的咽喉，一口把她的脖子咬穿。千万记牢，头一口就要咬透；这么着，往后那巫婆就再也搅扰不着你了。事办完了，你回到这儿来，俺再把你变回原身，你也好回家安睡一夜。』」

===Original===
“Den de cunjuh man gun Dan sump’n nice en sweet ter drink out’n a new go’d, en in ’bout a minute Dan foun’ hisse’f tu’nt ter a gray wolf; en soon ez he felt all fo’ er his noo feet on de groun’, he sta’ted off fas’ ez he could fer his own cabin, so he could be sho’ en be dere time ernuff ter ketch de witch, en put a’ een’ ter her kyarin’s-on.

===Chinese===
「随后，巫师拿一只新葫芦，舀了些又香又甜的东西给丹喝。约莫过了一分钟，丹发觉自己变成了一头灰狼；四只新脚爪一沾地，他便撒开腿，拼命往自家小屋跑，好稳稳赶在时辰前头截住那巫婆，把她那些营生一笔勾销。」

===Original===
“Ez soon ez Dan wuz gone good, de cunjuh man tuk de rock off’n de bo’d, en de bo’d off’n de bairl, en out le’p’ Mahaly en sta’ted fer ter go home, des lack a cat er a ’oman er anybody e’se would w’at wuz in trouble; en it wa’n’t many minutes befo’ she wuz gwine up de path ter her own do’.

===Chinese===
「丹走利索了，巫师便搬开木板上的石头，揭掉木桶上的板；马哈利一纵身跳出来，撒腿就往家跑——猫也好，女人也好，随便谁落了难都会这样；没几分钟，她已经踏上通向自家门口的小道。」

===Original===
“Meanw’iles, w’en Dan had retch’ de cabin, he had hid hisse’f in a bunch er jimson weeds in de ya’d. He hadn’ wait’ long befo’ he seed a black cat run up de path to’ds de do’. Des ez soon ez she got close ter ’im, he le’p’ out en ketch’ her by de th’oat, en got a grip on her, des lack de cunjuh man had tol’ ’im ter do. En lo en behol’! no sooner had de blood ’mence’ ter flow dan de black cat tu’nt back ter Mahaly, en Dan seed dat he had killt his own wife. En w’iles her bref wuz gwine she call’ out:

“ ‘*O* Dan! *O* my husban’! come en he’p me! come en sabe me fum dis wolf w’at’s killin’ me!’

===Chinese===
「这厢丹一到小屋，就把自己藏进了院子里一丛曼陀罗草。没等多大工夫，他就看见一只黑猫顺着小道朝门口跑来。她刚凑近，他便一纵身扑出去，一口咬住她的咽喉，钳得死死的，全照巫师教的办。哪承想！血刚开始淌，那黑猫就变回了马哈利，丹这才看清，他咬死的是自己的媳妇。她还有一口气的工夫，喊出了声——

「『*哦*，丹！*哦*，俺的男人！快来帮俺！快来把俺从这头杀俺的狼嘴里救出去啊！』」

===Original===
“W’en po’ Dan sta’ted to’ds her, ez any man nach’ly would, it des made her holler wuss en wuss; fer she didn’ knowed dis yer wolf wuz her Dan. En Dan des had ter hide in de weeds, en grit his teef en hol’ hisse’f in, ’tel she passed out’n her mis’ry, callin’ fer Dan ter de las’, en wond’rin’ w’y he didn’ come en he’p her. En Dan ’lowed ter hisse’f he’d ruther ’a’ be’n killt a dozen times ’n ter ’a’ done w’at he had ter Mahaly.

===Chinese===
「可怜的丹听见喊声，任谁都会奔过去的，他自然也朝她奔去；谁知这一来，倒叫她喊得一声惨过一声；因为她不知道，这头狼就是她的丹。丹只得缩回草丛，咬紧牙关，死死忍住，直到她咽了气、脱了这场苦；到末一口气，她还在喊丹，还纳闷他怎么不来救她。丹心里想：宁可叫人打死十回，也强似做出他方才对马哈利做出的事。」

===Original===
“Dan wuz mighty nigh ’stracted, but w’en Mahaly wuz dead en he got his min’ straighten’ out a little, it didn’ take ’im mo’ d’n a minute er so fer ter see th’oo all de cunjuh man’s lies, en how de cunjuh man had fooled ’im en made ’im kill Mahaly, fer ter git eben wid ’im fer killin’ er his son. He kep’ gittin’ madder en madder, en Mahaly hadn’ much mo’ d’n drawed her’ las bref befo’ he sta’ted back ter de cunjuh man’s cabin ha’d ez he could run.

===Chinese===
「丹简直急得发了疯；可等马哈利死了、他的神志稍稍归拢，不消一分钟，他就把巫师的谎话从头到尾看穿了——巫师是怎样愚弄他、支使他杀掉马哈利，好报他打死儿子那笔仇。他越想越怒；马哈利最后一口气还没咽利索，他已经掉转身，朝巫师的小屋没命地奔去。」

===Original===
“W’en he got dere, de do’ wuz stan’in’ open; a lighterd-knot wuz flick’rin’ on de h’a’th, en de ole cunjuh man wuz settin’ dere noddin’ in de corner. Dan le’p’ in de do’ en jump’ fer dis man’s th’oat, en got de same grip on ’im w’at de cunjuh man had tol’ ’im ’bout half a’ hour befo’. It wuz ha’d wuk dis time, fer de ole man’s neck wuz monst’us tough en stringy, but Dan hilt on long ernuff ter be sho’ his job wuz done right. En eben den he didn’ hol’ on long ernuff; fer w’en he tu’nt de cunjuh man loose en he fell ober on de flo’, de cunjuh man rollt his eyes at Dan, en sezee:—

===Chinese===
「他赶到时，屋门敞着；炉膛里一截松明疙瘩忽闪着火光，老巫师正坐在角落里打盹。丹从门口一跃而入，直奔这人的咽喉，用的正是半个钟头前巫师教他的那个咬口。这一回是硬仗：老头子的脖子又老又韧、筋筋拉拉；可丹死咬着不放，直咬到他敢保这桩活计做得万无一失。饶是这样，他还是撒口撒早了——他一松开，巫师便栽倒在地板上，翻起眼睛望着丹，说道——」

===Original===
“ ‘I’s eben wid you, Brer Dan, en you er eben wid me; you killt my son en I killt yo’ ’oman. En ez I doan want no mo’ d’n w’at’s fair ’bout dis thing, ef you’ll retch up wid yo’ paw en take down dat go’d hangin’ on dat peg ober de chimbly, en take a sip er dat mixtry, it’ll tu’n you back ter a nigger ag’in, en I kin die mo’ sad’sfied ’n ef I lef’ you lack you is.’

===Chinese===
「『咱俩两清了，丹兄弟：你打死俺儿子，俺弄死你女人。这件事上，俺不想多占你一分；你只消抬起爪子，把烟囱上头钉子上挂的那只葫芦取下来，抿一口里头的药水，你就变回黑人；俺死也死得比留你这副模样心满意足。』」

===Original===
“Dan nebber ’lowed fer a minute dat a man would lie wid his las’ bref, en co’se he seed de sense er gittin’ tu’nt back befo’ de cunjuh man died; so he clumb on a chair en retch’ fer de go’d, en tuk a sip er de mixtry. En ez soon ez he’d done dat de cunjuh man lafft his las’ laf, en gapsed out wid ’is las’ gaps:—

===Chinese===
「丹压根儿没想过，一个人临了咽气还会撒谎；他自然也明白，赶在巫师死前变回原身才是正理。于是他爬上椅子，够下那只葫芦，抿了一口那药水。他刚咽下去，巫师便发出最后一声笑，喘着最后几口气，漏出话来——」

===Original===
“ ‘Uh huh! I reckon I’s square wid you now fer killin’ me, too; fer dat goopher on you is done fix’ en sot now fer good, en all de cunj’in’ in de worl’ won’t nebber take it off.

> ‘Wolf you is en wolf you stays,
 

 All de rest er yo’ bawn days.’

===Chinese===
「『呃哼！连你咬死俺这一桩，俺想这回也算跟你两讫了；你身上那道咒，如今可上死了扣、定死了局——天底下多少巫术，也再休想把它褪掉。

> 『你做了狼，就永远是狼，
 
 余下的阳寿，天天做狼。』」

===Original===
“Co’se Brer Dan couldn’ do nuffin. He knowed it wa’n’t no use, but he clumb up on de chimbly en got down de go’ds en bottles en yuther cunjuh fixin’s, en tried ’em all on hisse’f, but dey didn’ do no good. Den he run down ter ole Aun’ Peggy, but she didn’ know de wolf langwidge, en couldn’t ’a’ tuk off dis yuther goopher nohow, eben ef she’d ’a’ unnerstood w’at Dan wuz sayin’. So po’ Dan wuz bleedgd ter be a wolf all de rest er his bawn days.

===Chinese===
「丹兄弟自然再没了法子。他明知无用，还是爬上烟囱，把葫芦、瓶子同旁的巫术家什都取下来，一样样往自己身上使，可全不见效。他又跑下老佩姬大娘那儿去；可她不懂狼的话，就算听得懂丹说的是什么，这道旁人下的咒，她也照样解不开。就这样，可怜的丹只落得做了一辈子的狼，到死不得脱身。」

===Original===
“Dey foun’ Mahaly down by her own cabin nex’ mawnin’, en eve’ybody made a great ’miration ’bout how she’d be’n killt. De niggers ’lowed a wolf had bit her. De w’ite folks say no, dey ain’ be’n no wolves ’roun’ dere fer ten yeahs er mo’; en dey didn’ know w’at ter make out’n it. En w’en dey couldn’ fin’ Dan nowhar, dey ’lowed he’d quo’lled wid Mahaly en killt her, en run erway; en dey didn’ know w’at ter make er dat, fer Dan en Mahaly wuz de mos’ lovin’ couple on de plantation. Dey put de dawgs on Dan’s scent, en track’ ’im down ter ole Unk’ Jube’s cabin, en foun’ de ole man dead, en dey didn’ know w’at ter make er dat; en den Dan’s scent gun out, en dey didn’ know w’at ter make er dat. Mars Dugal’ tuk on a heap ’bout losin’ two er his bes’ han’s in one day, en ole missis ’lowed it wuz a jedgment on ’im fer sump’n he’d done. But dat fall de craps wuz monst’us big, so Mars Dugal’ say de Lawd had temper’ de win’ ter de sho’n ram, en make up ter ’im fer w’at he had los’.

===Chinese===
「第二天早上，人们在马哈利自家小屋边找到了她；她怎么遭的毒手，人人都惊诧了一回。黑人们说，是狼咬死了她。白人说不对，这一带十多年没见过狼了；这事儿他们也说不出个道道。后来他们又哪儿都寻不着丹，便说准是丹同马哈利拌了嘴，打死她跑了；可这个他们也对不上号——丹和马哈利，本是庄园上顶恩爱的一对。他们放狗衔着丹的气味追下去，一路追到老朱伯大叔的小屋，见老头子死了——这他们也纳闷；再往前，丹的气味断了——这他们更纳闷。杜加尔老爷一天里折了两名顶好的劳力，着实痛惜了一场；老夫人说，这是他做下什么亏心事，遭了天谴。可那年秋天，收成好得出奇；杜加尔老爷便说，可见老天爷体恤剪了毛的羊、特意把风放软了，把他损失的都补还给他了。」

===Original===
“Dey buried Mahaly down in dat piece er low groun’ you er talkin’ ’bout cl’arin’ up. Ez fer po’ Dan, he didn’ hab nowhar e’se ter go, so he des stayed ’roun’ Mahaly’s grabe, w’en he wa’n’t out in de yuther woods gittin’ sump’n ter eat. En sometimes, w’en night would come, de niggers useter heah him howlin’ en howlin’ down dere, des fittin’ ter break his hea’t. En den some mo’ un ’em said dey seed Mahaly’s ha’nt dere ’bun’ance er times, colloguin’ wid dis gray wolf. En eben now, fifty yeahs sence, long atter ole Dan has died en dried up in de woods, his ha’nt en Mahaly’s hangs ’roun’ dat piece er low groun’, en eve’body w’at goes ’bout dere has some bad luck er ’nuther; fer ha’nts doan lack ter be ’sturb’ on dey own stompin’-groun’.”

===Chinese===
「他们把马哈利埋进了您正说要清理的那片洼地。至于可怜的丹，他没处可去，就守在马哈利坟边转悠，不出去寻吃食的时候，总在那儿。有时夜里来了，黑人们听得见他在下面嚎啊、嚎啊，嚎得人心里发碎。后来又有人接话说，他们多少回瞧见马哈利的鬼魂在那儿，同这头灰狼偎在一处絮絮低语。直到如今，五十年过去，老丹早死在林子里、干得没了形迹，他的鬼魂同马哈利的还恋着那片洼地；凡到那一带走动的人，没有不沾上几分霉运的——鬼魂这东西，最见不得人踩到自家地盘上来搅扰。」

===Original===
The air had darkened while the old man related this harrowing tale. The rising wind whistled around the eaves, slammed the loose window-shutters, and, still increasing, drove the rain in fiercer gusts into the piazza. As Julius finished his story and we rose to seek shelter within doors, the blast caught the angle of some chimney or gable in the rear of the house, and bore to our ears a long, wailing note, an epitome, as it were, of remorse and hopelessness.

===Chinese===
老人讲这段揪心故事的当口，天色愈发黑了。风渐渐大起来，绕着屋檐呼啸，摔打松动的窗板，风势越增越猛，把雨一阵紧似一阵地卷上游廊。朱利叶斯刚把故事讲完，我们起身要进屋避一避，一阵狂风正撞上屋后哪座烟囱或山墙的棱角，给我们送来一长声呜咽似的哀音——简直像悔恨与无望的一个总括。

===Original===
“Dat’s des lack po’ ole Dan useter howl,” observed Julius, as he reached for his umbrella, “en w’at I be’n tellin’ you is de reason I doan lack ter see dat neck er woods cl’ared up. Co’se it b’longs ter you, en a man kin do ez he choose’ wid ’is own. But ef you gits rheumatiz er fever en agur, er ef you er snake-bit er p’isen’ wid some yarb er ’nuther, er ef a tree falls on you, er a ha’nt runs you en makes you git ’stracted in yo’ min’, lack some folks I knows w’at went foolin’ ’roun’ dat piece er lan’, you can’t say I neber wa’ned you, suh, en tol’ you w’at you mought look fer en be sho’ ter fin’.”

===Chinese===
「可怜的老丹从前就是这么嚎的，」朱利叶斯说着，伸手去取他的伞，「俺方才讲给您二位听的，就是俺不愿意看着那片林子叫清理掉的缘故。那地自然是您的；自家的东西，愿意怎么处置就怎么处置。可往后您要是得了风湿、发了热、打起摆子，或是叫蛇咬了、叫什么草药毒着了，或是叫倒下来的树砸了，或是叫鬼魂撵得失了心神——就像俺认得的几个到那块地上乱闯的人那样——您可不能说，俺朱利叶斯打没提醒过您，先生，也没少告诉您：您会撞上什么，而且是保准撞上。」

---

===Original===
When I cleared up the land in question, which was not until the following year, I recalled the story Julius had told us, and looked in vain for a sunken grave or perhaps a few weather-bleached bones of some denizen of the forest. I cannot say, of course, that someone had not been buried there; but if so, the hand of time had long since removed any evidence of the fact. If some lone wolf, the last of his pack, had once made his den there, his bones had long since crumbled into dust and gone to fertilize the rank vegetation that formed the undergrowth of this wild spot. I did find, however, a bee-tree in the woods, with an ample cavity in its trunk, and an opening through which convenient access could be had to the stores of honey within. I have reason to believe that ever since I had bought the place, and for many years before, Julius had been getting honey from this tree. The gray wolf’s haunt had doubtless proved useful in keeping off too inquisitive people, who might have interfered with his monopoly.

===Chinese===
待到我清理那块地——那已是第二年——我想起了朱利叶斯讲给我们听的故事，特意寻一座下陷的坟，或者林中哪位旧客经风漂白的几根枯骨，却是枉然。当然，我不能说那儿没埋过人；但纵然埋过，时光之手也早把一切凭据收拾干净了。倘若有哪只孤狼——狼群中最后的一只——曾在那里做过窝，它的骸骨也早已碎作尘土，充了肥料，养肥了这片荒地上疯长的下层草木。不过，我倒当真在林子里寻见一棵蜂树：树干里有个宽敞的空洞，开着一道口子，打那儿取用里头的存蜜，再顺手不过。我有理由相信：打我买下这处产业起，还更早许多年，朱利叶斯就一直打这棵树上取蜜。那灰狼的鬼魂，想必是大有用场的——它挡开了太多好事多问的人，不然，人家早搅了他这门独一份的买卖了。

## Hot-Foot Hannibal / 热脚汉尼拔

===Original===
“I hate you and despise you! I wish never to see you or speak to you again!”

“Very well; I will take care that henceforth you have no opportunity to do either.”

===Chinese===
「我恨你，瞧不起你！但愿今生今世再也不要见到你，再也不要跟你说话！」

「很好；往后我会留神，叫你两样都没有机会。」

===Original===
These words﻿—the first in the passionately vibrant tones of my sister-in-law, and the latter in the deeper and more restrained accents of an angry man﻿—startled me from my nap. I had been dozing in my hammock on the front piazza, behind the honeysuckle vine. I had been faintly aware of a buzz of conversation in the parlor, but had not at all awakened to its import until these sentences fell, or, I might rather say, were hurled upon my ear. I presume the young people had either not seen me lying there﻿—the Venetian blinds opening from the parlor windows upon the piazza were partly closed on account of the heat﻿—or else in their excitement they had forgotten my proximity.

===Chinese===
这几句话——前一句出自我妻妹之口，音调激越颤动；后一句出自一个怒极的男人，嗓音更深沉、更克制——把我从午睡中惊醒过来。我本来正在屋前游廊的吊床上打盹，躲在金银花藤后面。客厅里有一片嗡嗡的话音，我一直隐隐约约有所察觉，却全然不曾醒悟其中的分量，直到这几句话落下来——或者不如说，简直是劈头砸到我耳朵上。我估摸着，这两个年轻人要么压根没瞧见我躺在那儿——客厅窗子通游廊的软百叶帘因为天热拉上了一半——要么就是激动过了头，把我就在近旁这回事给忘了。

===Original===
I felt somewhat concerned. The young man, I had remarked, was proud, firm, jealous of the point of honor, and, from my observation of him, quite likely to resent to the bitter end what he deemed a slight or an injustice. The girl, I knew, was quite as high-spirited as young Murchison. I feared she was not so just, and hoped she would prove more yielding. I knew that her affections were strong and enduring, but that her temperament was capricious, and her sunniest moods easily overcast by some small cloud of jealousy or pique. I had never imagined, however, that she was capable of such intensity as was revealed by these few words of hers. As I say, I felt concerned. I had learned to like Malcolm Murchison, and had heartily consented to his marriage with my ward; for it was in that capacity that I had stood for a year or two to my wife’s younger sister, Mabel. The match thus rudely broken off had promised to be another link binding me to the kindly Southern people among whom I had not long before taken up my residence.

===Chinese===
我心里不免有几分担忧。据我观察，这年轻人自尊，倔强，在脸面荣辱上寸步不让；凡是他认定的轻慢或不公，照我对他的了解，他多半会衔恨到底。至于那姑娘，我知道，性子同年轻的默奇森一样刚烈；我只怕她不那么讲道理，又指望她能多让着些。我知道她感情深挚持久，也知道她性子喜怒无常，最晴朗的心情也容易叫一小片嫉妒或怄气的阴云遮暗。只是我从未想到，她竟有这几句话里露出来的这般激烈的心性。正如方才所说，我心里不免担忧。我本已渐渐喜欢上了马尔科姆·默奇森（Malcolm Murchison），由衷赞成他同我的受监护人结婚——我正是以这个身份，照看了妻子的妹妹梅贝尔（Mabel）一两年。这门就这样被粗暴断送的亲事，本有望再添一条纽带，把我同这些和善的南方人联结起来——我不久前才在他们中间安下家来。

===Original===
Young Murchison came out of the door, cleared the piazza in two strides without seeming aware of my presence, and went off down the lane at a furious pace. A few moments later Mabel began playing the piano loudly, with a touch that indicated anger and pride and independence and a dash of exultation, as though she were really glad that she had driven away forever the young man whom the day before she had loved with all the ardor of a first passion.

===Chinese===
年轻的默奇森从门里出来，两大步跨过游廊，仿佛全没察觉我在跟前，顺着小巷怒冲冲疾走而去。过了片刻，梅贝尔用力弹起钢琴来，那指触里透着恼怒、傲气、独立不羁，还夹着一丝扬眉吐气，仿佛她当真心存快意，把前一天还以初恋的全部热烈爱着的年轻人，永远赶走了。

===Original===
I hoped that time might heal the breach and bring the two young people together again. I told my wife what I had overheard. In return she gave me Mabel’s version of the affair.

===Chinese===
我指望时间能弥合这道裂痕，让两个年轻人重新走到一处。我把偷听来的话告诉了妻子；作为交换，她给我讲了梅贝尔说法里这段事的来历。

===Original===
“I do not see how it can ever be settled,” my wife said. “It is something more than a mere lovers’ quarrel. It began, it is true, because she found fault with him for going to church with that hateful Branson girl. But before it ended there were things said that no woman of any spirit could stand. I am afraid it is all over between them.”

===Chinese===
「我看不出这事还怎么收拾，」我妻子说。「这可不是一场寻常的情人拌嘴。起因呢，的确是她怪他陪着那个讨人嫌的布兰森（Branson）家姑娘上教堂。可是吵到后来，有些话说出口，是任何一个有血性的女人都咽不下的。恐怕他们两个是彻底完了。」

===Original===
I was sorry to hear this. In spite of the very firm attitude taken by my wife and her sister, I still hoped that the quarrel would be made up within a day or two. Nevertheless, when a week had passed with no word from young Murchison, and with no sign of relenting on Mabel’s part, I began to think myself mistaken.

===Chinese===
这话我听着心里难受。尽管妻子和她妹妹态度都非常坚决，我仍指望这一场口角不出一两天就会言归于好。然而一个礼拜过去，年轻的默奇森音信全无，梅贝尔那边也不见半分软化，我开始觉得自己想错了。

===Original===
One pleasant afternoon, about ten days after the rupture, old Julius drove the rockaway up to the piazza, and my wife, Mabel, and I took our seats for a drive to a neighbor’s vineyard, over on the Lumberton plank-road.

===Chinese===
决裂后大约第十天，一个晴爽宜人的下午，老朱利叶斯把四轮敞篷马车赶到游廊跟前，我妻子、梅贝尔和我坐上去，要到伦伯顿木板路那头一位邻家的葡萄园兜风去。

===Original===
“Which way shall we go,” I asked﻿—“the short road or the long one?”

“I guess we had better take the short road,” answered my wife. “We will get there sooner.”

“It’s a mighty fine dribe roun’ by de big road, Mis’ Annie,” observed Julius, “en it doan take much longer to git dere.”

“No,” said my wife, “I think we will go by the short road. There is a bay-tree in blossom near the mineral spring, and I wish to get some of the flowers.”

“I ’spec’s you’d fin’ some bay-trees ’long de big road, ma’m,” suggested Julius.

“But I know about the flowers on the short road, and they are the ones I want.”

===Chinese===
「走哪条路，」我问，「近路还是远路？」

「我看还是走近路好，」妻子答道。「到得快些。」

「打大路绕过去，一路可是难得的好景致呐，安妮太太，」朱利叶斯搭话道，「到那边也多费不了多少工夫。」

「不，」我妻子说，「我想还是走近路。矿泉旁边有一棵月桂树正开着花，我想采些花。」

「俺寻思大路沿途您也能碰上几棵月桂树呐，太太。」朱利叶斯提点道。

「可近路那棵的花我摸得准，我要的就是那棵。」

===Original===
We drove down the lane to the highway, and soon struck into the short road leading past the mineral spring. Our route lay partly through a swamp, and on each side the dark, umbrageous foliage, unbroken by any clearing, lent to the road solemnity, and to the air a refreshing coolness. About half a mile from the house, and about halfway to the mineral spring, we stopped at the tree of which my wife had spoken, and reaching up to the low-hanging boughs, I gathered a dozen of the fragrant white flowers. When I resumed my seat in the rockaway, Julius started the mare. She went on for a few rods, until we had reached the edge of a branch crossing the road, when she stopped short.

===Chinese===
马车出了小巷上了大道，不久便拐进经过矿泉的那条近路。我们的路有一段穿行在沼泽里，两旁浓荫匝地的深色树冠，不曾被任何开垦地豁开，给这条路平添了几分肃穆，也给空气送来沁人的凉意。离我家约半英里、离矿泉约半程的地方，我们在妻子说到的那棵树前停了车；我探手够着低垂的枝条，采下一打芬芳的白花。等我重新在马车里坐定，朱利叶斯吆喝起母马。它往前走了没几杆，到了横过路面的一道小溪边上，猛地站住了。

===Original===
“Why did you stop, Julius?” I asked.

“I didn’, suh,” he replied. “ ‘T wuz de mare stop’. G’ ’long dere, Lucy! Wat you mean by dis foolis’ness?”

Julius jerked the reins and applied the whip lightly, but the mare did not stir.

“Perhaps you had better get down and lead her,” I suggested. “If you get her started, you can cross on the log and keep your feet dry.”

Julius alighted, took hold of the bridle, and vainly essayed to make the mare move. She planted her feet with even more evident obstinacy.

“I don’t know what to make of this,” I said. “I have never known her to balk before. Have you, Julius?”

===Chinese===
「怎么停下了，朱利叶斯？」我问。

「不是俺停的，先生，」他答道。「是马自己停的。走哇，露西（Lucy）！你这是犯的哪门子糊涂？」

朱利叶斯拽了拽缰绳，又轻轻抽了一鞭，母马却纹丝不动。

「要不你下来牵着它走，」我提议道。「只要把它带起了步，就能踩着那根圆木过溪，鞋也湿不了。」

朱利叶斯跳下车，抓住笼头，想牵母马挪步，却是徒劳。它四蹄钉在地上，犟得越发分明。

「这我可弄不明白了，」我说。「我从没见它闹过倔。你见过吗，朱利叶斯？」

===Original===
“No, suh,” replied the old man, “I neber has. It’s a cu’ous thing ter me, suh.”

“What’s the best way to make her go?”

“I ’spec’s, suh, dat ef I’d tu’n her ’roun’, she’d go de udder way.”

“But we want her to go this way.”

“Well, suh, I ’low ef we des set heah fo’ er fibe minutes, she’ll sta’t up by herse’f.”

“All right,” I rejoined; “it is cooler here than any place I have struck today. We’ll let her stand for a while, and see what she does.”

===Chinese===
「没见过，先生，」老人答道，「俺也从没见过。这事俺也纳闷得很呐，先生。」

「怎么才能叫它走？」

「俺寻思啊，先生，要是把它掉个头，它保准往另一边走。」

「可我们要它往这边走。」

「要不这样，先生，俺琢磨咱们就搁这儿坐上个五分钟，它自个儿就会起步了。」

「也好，」我应道，「这儿比今天走过的哪一处都凉快。就让它站一会儿，看它怎么样。」

===Original===
We had sat in silence for a few minutes, when Julius suddenly ejaculated, “Uh huh! I knows w’y dis mare doan go. It des flash’ ’cross my recommemb’ance.”

===Chinese===
我们默默坐了几分钟，朱利叶斯忽然叫出声来：「哦嗬！俺知道这马干吗不走了。俺这一下猛然想起来了。」

===Original===
“Why is it, Julius?” I inquired.

“’Ca’se she sees Chloe.”

“Where is Chloe?” I demanded.

“Chloe’s done be’n dead dese fo’ty years er mo’,” the old man returned. “Her ha’nt is settin’ ober yander on de udder side er de branch, unner dat willer-tree, dis blessed minute.”

===Chinese===
「怎么回事，朱利叶斯？」我问。

「因为它瞧见克洛伊（Chloe）了。」

「克洛伊在哪儿？」我追问。

「克洛伊已经死了四十来年啦，」老人回道。「她的鬼魂（ha’nt）这会儿就坐在溪那边那棵柳树底下，千真万确，就在这当口。」

===Original===
“Why, Julius!” said my wife, “do you see the haunt?”

“No’m,” he answered, shaking his head, “I doan see ’er, but de mare sees ’er.”

“How do you know?” I inquired.

“Well, suh, dis yer is a gray hoss, en dis yer is a Friday; en a gray hoss kin alluz see a ha’nt w’at walks on Friday.”

===Chinese===
「哎呀，朱利叶斯！」我妻子说，「你看见那作祟的鬼魂了？」

「没有，太太，」他摇头答道，「俺看不见她，可这马看得见。」

「你怎么知道？」我问。

「是这么回事，先生，这是匹灰马，今儿个又是礼拜五；礼拜五出来游荡的鬼魂，灰马向来瞧得见。」

===Original===
“Who was Chloe?” said Mabel.

“And why does Chloe’s haunt walk?” asked my wife.

“It’s all in de tale, ma’m,” Julius replied, with a deep sigh. “It’s all in de tale.”

“Tell us the tale,” I said. “Perhaps, by the time you get through, the haunt will go away and the mare will cross.”

I was willing to humor the old man’s fancy. He had not told us a story for some time; and the dark and solemn swamp around us; the amber-colored stream flowing silently and sluggishly at our feet, like the waters of Lethe; the heavy, aromatic scent of the bays, faintly suggestive of funeral wreaths﻿—all made the place an ideal one for a ghost story.

===Chinese===
「克洛伊是谁？」梅贝尔问。

「克洛伊的鬼魂为什么出来游荡？」我妻子问。

「都在这段故事里头呐，太太，」朱利叶斯深深叹了口气，答道。「都在这段故事里头。」

「那就讲讲吧，」我说。「说不定等你讲完，鬼魂走了，马也就过溪了。」

我乐得顺着老人这份奇想。他有好一阵没给我们讲故事了；四下里幽暗肃穆的沼泽；脚边琥珀色的溪水悄然缓缓地流着，如同忘川之水；月桂浓重的香气，隐隐叫人想起葬礼的花圈——这一切都使这里成了讲鬼故事的绝好所在。

===Original===
“Chloe,” Julius began in a subdued tone, “use’ ter b’long ter ole Mars’ Dugal’ McAdoo﻿—my ole marster. She wuz a lackly gal en a smart gal, en ole mis’ tuk her up ter de big house, en l’arnt her ter wait on de w’ite folks, ’tel bimeby she come ter be mis’s own maid, en ’peared ter ’low she run de house herse’f, ter heah her talk erbout it. I wuz a young boy den, en use’ ter wuk ’bout de stables, so I knowed eve’ythin’ dat wuz gwine on ’roun’ de plantation.

===Chinese===
「克洛伊，」朱利叶斯压低了嗓音开讲，「从前的主人是老杜加尔·麦卡杜（Dugal’ McAdoo）老爷——俺的老主人。她是个标致姑娘，又机灵，老夫人把她提拔到大宅去，调教她伺候白人，到后来她成了夫人的贴身丫头，听她讲起来的口气，仿佛大宅里里外外都是她一个人当家似的。俺那会儿还是个半大小子，在马棚一带做活，所以庄园上零零碎碎的事，俺没有不知道的。

===Original===
“Well, one time Mars’ Dugal’ wanted a house boy, en sont down ter de qua’ters fer ter hab Jeff en Hannibal come up ter de big house nex’ mawnin’. Ole marster en ole mis’ look’ de two boys ober, en ’sco’sed wid deyse’ves fer a little w’ile, en den Mars’ Dugal’ sez, sezee:—

“ ‘We likes Hannibal de bes’, en we gwine ter keep him. Heah, Hannibal, you’ll wuk at de house fum now on. En ef you er a good nigger en min’s yo’ bizness, I’ll gib you Chloe fer a wife nex’ spring. You other nigger, you Jeff, you kin go back ter de qua’ters. We ain’ gwine ter need you.’

===Chinese===
「这么说吧，有一回，杜加尔老爷想添个宅子里当差的小子，就打发人到黑奴住区传话，叫杰夫（Jeff）和汉尼拔（Hannibal）第二天一早上大宅来。老主人和老夫人把两个小子上下打量了一番，私下合计了一小会儿，然后杜加尔老爷开了口，他说：——

「『我们相中的是汉尼拔，要把他留下。听着，汉尼拔，打今儿起你就在宅子里当差。你要是当个规规矩矩的黑鬼，安分守己，明年开春我就把克洛伊许给你做老婆。你，另一个黑鬼——杰夫，你回住区去吧。我们用不着你。』

===Original===
“Now Chloe had be’n stan’in’ dere behin’ ole mis’ dyoin’ all er dis yer talk, en Chloe made up her min’ fum de ve’y fus’ minute she sot eyes on dem two dat she didn’ lack dat nigger Hannibal, en wa’n’t neber gwine keer fer ’im, en she wuz des ez sho’ dat she lack’ Jeff, en wuz gwine ter set sto’ by ’im, whuther Mars’ Dugal’ tuk ’im in de big house er no; en so co’se Chloe wuz monst’us sorry w’en ole Mars’ Dugal’ tuk Hannibal en sont Jeff back. So she slip’ roun’ de house en waylaid Jeff on de way back ter de qua’ters, en tol’ ’im not ter be down-hea’ted, fer she wuz gwine ter see ef she couldn’ fin’ some way er ’nuther ter git rid er dat nigger Hannibal, en git Jeff up ter de house in his place.

===Chinese===
「克洛伊呢，这些话从头到尾都站在老夫人身后听着。打第一眼瞧见他们两个起，克洛伊就拿定了主意：她不喜欢那个黑人汉尼拔，往后也决计不会把他放在心上；同时她笃定自己喜欢杰夫，不管杜加尔老爷留不留他进大宅，她都要把他搁在心尖上。所以老杜加尔老爷留下汉尼拔、把杰夫打发回去的时候，克洛伊自然伤心得不行。于是她绕过大宅，在回住区的半道上截住杰夫，叫他别灰心丧气，说她要想法子——不拘什么法子——甩开那个黑人汉尼拔，好把杰夫弄进大宅去顶他的缺。

===Original===
“De noo house boy kotch’ on monst’us fas’, en it wa’n’t no time ha’dly befo’ Mars’ Dugal’ en ole mis’ bofe ’mence’ ter ’low Hannibal wuz de bes’ house boy dey eber had. He wuz peart en soopl’, quick ez lightnin’, en sha’p ez a razor. But Chloe didn’ lack his ways. He wuz so sho’ he wuz gwine ter git ’er in de spring, dat he didn’ ’pear ter ’low he had ter do any co’tin’, en w’en he’d run ’cross Chloe ’bout de house, he’d swell roun’ ’er in a biggity way en say:—

“ ‘Come heah en kiss me, honey. You gwine ter be mine in de spring. You doan ’pear ter be ez fon’ er me ez you oughter be.’

===Chinese===
「新来的宅童上手快得不得了，没多大工夫，杜加尔老爷和老夫人就都夸开了，说汉尼拔是他们使唤过的最好的宅童。他精神、利索，快得像闪电，锋利得像剃刀。可克洛伊看不惯他那副做派。他拿准了开春就能把她弄到手，就仿佛犯不着再献什么殷勤；在宅子里撞见克洛伊，他就神气活现地凑到她跟前，说：——

「『过来亲亲俺，宝贝儿。开春你就是俺的人了。你瞧着，可不像你该有的那么稀罕俺呐。』

===Original===
“Chloe didn’ keer nuffin fer Hannibal, en hadn’ keered nuffin fer ’im, en she sot des ez much sto’ by Jeff ez she did de day she fus’ laid eyes on ’im. En de mo’ fermilyus dis yer Hannibal got, de mo’ Chloe let her min’ run on Jeff, en one ebenin’ she went down ter de qua’ers en watch’, ’tel she got a chance fer ter talk wid ’im by hisse’f. En she tol’ Jeff fer ter go down en see ole Aun’ Peggy, de cunjuh ’oman down by de Wim’l’ton Road, en ax her ter gib ’im sump’n ter he’p git Hannibal out’n de big house, so de w’ite folks u’d sen’ fer Jeff ag’in. En bein’ ez Jeff didn’ hab nuffin ter gib Aun’ Peggy, Chloe gun ’im a silber dollah en a silk han’kercher fer ter pay her wid, fer Aun’ Peggy neber lack ter wuk fer nobody fer nuffin.

===Chinese===
「克洛伊对汉尼拔半点意思也没有，从来就没有过；她把杰夫搁在心尖上，分量同她头一回瞧见他那天一般无二。这汉尼拔越是跟她熟不拘礼，克洛伊的心思越是往杰夫身上跑。一天傍晚，她下到住区去守着，瞅准个机会，同杰夫单独说上了话。她叫杰夫去见见住在威明顿大道边上的老巫女佩姬大娘，求她给点什么，好把汉尼拔从大宅里弄出去，叫白人重新来传杰夫。杰夫两手空空，没什么好孝敬佩姬大娘的，克洛伊就给了他一块银元、一条绸手绢，叫他拿去当谢礼——佩姬大娘从来不肯白白给谁办事。

===Original===
“So Jeff slip’ off down ter Aun’ Peggy’s one night, en gun ’er de present he brung, en tol’ ’er all ’bout ’im en Chloe en Hannibal, en ax’ ’er ter he’p ’im out. Aun’ Peggy tol’ ’im she’d wuk ’er roots, en fer ’im ter come back de nex’ night, en she’d tell ’im w’at she c’d do fer ’im.

“So de nex’ night Jeff went back, en Aun’ Peggy gun ’im a baby doll, wid a body made out’n a piece er co’n-stalk, en wid splinters fer a’ms en laigs, en a head made out’n elderberry peth, en two little red peppers fer feet.

===Chinese===
「于是杰夫一天夜里溜到佩姬大娘家，送上带来的礼物，把他同克洛伊、汉尼拔之间的事一五一十讲了，求她帮这个忙。佩姬大娘说她要念咒作法一番，叫他第二天夜里再来，她再告诉他能帮到哪一步。

「第二天夜里杰夫去了，佩姬大娘给了他一个娃娃：身子是一截玉米秆做的，胳膊腿是细木条，脑袋是接骨木的木芯，两只脚是两个小红辣椒。

===Original===
“ ‘Dis yer baby doll,’ sez she, ‘is Hannibal. Dis yer peth head is Hannibal’s head, en dese yer pepper feet is Hannibal’s feet. You take dis en hide it unner de house, on de sill unner de do’, whar Hannibal’ll hafter walk ober it eve’y day. En ez long ez Hannibal comes anywhar nigh dis baby doll, he’ll be des lack it is﻿—light-headed en hot-footed; en ef dem two things doan git ’im inter trouble mighty soon, den I’m no cunjuh ’oman. But w’en you git Hannibal out’n de house, en git all th’oo wid dis baby doll, you mus’ fetch it back ter me, fer it’s monst’us powerful goopher, en is liable ter make mo’ trouble ef you leabe it layin’ roun’.’

===Chinese===
