「『这个娃娃，』她说，『就是汉尼拔。这木芯脑袋是汉尼拔的脑袋，这两个辣椒脚是汉尼拔的脚。你把它拿去，藏到大宅底下，就搁在前门的门槛底下，叫汉尼拔天天都得打它上头跨过去。只要汉尼拔挨近这娃娃，他就会跟它一个样——脑袋发飘，脚底发烫；这两样要不快快给他招来祸事，俺就不配当巫女。不过等你们把汉尼拔撵出了大宅，用完了这娃娃，务必把它给俺送回来，因为这是一道厉害无比的咒（goopher），随手乱搁是要惹出更多乱子的。』

===Original===
“Well, Jeff tuk de baby doll, en slip’ up ter de big house, en whistle’ ter Chloe, en w’en she come out he tol’ ’er w’at ole Aun’ Peggy had said. En Chloe showed ’im how ter git unner de house, en w’en he had put de cunjuh doll on de sill, he went ’long back ter de qua’ters﻿—en des waited.

“Nex’ day, sho’ ’nuff, de goopher ’mence’ ter wuk. Hannibal sta’ted in de house soon in de mawnin’ wid a armful er wood ter make a fire, en he hadn’ mo’ d’n got ’cross de do’-sill befo’ his feet begun ter bu’n so dat he drap’ de armful er wood on de flo’ en woke ole mis’ up a’ hour sooner ’n yushal, en co’se ole mis’ didn’ lack dat, en spoke sha’p erbout it.

===Chinese===
「杰夫收了娃娃，溜到大宅跟前，打个呼哨唤克洛伊；她一出来，他就把佩姬大娘的话一五一十学了。克洛伊指点他怎么钻到宅子底下；他把那施过咒的娃娃搁上门槛，便径自回住区去了——单等着瞧。

「第二天，果然，咒发作起来。一大清早，汉尼拔抱着一抱柴火进宅子生火，刚跨过门槛，两只脚就烧得他受不住，一抱柴火全撂在了地板上，把老夫人提前一个钟头吵醒了。老夫人自然不痛快，疾言厉色数落了他几句。

===Original===
“W’en dinner-time come, en Hannibal wuz help’n’ de cook kyar de dinner f’m de kitchen inter de big house, en wuz gittin’ close ter de do’ whar he had ter go in, his feet sta’ted ter bu’n en his head begun ter swim, en he let de big dish er chicken en dumplin’s fall right down in de dirt, in de middle er de ya’d, en de w’ite folks had ter make dey dinner dat day off’n col’ ham en sweet’n’ ’taters.

“De nex’ mawnin’ he overslep’ hisse’f, en got inter mo’ trouble. Atter breakfus’, Mars’ Dugal’ sont ’im ober ter Mars’ Marrabo Utley’s fer ter borry a monkey wrench. He oughter be’n back in ha’f a’ hour, but he come pokin’ home ’bout dinner-time wid a screw-driver stidder a monkey wrench. Mars’ Dugal’ sont ernudder nigger back wid de screw-driver, en Hannibal didn’ git no dinner. ’Long in de atternoon, ole mis’ sot Hannibal ter weedin’ de flowers in de front gya’den, en Hannibal dug up all de bulbs ole mis’ had sont erway fer, en paid a lot er money fer, en tuk ’em down ter de hawg-pen by de ba’nya’d, en fed ’em ter de hawgs. W’en ole mis’ come out in de cool er de ebenin’, en seed w’at Hannibal had done, she wuz mos’ crazy, en she wrote a note en sont Hannibal down ter de oberseah wid it.

===Chinese===
「到了正餐时分，汉尼拔帮着厨娘把饭菜从厨房往大宅里端，快到进门的当口，他的脚烧起来，脑袋发起晕，一大盆鸡肉面疙瘩当场摔进了院子当中的泥地里；白人们那天的正餐，只好拿冷火腿和红薯对付。

「第二天一早他又睡过了头，又惹了场没趣。早饭过后，杜加尔老爷打发他上马拉博·奥特利（Marrabo Utley）老爷家去借一把活动扳手。本该半个钟头就回来的，他磨磨蹭蹭挨到正餐时分才进家门，手里拿的却是一把螺丝刀。杜加尔老爷另派了个黑人把螺丝刀送回去，汉尼拔的正餐也落了空。午后，老夫人派汉尼拔在前院花坛里薅草，汉尼拔倒好，把老夫人特地函购、花了大价钱买来的球根一古脑儿刨了出来，搬到谷场边的猪圈里喂了猪。傍晚凉快下来，老夫人出来一见汉尼拔干的好事，气得几乎发了疯，当下写了张字条，叫汉尼拔给监工送去。

===Original===
“But w’at Hannibal got fum de oberseah didn’ ’pear ter do no good. Eve’y now en den ’is feet’d ’mence ter torment ’im, en ’is min’ ’u’d git all mix’ up, en his conduc’ kep’ gittin’ wusser en wusser, ’tel fin’lly de w’ite folks couldn’ stan’ it no longer, en Mars’ Dugal’ tuk Hannibal back down ter de qua’ters.

“ ‘Mr. Smif,’ sez Mars’ Dugal’ ter de oberseah, ‘dis yer nigger has done got so triflin’ yer lately dat we can’t keep ’im at de house no mo’, en I’s fotch’ ’im ter you ter be straighten’ up. You’s had ’casion ter deal wid ’im once, so he knows w’at ter expec’. You des take ’im in han’, en lemme know how he tu’ns out. En w’en de han’s comes in fum de fiel’ dis ebenin’ you kin sen’ dat yaller nigger Jeff up ter de house. I’ll try ’im, en see ef he’s any better ’n Hannibal.’

===Chinese===
「可汉尼拔从监工那儿领的那顿教训，似乎没起什么效用。隔三差五，他的两只脚就作起怪来，脑子也搅成一锅粥，行止一天坏似一天，到末了白人们再也忍不下去，杜加尔老爷把汉尼拔打发回了住区。

「『史密斯（Smif）先生，』杜加尔老爷对监工说，『这个黑鬼近来懒散得没了样子，宅子里再留他不得，我把他交到你手上，给我把他扳过来。你从前也教训过他一回，他知道等着他的是什么。你只管放手管教，管出个什么样子，告诉我一声。今儿傍晚地里的人下工回来，你打发那个黄皮黑鬼杰夫上大宅来。我拿他试试，看比汉尼拔强不强。』

===Original===
“So Jeff went up ter de big house, en pleas’ Mars’ Dugal’ en ole mis’ en de res’ er de fambly so well dat dey all got ter lackin’ ’im fus’rate; en dey’d ’a’ fergot all ’bout Hannibal, ef it hadn’ be’n fer de bad repo’ts w’at come up fum de qua’ters ’bout ’im fer a mont’ er so. Fac’ is, dat Chloe en Jeff wuz so int’rusted in one ernudder sence Jeff be’n up ter de house, dat dey fergot all ’bout takin’ de baby doll back ter Aun’ Peggy, en it kep’ wukkin’ fer a w’ile, en makin’ Hannibal’s feet bu’n mo’ er less, ’tel all de folks on de plantation got ter callin’ ’im Hot-Foot Hannibal. He kep’ gittin’ mo’ en mo’ triflin’, ’tel he got de name er bein’ de mos’ no ’countes’ nigger on de plantation, en Mars’ Dugal’ had ter th’eaten ter sell ’im in de spring, w’en bimeby de goopher quit wukkin’, en Hannibal ’mence’ ter pick up some en make folks set a little mo’ sto’ by ’im.

===Chinese===
「杰夫上了大宅，把杜加尔老爷、老夫人合家上下伺候得妥妥帖帖，一家人都头等喜欢他。若不是随后个把月光景，住区那边不断把汉尼拔的坏话传上来，他们早把他忘到脑后去了。实情是，杰夫上了大宅之后，同克洛伊两个情投意合，把给佩姬大娘送还娃娃的事忘了个干净。娃娃又使了一阵子劲，烧得汉尼拔的两只脚或轻或重不得消停，到后来，全庄园的人都管他叫热脚汉尼拔了。他越发懒散下去，落了个全庄园最不中用的黑人的名声，杜加尔老爷只得吓唬说，开春要把他卖掉；不料这时咒渐渐失了效，汉尼拔缓过一些劲来，人们对他的看法也稍稍抬高了一分。

===Original===
“Now, dis yer Hannibal was a monst’us sma’t nigger, en w’en he got rid er dem so’ feet, his min’ kep’ runnin’ on ’is udder troubles. Heah th’ee er fo’ weeks befo’ he’d had a’ easy job, waitin’ on de w’ite folks, libbin’ off’n de fat er de lan’, en promus’ de fines’ gal on de plantation fer a wife in de spring, en now heah he wuz back in de co’n-fiel’, wid de oberseah a-cussin’ en a-r’arin’ ef he didn’ get a ha’d tas’ done; wid nuffin but co’n bread en bacon en merlasses ter eat; en all de fiel’-han’s makin’ rema’ks, en pokin’ fun at ’im ’ca’se he’d be’n sont back fum de big house ter de fiel’. En de mo’ Hannibal studied ’bout it de mo’ madder he got, ’tel he fin’lly swo’ he wuz gwine ter git eben wid Jeff en Chloe, ef it wuz de las’ ac’.

===Chinese===
「说起来，这汉尼拔是个精明透顶的黑人，脚上的痛一摆脱，心思就一个劲儿转到别的委屈上。想想三四个礼拜之前，他还干着轻省差事，伺候白人，吃着肥甘，开春还订下全庄园最出挑的姑娘做媳妇；如今呢，又回到玉米地里，硬活计赶不出来，监工就骂骂咧咧、暴跳如雷；吃的只剩玉米饼、咸肉加糖蜜；地里的人还个个拿话点他、戳他脊梁骨，笑他被大宅撵回了地里。汉尼拔越琢磨越恼，到末了发起誓来：哪怕这是他这辈子干的最后一件事，也要同杰夫和克洛伊把账算清。

===Original===
“So Hannibal slipped ’way fum de qua’ters one Sunday en hid in de co’n up close ter de big house, ’tel he see Chloe gwine down de road. He waylaid her, en sezee:—

“ ‘Hoddy, Chloe?’

“ ‘I ain’ got no time fer ter fool wid fiel’-han’s,’ sez Chloe, tossin’ her head; ’w’at you want wid me, Hot-Foot?’

“ ‘I wants ter know how you en Jeff is gittin’ ’long.’

“ ‘I ’lows dat’s none er yo’ bizness, nigger. I doan see w’at ’casion any common fiel’-han’ has got ter mix in wid de ’fairs er folks w’at libs in de big house. But ef it’ll do you any good ter know, I mought say dat me en Jeff is gittin’ ’long mighty well, en we gwine ter git married in de spring, en you ain’ gwine ter be ’vited ter de weddin’ nuther.’

===Chinese===
「于是一个礼拜天，汉尼拔从住区溜出来，藏在大宅近旁的玉米地里，瞅见克洛伊顺着大路走过来，便上去拦住了她，说道：——

「『好啊，克洛伊？』

「『俺可没工夫搭理地里干活的，』克洛伊一扬脑袋，说道；『找俺什么事，热脚？』

「『俺想知道，你跟杰夫处得怎么样了。』

「『俺看这不关你的事，黑小子。俺不明白，一个地里刨食的粗人，有什么由头掺和大宅里人的事体。不过，要是你听了能长些见识，俺不妨说给你：俺跟杰夫好得很，开春就要成亲；喜酒嘛，是不会请你的。』

===Original===
“ ‘No, no!’ sezee, ‘I wouldn’ ’spec’ ter be ’vited ter de weddin’﻿—a common, low-down fiel’-han’ lack *I* is. But I’s glad ter heah you en Jeff is gittin’ ’long so well. I didn’ knowed but w’at he had ’mence’ ter be a little ti’ed.’

“ ‘Ti’ed er me? Dat’s rediklus!’ sez Chloe. ‘W’y, dat nigger lubs me so I b’liebe he’d go th’oo fire en water fer me. Dat nigger is des wrop’ up in me.’

“ ‘Uh huh,’ sez Hannibal, ‘den I reckon it mus’ be some udder nigger w’at meets a ’oman down by de crick in de swamp eve’y Sunday ebenin’, ter say nuffin ’bout two er th’ee times a week.’

“ ‘Yas, hit is ernudder nigger, en you is a liah w’en you say it wuz Jeff.’

“ ‘Mebbe I is a liah, en mebbe I ain’ got good eyes. But ’less’n I *is* a liah, en ’less’n I *ain’t* got good eyes, Jeff is gwine ter meet dat ’oman dis ebenin’ ’long ’bout eight o’clock right down dere by de crick in de swamp ’bout half-way betwix’ dis plantation en Mars’ Marrabo Utley’s.’

===Chinese===
「『那可不，那可不！』他说，『像俺这样不入流的地里粗人，本来也不敢指望喜帖。不过，听说你跟杰夫处得这么好，俺真替你们高兴。俺还只当他有点儿腻味了呢。』

「『腻味俺？亏你说得出口！』克洛伊道。『嗐，那黑人爱俺爱得，俺看他火里水里都肯替俺蹚。那黑人呐，一门心思全拴在俺身上。』

「『哦嗬，』汉尼拔道，『那就一定是另一个黑人喽——每逢礼拜天傍晚，到沼泽里小溪边上去会女人的那个；一个礼拜还得另加两三回的那种。』

「『对，就是另一个黑人；可你要说那是杰夫，你就是个撒谎精。』

「『俺兴许是个撒谎精，兴许眼神也不济。可除非俺当真是个撒谎精，除非俺的眼神当真不济，今天傍晚八点来钟，杰夫就要到沼泽里那条小溪边上去会那个女人——就在这庄园同马拉博·奥特利老爷庄园的正中间那一段。』

===Original===
“Well, Chloe tol’ Hannibal she didn’ b’liebe a wo’d he said, en call’ ’im a low-down nigger, who wuz tryin’ ter slander Jeff ’ca’se he wuz mo’ luckier ’n he wuz. But all de same, she couldn’ keep her min’ fum runnin’ on w’at Hannibal had said. She ’membered she’d heared one er de niggers say dey wuz a gal ober at Mars’ Marrabo Utley’s plantation w’at Jeff use’ ter go wid some befo’ he got ’quainted wid Chloe. Den she ’mence’ ter figger back, en sho’ ’nuff, dey wuz two er th’ee times in de las’ week w’en she’d be’n he’pin’ de ladies wid dey dressin’ en udder fixin’s in de ebenin’, en Jeff mought ’a’ gone down ter de swamp widout her knowin’ ’bout it at all. En den she ’mence’ ter ’member little things w’at she hadn’ tuk no notice of befo’, en w’at ’u’d make it ’pear lack Jeff had sump’n on his min’.

===Chinese===
「克洛伊当面告诉汉尼拔，他的话她一个字也不信，还骂他是个下作的黑小子，见杰夫比自己走运，存心糟践他。可话虽这么说，她那颗心却管束不住，一个劲儿往汉尼拔那几句话上想。她记起来，曾听哪个黑人说起，马拉博·奥特利老爷庄园上有个姑娘，杰夫同她相熟之前，有一阵子常往那边跑。于是她掐着指头往回盘算，可不是么：上个礼拜倒有两三回，傍晚她在伺候太太小姐们穿衣打扮、梳这个弄那个，杰夫全可能趁她不知道，溜到沼泽去了。接着她又记起一桩桩先前没留意的小事，桩桩都显得杰夫像是心里搁着事。

===Original===
“Chloe set a monst’us heap er sto’ by Jeff, en would ’a’ done mos’ anythin’ fer ’im, so long ez he stuck ter her. But Chloe wuz a mighty jealous ’oman, en w’iles she didn’ b’liebe w’at Hannibal said, she seed how it *could* ’a’ be’n so, en she ’termine’ fer ter fin’ out fer herse’f whuther it *wuz* so er no.

“Now, Chloe hadn’ seed Jeff all day, fer Mars’ Dugal’ had sont Jeff ober ter his daughter’s house, young Mis’ Ma’g’ret’s, w’at libbed ’bout fo’ miles fum Mars’ Dugal’s, en Jeff wuzn’ ’spected home ’tel ebenin’. But des atter supper wuz ober, en w’iles de ladies wuz settin’ out on de piazzer, Chloe slip’ off fum de house en run down de road﻿—dis yer same road we come; en w’en she got mos’ ter de crick﻿—dis yer same crick right befo’ us﻿—she kin’ er kep’ in de bushes at de side er de road, ’tel fin’lly she seed Jeff settin’ on de bank on de udder side er de crick﻿—right unner dat ole willer-tree droopin’ ober de water yander. En eve’y now en den he’d git up en look up de road to’ds Mars’ Marrabo’s on de udder side er de swamp.

===Chinese===
「克洛伊把杰夫看得比什么都重，只要他守着她，她几乎什么都肯为他做。可克洛伊又是个妒心极重的女人；汉尼拔的话她虽不信，却也看得出这事实有蹊跷的可能，便打定主意，要亲眼弄清到底有没有这么一回事。

「那天克洛伊一整天没见着杰夫——杜加尔老爷打发他上他女儿小玛格丽特（Margaret）太太家去了，那地方离杜加尔老爷家约莫四英里，杰夫要到傍晚才指望回来。可是晚饭刚过、太太们还坐在游廊上的当口，克洛伊就从大宅溜了出来，顺着大路一路跑下去——就是咱们来时走的这条路；等她跑到小溪跟前——就是咱们眼前这条溪——她就挨着路边的树丛猫了起来，到后来，果然望见杰夫坐在溪对岸的坡上——就在那边那棵枝条垂到水面上来的老柳树底下。他隔一阵就站起来，朝沼泽那头马拉博老爷家的方向，顺着大路张望。

===Original===
“Fus’ Chloe felt lack she’d go right ober de crick en gib Jeff a piece er her min’. Den she ’lowed she better be sho’ befo’ she done anythin’. So she helt herse’f in de bes’ she could, gittin’ madder en madder eve’y minute, ’tel bimeby she seed a ’oman comin’ down de road on de udder side fum to’ds Mars’ Marrabo Utley’s plantation. En w’en she seed Jeff jump up en run to’ds dat ’oman, en th’ow his a’ms roun’ her neck, po’ Chloe didn’ stop ter see no mo’, but des tu’nt roun’ en run up ter de house, en rush’ up on de piazzer, en up en tol’ Mars’ Dugal’ en ole mis’ all ’bout de baby doll, en all ’bout Jeff gittin’ de goopher fum Aun’ Peggy, en ’bout w’at de goopher had done ter Hannibal.

===Chinese===
「起先，克洛伊恨不得当场蹚过溪去，痛痛快快把杰夫数落一顿。转念又想，动手之前总得先拿准。于是她死死按住性子，一分钟比一分钟火大，后来果然望见一个女人顺着对岸的大路走来，正是从马拉博·奥特利老爷庄园那个方向来的。她眼看着杰夫一跃而起，朝那女人奔过去，一把搂住她的脖子——可怜的克洛伊再没心肠看下去，转身就往大宅跑，冲上游廊，一股脑儿把娃娃的事全抖了出来：杰夫怎么从佩姬大娘那里求来咒，这咒又怎么作弄了汉尼拔，一五一十，全告诉了杜加尔老爷和老夫人。

===Original===
“Mars’ Dugal’ wuz monst’us mad. He didn’ let on at fus’ lack he b’liebed Chloe, but w’en she tuk en showed ’im whar ter fin’ de baby doll, Mars’ Dugal’ tu’nt w’ite ez chalk.

“ ‘Wat debil’s wuk is dis?’ sezee. ‘No wonder de po’ nigger’s feet eetched. Sump’n got ter be done ter l’arn dat ole witch ter keep her han’s off’n my niggers. En ez fer dis yer Jeff, I’m gwine ter do des w’at I promus’, so de darkies on dis plantation’ll know I means w’at I sez.’

===Chinese===
「杜加尔老爷气得不行。起初他还装出不相信克洛伊的样子；可等她领着他，指给他到哪儿去找那个娃娃，杜加尔老爷的脸唰地白了，白得像粉笔。

「『这是造的什么孽？』他说。『怪不得这可怜的黑鬼脚发烧发痒。总得想个法子，教训教训那个老巫婆，叫她再不敢碰我的黑鬼。至于这个杰夫，我说过的话就要照办，好叫这庄园上的黑奴都知道，我说话是算数的。』

===Original===
“Fer Mars’ Dugal’ had warned de han’s befo’ ’bout foolin’ wid cunju’ation; fac’, he had los’ one er two niggers hisse’f fum dey bein’ goophered, en he would ’a’ had ole Aun’ Peggy whip’ long ago, on’y Aun’ Peggy wuz a free ’oman, en he wuz ’feard she’d cunjuh him. En w’iles Mars’ Dugal’ say he didn’ b’liebe in cunj’in’ en sich, he ’peared ter ’low it wuz bes’ ter be on de safe side, en let Aun’ Peggy alone.

“So Mars’ Dugal’ done des ez he say. Ef ole mis’ had ple’d fer Jeff, he mought ’a’ kep’ ’im. But ole mis’ hadn’ got ober losin’ dem bulbs yit, en she neber said a wo’d. Mars’ Dugal’ tuk Jeff ter town nex’ day en’ sol’ ’im ter a spekilater, who sta’ted down de ribber wid ’im nex’ mawnin’ on a steamboat, fer ter take ’im ter Alabama.

===Chinese===
「原来杜加尔老爷早警告过庄里的人，不许招惹巫咒这一路事；实情是，他自己就折过一两个黑人，都是着了咒的。他老早就想叫人鞭佩姬大娘一顿，只是佩姬大娘是个自由人，他又怕她把咒下到自己头上。杜加尔老爷嘴上说不信施咒这一套，可瞧那意思，他觉得还是小心为上，不去招惹佩姬大娘为妙。

「于是杜加尔老爷果然说到做到。那会儿老夫人若肯替杰夫求个情，兴许还留得下他。可老夫人还没消了球根的气，一个字也没说。第二天，杜加尔老爷把杰夫带进城，卖给一个人贩子（spekilater）；人贩子第二天一早押着他搭上轮船顺河而下，要把他带往阿拉巴马（Alabama）。

===Original===
“Now, w’en Chloe tol’ ole Mars’ Dugal’ ’bout dis yer baby doll en dis udder goopher, she hadn’ ha’dly ’lowed Mars’ Dugal’ would sell Jeff down Souf. Howsomeber, she wuz so mad wid Jeff dat she ’suaded herse’f she didn’ keer; en so she hilt her head up en went roun’ lookin’ lack she wuz rale glad ’bout it. But one day she wuz walkin’ down de road, w’en who sh’d come ’long but dis yer Hannibal.

“W’en Hannibal seed ’er, he bus’ out laffin’ fittin’ fer ter kill: ‘Yah, yah, yah! ho, ho, ho! ha, ha, ha! Oh, hol’ me, honey, hol’ me, er I’ll laf myse’f ter def. I ain’ nebber laf’ so much sence I be’n bawn.’

===Chinese===
「克洛伊向老杜加尔老爷告发这娃娃、这道咒的时候，可没料到杜加尔老爷会把杰夫卖到南方去。不过她正在气头上，硬是劝服自己说毫不在乎；于是她昂着头，四处走动，一副当真乐见其成的样子。可是有一天，她正顺着大路走，迎面来的不是别人，偏偏是这个汉尼拔。

「汉尼拔一见她，顿时放声大笑，笑得几乎背过气去：『呀，呀，呀！嗬，嗬，嗬！哈，哈，哈！哎哟，扶住俺，老天爷，快扶住俺，不然俺要笑死过去了。俺打娘胎里出来，就没这么笑过。』

===Original===
“ ‘Wat you laffin’ at, Hot-Foot?’

“ ‘Yah, yah, yah! Wat I laffin’ at? W’y, I’s laffin’ at myse’f, tooby sho’﻿—laffin’ ter think w’at a fine ’oman I made.’

===Chinese===
「『笑什么呢，热脚？』

「『呀，呀，呀！俺笑什么？嗐，俺笑的是俺自个儿，一点不假——笑俺扮成的那副女人模样，可真够体面的。』

===Original===
“Chloe tu’nt pale, en her hea’t come up in her mouf.

“ ‘Wat you mean, nigger?’ sez she, ketchin’ holt er a bush by de road fer ter stiddy herse’f. ‘Wat you mean by de kin’ er ’oman you made?’

“ ‘Wat do I mean? I means dat I got squared up wid you fer treatin’ me de way you done, en I got eben wid dat yaller nigger Jeff fer cuttin’ me out. Now, he’s gwine ter know w’at it is ter eat co’n bread en merlasses once mo’, en wuk fum daylight ter da’k, en ter hab a oberseah dribin’ ’im fum one day’s een’ ter de udder. I means dat I sont wo’d ter Jeff dat Sunday dat you wuz gwine ter be ober ter Mars’ Marrabo’s visitin’ dat ebenin’, en you want ’im ter meet you down by de crick on de way home en go de rest er de road wid you. En den I put on a frock en a sunbonnet, en fix’ myse’f up ter look lack a ’oman; en w’en Jeff seed me comin’, he run ter meet me, en you seed ’im﻿—fer I’d be’n watchin’ in de bushes befo’ en ’skivered you comin’ down de road. En now I reckon you en Jeff bofe knows w’at it means ter mess wid a nigger lack me.’

===Chinese===
「克洛伊脸色煞白，心口一下子提到了嗓子眼。

「『你这话什么意思，黑小子？』她说着，一把抓住路边的灌木，好稳住身子。『你把你做成的那个女人，究竟是什么意思？』

「『什么意思？意思是：你那样作践俺，俺跟你两清了；那个黄皮小子杰夫横刀夺爱，俺也跟他算清了账。往后他就该知道玉米饼就糖蜜是什么滋味了，就要天亮干到天黑，让监工从一天撵到下一天了。意思是：那个礼拜天，俺给杰夫捎了话，就说你那天傍晚要上马拉博老爷家做客，叫他回家路上到小溪边上等你，陪你把剩下的路走完。然后俺呢，穿上长裙，戴上遮阳帽，把自己拾掇成一个女人的模样；杰夫一见俺走来，就跑上来迎俺——这一幕你亲眼瞧见了，因为俺先前就猫在树丛里，早瞅见你顺着大路下来了。这下俺寻思，你和杰夫两个该都知道了：跟俺这样的黑人过不去，是个什么下场。』

===Original===
“Po’ Chloe hadn’ heared mo’ d’n half er de las’ part er w’at Hannibal said, but she had heared ’nuff to l’arn dat dis nigger had fooled her en Jeff, en dat po’ Jeff hadn’ done nuffin, en dat fer lovin’ her too much en goin’ ter meet her she had cause’ ’im ter be sol’ erway whar she’d neber, neber see ’im no mo’. De sun mought shine by day, de moon by night, de flowers mought bloom, en de mawkin’-birds mought sing, but po’ Jeff wuz done los’ ter her fereber en fereber.

===Chinese===
「汉尼拔后半截话，可怜的克洛伊听全的不到一半；可她听到的已经尽够她明白：是这个黑人耍了她和杰夫；可怜的杰夫什么也没做错；只因为他爱她爱得太深、赶去会她，她却害得他被卖到他乡，从此永远、永远再见不着了。太阳照旧可以白天升，月亮照旧可以夜里亮，花儿照旧可以开，反舌鸟照旧可以唱——可可怜的杰夫，是永远永远地失去，再也回不来了。

===Original===
“Hannibal hadn’ mo’ d’n finish’ w’at he had ter say, w’en Chloe’s knees gun ’way unner her, en she fell down in de road, en lay dere half a’ hour er so befo’ she come to. W’en she did, she crep’ up ter de house des ez pale ez a ghos’. En fer a mont’ er so she crawled roun’ de house, en ’peared ter be so po’ly dat Mars’ Dugal’ sont fer a doctor; en de doctor kep’ on axin’ her questions ’tel he foun’ she wuz des pinin’ erway fer Jeff.

“W’en he tol’ Mars’ Dugal’, Mars’ Dugal’ lafft, en said he’d fix dat. She could hab de noo house boy fer a husban’. But ole mis’ say, no, Chloe ain’ dat kin’er gal, en dat Mars’ Dugal’ sh’d buy Jeff back.

===Chinese===
「汉尼拔话音才落，克洛伊双膝一软，瘫倒在大路上，躺了约莫半个钟头才缓醒过来。醒转之后，她一路爬回大宅，脸白得像个鬼。此后个把月，她在宅子里拖着步子挨日子，瞧那光景病得不轻，杜加尔老爷请来医生；医生问呀问呀，问到后来，才问出她只是为杰夫一日一日枯萎下去。

「医生告诉杜加尔老爷，老爷哈哈一笑，说这个好办：新来的宅童正好给她做丈夫。可老夫人说，不行，克洛伊不是那路姑娘，杜加尔老爷应当把杰夫赎回来。

===Original===
“So Mars’ Dugal’ writ a letter ter dis yer spekilater down ter Wim’l’ton, en tol’ ef he ain’ done sol’ dat nigger Souf w’at he bought fum ’im, he’d lack ter buy ’im back ag’in. Chloe ’mence’ ter pick up a little w’en ole mis’ tol’ her ’bout dis letter. Howsomeber, bimeby Mars’ Dugal’ got a’ answer fum de spekilater, who said he wuz monst’us sorry, but Jeff had fell ove’boa’d er jumped off’n de steamboat on de way ter Wim’l’ton, en got drownded, en co’se he couldn’ sell ’im back, much ez he’d lack ter ’bleedge Mars’ Dugal’.

“Well, atter Chloe heared dis, she wa’n’t much mo’ use ter nobody. She pu’tended ter do her wuk, en ole mis’ put up wid her, en had de doctor gib her medicine, en let ’er go ter de circus, en all so’ts er things fer ter take her min’ off’n her troubles. But dey didn’ none un ’em do no good. Chloe got ter slippin’ down here in de ebenin’ des lack she ’uz comin’ ter meet Jeff, en she’d set dere unner dat willer-tree on de udder side, en wait fer ’im, night atter night. Bimeby she got so bad de w’ite folks sont her ober ter young Mis’ Ma’g’ret’s fer ter gib her a change; but she runned erway de fus’ night, en w’en dey looked fer ’er nex’ mawnin’, dey foun’ her co’pse layin’ in de branch yander, right ’cross fum whar we’re settin’ now.

===Chinese===
「于是杜加尔老爷给威明顿的那个人贩子写了一封信，说要是他还没把从自己手里买走的那个黑人卖到南方，他想把他再买回来。老夫人把写信的事告诉克洛伊，克洛伊才稍稍振作了一点。可是过了些日子，杜加尔老爷收到人贩子的回信：万分抱歉，杰夫在来威明顿的半路上，从轮船上失足落了水——要不就是自己跳了下去——淹死了；他自然没法把他再卖回去，虽然他万分愿意为杜加尔老爷效这个劳。

「唉，克洛伊听了这个信儿之后，人就再没多大用处了。她勉勉强强装着做事，老夫人也容让她，叫医生给她下药，放她去看马戏，变着法儿解她的心事。可一样也不顶用。克洛伊到了傍晚，就往这边溜，像当年来会杰夫那样，坐到溪那边的柳树底下去等他，一夜连着一夜。后来她病得实在不成样子，白人们送她过小玛格丽特太太家去，给她换换环境；头一天夜里她就跑了。第二天早上他们去找，发现她的尸身躺在那边溪水里，正对着咱们这会儿坐的地方。

===Original===
“Eber sence den,” said Julius in conclusion, “Chloe’s ha’nt comes eve’y ebenin’ en sets down unner dat willer-tree en waits fer Jeff, er e’se walks up en down de road yander, lookin’ en lookin’, en waitin’ en waitin’, fer her sweethea’t w’at ain’ neber, neber come back ter her no mo’.”

===Chinese===
「打那以后，」朱利叶斯收了尾，「克洛伊的鬼魂每天傍晚都来，在那棵柳树底下坐下来，等杰夫；要不就在那边大路上走来走去，望啊，望啊，等啊，等啊，等她那再也、再也不会回来的心上人。」

===Original===
There was silence when the old man had finished, and I am sure I saw a tear in my wife’s eye, and more than one in Mabel’s.

“I think, Julius,” said my wife, after a moment, “that you may turn the mare around and go by the long road.”

The old man obeyed with alacrity, and I noticed no reluctance on the mare’s part.

===Chinese===
老人讲完，四下一片寂静；我敢担保，我看见妻子眼里噙着一颗泪珠，梅贝尔眼里的更不止一颗。

「我看这样吧，朱利叶斯，」妻子过了一会儿说，「你把马掉个头，走远路吧。」

老人欣然从命；我也留意到，母马那边没有半分勉强。

===Original===
“You are not afraid of Chloe’s haunt, are you?” I asked jocularly.

My mood was not responded to, and neither of the ladies smiled.

“Oh, no,” said Annie, “but I’ve changed my mind. I prefer the other route.”

===Chinese===
「你们不是怕了克洛伊的鬼魂吧，嗯？」我打趣道。

我这股兴致没人接应，两位女士谁也没笑。

「哦，不是，」安妮说，「只是我改了主意，还是走另一条路的好。」

===Original===
When we had reached the main road and had proceeded along it for a short distance, we met a cart driven by a young negro, and on the cart were a trunk and a valise. We recognized the man as Malcolm Murchison’s servant, and drew up a moment to speak to him.

“Who’s going away, Marshall?” I inquired.

“Young Mistah Ma’colm gwine ’way on de boat ter Noo Yo’k dis ebenin’, suh, en I’m takin’ his things down ter de wharf, suh.”

This was news to me, and I heard it with regret. My wife looked sorry, too, and I could see that Mabel was trying hard to hide her concern.

“He’s comin’ ’long behin’, suh, en I ’spec’s you’ll meet ’im up de road a piece. He’s gwine ter walk down ez fur ez Mistah Jim Williams’s, en take de buggy fum dere ter town. He ’spec’s ter be gone a long time, suh, en say prob’ly he ain’ neber comin’ back.”

The man drove on. There were a few words exchanged in an undertone between my wife and Mabel, which I did not catch. Then Annie said: “Julius, you may stop the rockaway a moment. There are some trumpet-flowers by the road there that I want. Will you get them for me, John?”

===Chinese===
我们上了大道，前行没多远，迎面遇上一辆大车，赶车的是个年轻黑人，车上一口衣箱、一只旅行提包。我们认出那人是马尔科姆·默奇森的仆人，便停车同他说了几句话。

「谁要出远门，马歇尔（Marshall）？」我问。

「小马尔科姆（Ma’colm）先生今儿傍晚搭船去纽约，先生；俺正把他的行李送到码头去呢，先生。」

这消息我头一回听说，听着心里很惋惜。我妻子也面带戚容；我还看得出，梅贝尔在竭力掩住她的不安。

「他本人随后就到，先生，俺寻思你们往前走一截就能碰上他。他打算先走到吉姆·威廉姆斯（Jim Williams）先生家，再从那儿换轻便马车进城。他说这一去要好久呢，先生，还说八成永远不回来了。」

大车赶过去了。我妻子和梅贝尔低声换了几句话，我没听清。随后安妮说：「朱利叶斯，把车停一停。路边那儿有几朵凌霄花（trumpet-flower），我想要。你替我下去采一采好吗，约翰？」

===Original===
I sprang into the underbrush, and soon returned with a great bunch of scarlet blossoms.

“Where is Mabel?” I asked, noting her absence.

“She has walked on ahead. We shall overtake her in a few minutes.”

The carriage had gone only a short distance when my wife discovered that she had dropped her fan.

“I had it where we were stopping. Julius, will you go back and get it for me?”

Julius got down and went back for the fan. He was an unconscionably long time finding it. After we got started again we had gone only a little way, when we saw Mabel and young Murchison coming toward us. They were walking arm in arm, and their faces were aglow with the light of love.

===Chinese===
我跳进路边的灌木丛，不多时捧着一大束猩红的花回来了。

「梅贝尔呢？」我发觉她不在，问道。

「她往前头先走着呢。咱们一会儿就能赶上她。」

马车才行了没多远，我妻子发觉扇子丢了。

「就落在方才停车的地方。朱利叶斯，你回去替我捡一捡好吗？」

朱利叶斯下车回去找扇子，找了老半天，久得有点没道理。等我们重新上路，没走多远，就望见梅贝尔和年轻的默奇森朝我们走来。两人挽着胳膊，一张张脸上都焕发着爱的光彩。

---

===Original===
I do not know whether or not Julius had a previous understanding with Malcolm Murchison by which he was to drive us round by the long road that day, nor do I know exactly what motive influenced the old man’s exertions in the matter. He was fond of Mabel, but I was old enough, and knew Julius well enough, to be skeptical of his motives. It is certain that a most excellent understanding existed between him and Murchison after the reconciliation, and that when the young people set up housekeeping over at the old Murchison place, Julius had an opportunity to enter their service. For some reason or other, however, he preferred to remain with us. The mare, I might add, was never known to balk again.

===Chinese===
朱利叶斯是不是事先同马尔科姆·默奇森讲定了、那天特意拉我们绕远路，我不得而知；老人在这件事上这般卖力，究竟出于什么心思，我也不敢说摸得透。他是喜欢梅贝尔的；可我到底上了年纪，又深知朱利叶斯的为人，对他的动机总免不了将信将疑。可以肯定的是，两个年轻人和好之后，他同默奇森之间处得再融洽不过；小两口在老默奇森庄园那边成家立业的时候，朱利叶斯原有机会过去投到他们手下。然而不知出于什么缘故，他宁愿留在我们家。还有一句不妨附上：那匹母马，后来再没听说它闹过一次倔。

### Dave’s Neckliss / 戴夫的项链

===Original===
“Have some dinner, Uncle Julius?” said my wife.

It was a Sunday afternoon in early autumn. Our two women-servants had gone to a camp-meeting some miles away, and would not return until evening. My wife had served the dinner, and we were just rising from the table, when Julius came up the lane, and, taking off his hat, seated himself on the piazza.

===Chinese===
「吃点饭吧，朱利叶斯大叔？」我妻子说。

那是初秋一个礼拜天的下午。我家的两个女仆到几英里外赶野营布道会去了，要到傍晚才回来。饭是妻子盛摆的，我们刚从餐桌旁起身，朱利叶斯就顺着巷子走上来，摘下帽子，在游廊上坐了。

===Original===
The old man glanced through the open door at the dinner-table, and his eyes rested lovingly upon a large sugar-cured ham, from which several slices had been cut, exposing a rich pink expanse that would have appealed strongly to the appetite of any hungry Christian.

===Chinese===
老人透过敞开的门朝餐桌望了一眼，目光恋恋不舍地落在一只上好的糖腌火腿上——火腿上已经切下好几片，露出一大片丰润的粉红，任凭哪个饿着肚子的基督徒见了，都非食指大动不可。

===Original===
“Thanky, Miss Annie,” he said, after a momentary hesitation, “I dunno ez I keers ef I does tas’e a piece er dat ham, ef yer’ll cut me off a slice un it.”

“No,” said Annie, “I won’t. Just sit down to the table and help yourself; eat all you want, and don’t be bashful.”

Julius drew a chair up to the table, while my wife and I went out on the piazza. Julius was in my employment; he took his meals with his own family, but when he happened to be about our house at mealtimes, my wife never let him go away hungry.

===Chinese===
「多谢您呐，安妮太太，」他迟疑了片刻，说道，「您要是肯给俺切一片那火腿，俺想俺也不妨尝上一尝。」

「不，」安妮说，「我不切。你自己坐到桌边去，自己动手；想吃多少吃多少，别不好意思。」

朱利叶斯把一把椅子拖到桌前，我和妻子便出到游廊上。朱利叶斯是我雇下的仆役，平日在自家吃饭；可碰巧赶上饭点待在我们家，我妻子从不让他饿着肚子走。

===Original===
I threw myself into a hammock, from which I could see Julius through an open window. He ate with evident relish, devoting his attention chiefly to the ham, slice after slice of which disappeared in the spacious cavity of his mouth. At first the old man ate rapidly, but after the edge of his appetite had been taken off he proceeded in a more leisurely manner. When he had cut the sixth slice of ham (I kept count of them from a lazy curiosity to see how much he *could* eat) I saw him lay it on his plate; as he adjusted the knife and fork to cut it into smaller pieces, he paused, as if struck by a sudden thought, and a tear rolled down his rugged cheek and fell upon the slice of ham before him. But the emotion, whatever the thought that caused it, was transitory, and in a moment he continued his dinner. When he was through eating, he came out on the porch, and resumed his seat with the satisfied expression of countenance that usually follows a good dinner.

===Chinese===
我一仰身躺进吊床，从那儿隔着敞开的窗子正好望得见朱利叶斯。他吃得津津有味，心思大半在那只火腿上，火腿一片接一片，都消失进他那张宽阔的口腔里。起初老人吃得飞快，等饿劲儿过去，便从容了下来。到他切下第六片火腿时（我怀着偷懒的好奇，一片片数着，想看看他到底*能*吃多少），我见他把它搁进盘子；他正摆好刀叉要把它切成小块，忽然顿住了，像是被一个念头击中，一滴泪顺着粗粝的脸颊滚下来，落在面前那片火腿上。不过那阵激动——不管引起它的是什么念头——转瞬即逝，片刻之后他又接着吃了起来。吃完饭，他踱到廊前来，重新坐下，脸上带着一顿好饭之后惯有的那种餍足的神情。

===Original===
“Julius,” I said, “you seemed to be affected by something, a moment ago. Was the mustard so strong that it moved you to tears?”

“No, suh, it wa’n’t de mustard; I wuz studyin’ ’bout Dave.”

“Who was Dave, and what about him?” I asked.

===Chinese===
「朱利叶斯，」我说，「方才你像是被什么事触动了。是芥末太呛，把你呛出眼泪来了？」

「不是的，先生，不是芥末的事儿；俺是在想戴夫。」

「戴夫是谁？他怎么了？」我问。

===Original===
The conditions were all favorable to story-telling. There was an autumnal languor in the air, and a dreamy haze softened the dark green of the distant pines and the deep blue of the Southern sky. The generous meal he had made had put the old man in a very good humor. He was not always so, for his curiously undeveloped nature was subject to moods which were almost childish in their variableness. It was only now and then that we were able to study, through the medium of his recollection, the simple but intensely human inner life of slavery. His way of looking at the past seemed very strange to us; his view of certain sides of life was essentially different from ours. He never indulged in any regrets for the Arcadian joyousness and irresponsibility which was a somewhat popular conception of slavery; his had not been the lot of the petted house-servant, but that of the toiling field-hand. While he mentioned with a warm appreciation the acts of kindness which those in authority had shown to him and his people, he would speak of a cruel deed, not with the indignation of one accustomed to quick feeling and spontaneous expression, but with a furtive disapproval which suggested to us a doubt in his own mind as to whether he had a right to think or to feel, and presented to us the curious psychological spectacle of a mind enslaved long after the shackles had been struck off from the limbs of its possessor. Whether the sacred name of liberty ever set his soul aglow with a generous fire; whether he had more than the most elementary ideas of love, friendship, patriotism, religion﻿—things which are half, and the better half, of life to us; whether he even realized, except in a vague, uncertain way, his own degradation, I do not know. I fear not; and if not, then centuries of repression had borne their legitimate fruit. But in the simple human feeling, and still more in the undertone of sadness, which pervaded his stories, I thought I could see a spark which, fanned by favoring breezes and fed by the memories of the past, might become in his children’s children a glowing flame of sensibility, alive to every thrill of human happiness or human woe.

===Chinese===
讲故事的条件再合适不过了。空气里弥漫着秋日的倦怠，一层如梦的薄霭，把远处松林的深翠和南方天空的湛蓝都柔化了。这顿丰盛的饭菜让老人心情极好。他并不是一向如此，因为他那奇怪地未经充分发育的天性，容易起一种近乎孩子气的、说变就变的情绪。我们也只是偶尔才能借着他的回忆这一媒介，窥见奴隶制度下那种单纯却极富人情味的内在生活。他看待过去的那副眼光，在我们看来十分陌生；他对生活某些侧面的看法，同我们的有着本质的不同。他从不曾为那种田园牧歌式的欢乐与无忧无虑——一种流传颇广的关于奴隶制的想象——发出过任何惋惜之叹；他从来不是受宠家仆的命，而是苦劳田间汉的命。他提起当权者施与他和他的族人的种种善意，总是怀着热切的感激；可是说起一桩残忍的行径，他的口气却不是感受迅疾、抒发直率的人所惯有的那种义愤，而是一种躲躲闪闪的不以为然——这使我们隐约觉得，他自己心里也拿不准，他究竟有没有权利去想、去感受——也让我们目睹了一幕奇怪的心理奇观：一副头脑，在镣铐早已从它的主人的肢体上砸落之后许久许久，依然被奴役着。那神圣的自由之名，可曾在他灵魂里燃起过一团宽厚的火？爱情、友谊、爱国、宗教——这些于我们即是生命的一半、而且是更好的那一半的东西——他除了最粗浅的概念之外，可曾有更深一层的领会？他自身所受的屈辱，除了朦朦胧胧、影影绰绰的觉察，他可曾真正意识到？这些我都不知道。我怕是没有；倘若没有，那么几百年的压制，也就结出了它名下应有的果实。但是，在他那些故事里弥漫的单纯的人的情味，尤其是在沉底的那一缕悲凉之中，我想我看见了一粒火星——有和风吹拂，有往昔的记忆添薪，到了他儿女的儿女那一辈，这火星或许会燃成一团炽烈的感性之火，对人间每一阵欢欣或悲苦的悸动，都报以鲜活的回应。

===Original===
“Dave use’ ter b’long ter my ole marster,” said Julius; “he wuz raise’ on dis yer plantation, en I kin ’member all erbout ’im, fer I wuz ole ’nuff ter chop cotton w’en it all happen’. Dave wuz a tall man, en monst’us strong: he could do mo’ wuk in a day dan any yuther two niggers on de plantation. He wuz one er dese yer solemn kine er men, en nebber run on wid much foolishness, like de yuther darkies. He use’ ter go out in de woods en pray; en w’en he hear de han’s on de plantation cussin’ en gwine on wid dere dancin’ en foolishness, he use’ ter tell ’em ’bout religion en jedgmen’-day, w’en dey would haf ter gin account fer eve’y idle word en all dey yuther sinful kyarin’s-on.

===Chinese===
「戴夫从前往在俺老主人家里，」朱利叶斯说，「他就是在这座庄园上生养大的，他的事俺桩桩件件都记得，因为事情发生的当儿，俺已经到了下地锄棉花的年纪。戴夫是个高个子，力气大得吓人：他一天干的活，比庄上随便哪两个黑人加起来还多。他是那种板正持重的男人，不像别的黑人那样疯跑胡闹。他常到树林子里去祷告；听见庄上的劳力骂脏话、又跳舞又胡闹的时候，他就跟他们讲宗教、讲审判日，讲到头来人人都得为自己说过的每一句闲话、干下的其他种种罪过一一交账。」

===Original===
“Dave had l’arn’ how ter read de Bible. Dey wuz a free nigger boy in de settlement w’at wuz monst’us smart, en could write en cipher, en wuz alluz readin’ books er papers. En Dave had hi’ed dis free boy fer ter l’arn ’im how ter read. Hit wuz ’g’in’ de law, but co’se none er de niggers didn’ say nuffin ter de w’ite folks ’bout it. Howsomedever, one day Mars Walker﻿—he wuz de oberseah﻿—foun’ out Dave could read. Mars Walker wa’n’t nuffin but a po’ bockrah, en folks said he couldn’ read ner write hisse’f, en co’se he didn’ lack ter see a nigger w’at knowed mo’ d’n he did; so he went en tole Mars Dugal’. Mars Dugal’ sont fer Dave, en ax’ ’im ’bout it.

===Chinese===
「戴夫学会过念《圣经》。这一带住着个自由黑人的小伙子，机灵得不得了，会写字会算数，成天书啊报纸啊不离手。戴夫就花钱雇了这自由小伙子教自己认字。这是犯法的，可黑人自然谁也不去跟白人提这茬。可是有一天，沃克老爷（Mars Walker）——他是个监工——发现戴夫认得字。这位沃克老爷不过是个穷白人（bockrah），人家说他自己连认字写字都不会，他自然见不得一个黑人比他知道得多；于是他跑去告诉了杜加尔老爷。杜加尔老爷派人叫戴夫来，问他是怎么回事。」

===Original===
“Dave didn’t hardly knowed w’at ter do; but he couldn’ tell no lie, so he ’fessed he could read de Bible a little by spellin’ out de words. Mars Dugal’ look’ mighty solemn.

“ ‘Dis yer is a se’ious matter,’ sezee; ‘it’s ’g’in’ de law ter l’arn niggers how ter read, er ’low ’em ter hab books. But w’at yer l’arn out’n dat Bible, Dave?’

===Chinese===
「戴夫一时简直不知怎么办才好；可他不会撒谎，便老老实实招认了，说自己能把《圣经》上的字一个一个拼出来、念上一点。杜加尔老爷面色好不凝重。

「『这是桩非同小可的事，』他说，『教黑人认字是犯法的，许他们有书也是犯法的。不过，你从那本《圣经》里学到了些什么呀，戴夫？』

===Original===
“Dave wa’n’t no fool, ef he wuz a nigger, en sezee:﻿—

“ ‘Marster, I l’arns dat it’s a sin fer ter steal, er ter lie, er fer ter want w’at doan b’long ter yer; en I l’arns fer ter love de Lawd en ter ’bey my marster.’

===Chinese===
「戴夫虽是个黑人，可不是傻子，便答道——

「『老爷，俺学到的是：偷东西是罪，撒谎是罪，贪想不属于自家的东西也是罪；俺还学到要爱上帝，要服从俺的主人。』

===Original===
“Mars Dugal’ sorter smile’ en laf ter hisse’f, like he ’uz might’ly tickle’ ’bout sump’n, en sezee:﻿—

“ ‘Doan ’pear ter me lack readin’ de Bible done yer much harm, Dave. Dat’s w’at I wants all my niggers fer ter know. Yer keep right on readin’, en tell de yuther han’s w’at yer be’n tellin’ me. How would yer lack fer ter preach ter de niggers on Sunday?’

===Chinese===
「杜加尔老爷不觉莞尔，独自暗笑，像是叫什么大大逗乐了，便说——

「『照我看，念《圣经》并没有叫你学坏，戴夫。我正要我这些黑人都懂得这个。你只管照旧念下去，把方才跟我讲的这些也讲给别的劳力听。礼拜天叫你给黑人们讲道，你看怎么样？』

===Original===
“Dave say he’d be glad fer ter do w’at he could. So Mars Dugal’ tole de oberseah fer ter let Dave preach ter de niggers, en tell ’em w’at wuz in de Bible, en it would he’p ter keep ’em fum stealin’ er runnin’ erway.

“So Dave ’mence’ ter preach, en done de han’s on de plantation a heap er good, en most un ’em lef’ off dey wicked ways, en ’mence’ ter love ter hear ’bout God, en religion, en de Bible; en dey done dey wuk better, en didn’ gib de oberseah but mighty little trouble fer ter manage ’em.

===Chinese===
「戴夫说，力所能及的事他都乐意。于是杜加尔老爷吩咐监工，准许戴夫给黑人讲道，把《圣经》里的话讲给他们听，说这样也能管住他们，不偷东西、不逃跑。

「就这样，戴夫讲起道来，对庄上的劳力们大有好处，他们大多丢开了恶习，渐渐爱听上帝啊、宗教啊、《圣经》啊这些道理；活也干得更像样了，几乎不用监工费什么心去管。」

===Original===
“Dave wuz one er dese yer men w’at didn’ keer much fer de gals﻿—leastways he didn’ ’tel Dilsey come ter de plantation. Dilsey wuz a monst’us peart, good-lookin’, gingybread-colored gal﻿—one er dese yer high-steppin’ gals w’at hol’s dey heads up, en won’ stan’ no foolishness fum no man. She had b’long’ ter a gemman over on Rockfish, w’at died, en whose ’state ha’ ter be sol’ fer ter pay his debts. En Mars Dugal’ had be’n ter de oction, en w’en he seed dis gal a-cryin’ en gwine on ’bout bein’ sol’ erway fum her ole mammy, Aun’ Mahaly, Mars Dugal’ bid ’em bofe in, en fotch ’em ober ter our plantation.

===Chinese===
「戴夫本是那种对姑娘们不上心的男人——至少在迪尔西来到庄园上之前是这样。迪尔西是个顶活泼、顶标致的姜饼色皮肤的姑娘——就是那种高昂着头、哪个男人也别想在她跟前造次的高傲姑娘。她从前往在罗克菲什那边一位绅士家，绅士死了，产业得变卖抵债。杜加尔老爷去参加了那场拍卖，见这姑娘哭哭啼啼，伤心要被卖走、离开她老娘马哈利大娘，便把娘儿俩一并买下，带回了我们庄园。」

===Original===
“De young nigger men on de plantation wuz des wil’ atter Dilsey, but it didn’ do no good, en none un ’em couldn’ git Dilsey fer dey junesey,2 ’tel Dave ’mence’ fer ter go roun’ Aun’ Mahaly’s cabin. Dey wuz a fine-lookin’ couple, Dave en Dilsey wuz, bofe tall, en well-shape’, en soopl’. En dey sot a heap by one ernudder. Mars Dugal’ seed ’em tergedder one Sunday, en de nex’ time he seed Dave atter dat, sezee:﻿—

===Chinese===
「庄上的年轻黑人个个为迪尔西疯魔，可全然无用，谁也没能把迪尔西讨去做自己的心上人（junesey）[2]，直到戴夫开始往马哈利大娘的小屋那边跑。戴夫和迪尔西真是好看的一对儿，两个都身量高挑，体态匀称，身段柔软，彼此又情深意重。一个礼拜天，杜加尔老爷瞧见他们俩在一处，后来再见到戴夫，便说——

===Original===
“ ‘Dave, w’en yer en Dilsey gits ready fer ter git married, I ain’ got no rejections. Dey’s a poun’ er so er chawin’-terbacker up at de house, en I reckon yo’ mist’iss kin fine a frock en a ribbin er two fer Dilsey. Youer bofe good niggers, en yer neenter be feared er bein’ sol’ ’way fum one ernudder long ez I owns dis plantation; en I ’spec’s ter own it fer a long time yit.’

===Chinese===
「『戴夫，等你和迪尔西打定主意要成亲的时候，我没有二话。宅子里还有那么一磅嚼烟，我想你主母还能给迪尔西寻出一件袍子、一两条丝带来。你们俩都是好黑人，只要这庄园还归我一天，就用不着怕你们俩被拆开卖掉——我估摸着这庄园我还要持有好些年呢。』

===Original===
“But dere wuz one man on de plantation w’at didn’ lack ter see Dave en Dilsey tergedder ez much ez ole marster did. W’en Mars Dugal’ went ter de sale whar he got Dilsey en Mahaly, he bought ernudder han’, by de name er Wiley. Wiley wuz one er dese yer shiny-eyed, double-headed little niggers, sha’p ez a steel trap, en sly ez de fox w’at keep out’n it. Dis yer Wiley had be’n pesterin’ Dilsey ’fo’ she come ter our plantation, en had nigh ’bout worried de life out’n her. She didn’ keer nuffin fer ’im, but he pestered her so she ha’ ter th’eaten ter tell her marster fer ter make Wiley let her ’lone. W’en he come ober to our place it wuz des ez bad, ’tel bimeby Wiley seed dat Dilsey had got ter thinkin’ a heap ’bout Dave, en den he sorter hilt off aw’ile, en purten’ lack he gin Dilsey up. But he wuz one er dese yer ’ceitful niggers, en w’ile he wuz laffin’ en jokin’ wid de yuther han’s ’bout Dave en Dilsey, he wuz settin’ a trap fer ter ketch Dave en git Dilsey back fer hisse’f.

===Chinese===
「可庄上有一个人，见戴夫和迪尔西厮守在一处，心里远不如老主人那样舒坦。杜加尔老爷去那场拍卖会买迪尔西和马哈利娘儿俩的时候，还搭手买进了一个劳力，名叫威利（Wiley）。威利是那种眼睛贼亮、一肚子鬼主意的小个子黑人，机灵得像口钢夹子，奸猾得像躲得过夹子的狐狸。这个威利早在迪尔西来我们庄园之前就一直缠磨她，几乎把她的命都折磨掉了。她半点也不把他放在心上，可他纠缠得凶，她只得扬言要去禀告主人，好叫威利放她清净。到了我们庄上，他还是那副德行，直到后来威利见迪尔西对戴夫动了真情，才稍稍收了些日子，装出把迪尔西让出去的样子。可他是个惯会弄鬼的黑人，明里拿戴夫和迪尔西跟别的劳力打趣说笑，暗地里却设着一个圈套，要坑戴夫一个跟头，好把迪尔西夺回来归自己。

===Original===
“Dave en Dilsey made up dere min’s fer ter git married long ’bout Christmas time, w’en dey’d hab mo’ time fer a weddin’. But ’long ’bout two weeks befo’ dat time ole mars ’mence’ ter lose a heap er bacon. Eve’y night er so somebody ’ud steal a side er bacon, er a ham, er a shoulder, er sump’n, fum one er de smoke-’ouses. De smoke-’ouses wuz lock’, but somebody had a key, en manage’ ter git in some way er ’nudder. Dey’s mo’ ways ’n one ter skin a cat, en dey’s mo’ d’n one way ter git in a smoke-’ouse﻿—leastways dat’s w’at I hearn say. Folks w’at had bacon fer ter sell didn’ hab no trouble ’bout gittin’ rid un it. Hit wuz ’g’in’ de law fer ter buy things fum slabes; but Lawd! dat law didn’ ’mount ter a hill er peas. Eve’y week er so one er dese yer big covered waggins would come ’long de road, peddlin’ terbacker en w’iskey. Dey wuz a sight er room in one er dem big waggins, en it wuz monst’us easy fer ter swop off bacon fer sump’n ter chaw er ter wa’m yer up in de wintertime. I s’pose de peddlers didn’ knowed dey wuz breakin’ de law, caze de niggers alluz went at night, en stayed on de dark side er de waggin; en it wuz mighty hard fer ter tell *w’at* kine er folks dey wuz.

===Chinese===
「戴夫和迪尔西拿定了主意，打算赶圣诞节前后成亲，那时节办婚礼能宽裕些。可就在婚期前两个礼拜上下，老主人开始丢腊肉。隔三差五，就有人从哪间熏肉房里偷走一扇腊肉，一只火腿，一条前腿，或是别的什么。熏肉房都上了锁，可有人手里有钥匙，总有法子钻得进去。剥猫皮的法子不止一种，进熏肉房的门道也不止一条——反正外头是这么传的。手里有腊肉要出手的人，不愁脱不了手。向奴隶买东西是犯法的；可老天爷！那法律连一堆豌豆都不值。隔一两个礼拜，就有一辆那种带篷的大货车顺着大路过来，贩烟叶，卖威士忌。那样的大车肚量大得很，拿腊肉换点什么嚼头、换点什么冬天暖身的东西，再容易不过。俺估摸那些货郎未必知道自己是在犯法，因为黑人都挑夜里去，还专拣车子背光的那一面待着；那车上究竟是什么路数的人，实在难认得很。

===Original===
“Atter two er th’ee hund’ed er meat had be’n stole’, Mars Walker call all de niggers up one ebenin’, en tol’ ’em dat de fus’ nigger he cot stealin’ bacon on dat plantation would git sump’n fer ter ’member it by long ez he lib’. En he say he’d gin fi’ dollars ter de nigger w’at ’skiver’ de rogue. Mars Walker say he s’picion’ one er two er de niggers, but he couldn’ tell fer sho, en co’se dey all ’nied it w’en he ’cuse em un it.

===Chinese===
「丢了两三百磅肉之后，一天傍晚，沃克老爷把黑人统统叫拢来，放话说，在这座庄园上，头一个叫他逮着偷腊肉的，管保得点叫他一辈子忘不了的记性。他还说，谁查出那个贼来，他赏五块钱。沃克老爷讲，他疑心一两个黑人，只是拿不准是谁；当然啦，他一指责，他们个个都喊冤不认。

===Original===
“Dey wa’n’t no bacon stole’ fer a week er so, ’tel one dark night w’en somebody tuk a ham fum one er de smoke-’ouses. Mars Walker des cusst awful w’en he foun’ out de ham wuz gone, en say he gwine ter sarch all de niggers’ cabins; w’en dis yer Wiley I wuz tellin’ yer ’bout up’n say he s’picion’ who tuk de ham, fer he seed Dave comin’ ’cross de plantation fum to’ds de smoke-’ouse de night befo’. W’en Mars Walker hearn dis fum Wiley, he went en sarch’ Dave’s cabin, en foun’ de ham hid under de flo’.

===Chinese===
「头一个来礼拜没再丢腊肉，直到一个漆黑的夜里，又有人从一间熏肉房里偷走了一只火腿。沃克老爷发觉火腿丢了，破口大骂，说要挨个搜黑人的小屋；俺方才跟您说的那个威利，这时跳出来讲，他疑心是谁偷的火腿，因为头天夜里，他看见戴夫从种植园那头、从熏肉房的方向走过来。沃克老爷一听威利这话，便去搜了戴夫的小屋，在地板底下翻出了那只火腿。

===Original===
“Eve’ybody wuz ’stonish’; but dere wuz de ham. Co’se Dave ’nied it ter de las’, but dere wuz de ham. Mars Walker say it wuz des ez he ’spected: he didn’ b’lieve in dese yer readin’ en prayin’ niggers; it wuz all ’pocrisy, en sarve’ Mars Dugal’ right fer ’lowin’ Dave ter be readin’ books w’en it wuz ’g’in’ de law.

===Chinese===
「人人惊得目瞪口呆；可火腿就在那儿摆着。戴夫当然到死不认，可火腿就在那儿摆着。沃克老爷说，事情正不出他所料：他素来就不信这种又念书又祷告的黑人；满嘴全是假正经，杜加尔老爷准许戴夫念那犯法的书，活该落得这报应。

===Original===
“W’en Mars Dugal hearn ’bout de ham, he say he wuz might’ly ’ceived en disapp’inted in Dave. He say he wouldn’ nebber hab no mo’ conferdence in no nigger, en Mars Walker could do des ez he wuz a mineter wid Dave er any er de res’ er de niggers. So Mars Walker tuk’n tied Dave up en gin ’im forty; en den he got some er dis yer wire clof w’at dey uses fer ter make sifters out’n, en tuk’n wrap’ it roun’ de ham en fasten it tergedder at de little een’. Den he tuk Dave down ter de blacksmif-shop, en had Unker Silas, de plantation blacksmif, fasten a chain ter de ham, en den fasten de yuther een’ er de chain roun’ Dave’s neck. En den he says ter Dave, sezee:﻿—

“ ‘Now, suh, yer’ll wear dat neckliss fer de nex’ six mont’s; en I ’spec’s yer ner none er de yuther niggers on dis plantation won’ steal no mo’ bacon dyoin’ er dat time.’

===Chinese===
「杜加尔老爷听说火腿的事，说自己大大上了当，对戴夫寒了心。他说往后再也不信任何黑人了，戴夫也好，其余哪个黑人也好，沃克老爷高兴怎么处置就怎么处置。于是沃克老爷把戴夫绑起来，赏了他四十鞭；随后他弄来一块做筛子用的铁丝布，把那只火腿整个裹住，在细的那一头扎拢。接着他把戴夫带到铁匠铺，叫庄园的铁匠西拉斯大叔（Unker Silas）往火腿上拴了一条链子，再把链子的另一头拴上戴夫的脖子。然后他对戴夫说——

「『听着，先生，往后六个月，这条项链你就戴着；我料想这半年里，你也好，庄上哪个黑人也好，都不敢再偷腊肉了。』

===Original===
“Well, it des ’peared ez if fum dat time Dave didn’ hab nuffin but trouble. De niggers all turnt ag’in’ ’im, caze he be’n de ’casion er Mars Dugal’ turnin’ ’em all ober ter Mars Walker. Mars Dugal’ wa’n’t a bad marster hisse’f, but Mars Walker wuz hard ez a rock. Dave kep’ on sayin’ he didn’ take de ham, but none un ’em didn’ b’lieve ’im.

===Chinese===
「唉，打那时起，戴夫就仿佛只剩下苦日子了。黑人个个跟他反目，因为就是他，害得杜加尔老爷把管教他们的大权整个交到了沃克老爷手里。杜加尔老爷本人不算恶主，可沃克老爷硬得像块石头。戴夫一口咬定火腿不是他拿的，可没有一个人肯信。

===Original===
“Dilsey wa’n’t on de plantation w’en Dave wuz ’cused er stealin’ de bacon. Ole mist’iss had sont her ter town fer a week er so fer ter wait on one er her darters w’at had a young baby, en she didn’ fine out nuffin ’bout Dave’s trouble ’tel she got back ter de plantation. Dave had patien’ly endyoed de finger er scawn, en all de hard words w’at de niggers pile’ on ’im, caze he wuz sho’ Dilsey would stan’ by ’im, en wouldn’ b’lieve he wuz a rogue, ner none er de yuther tales de darkies wuz tellin’ ’bout ’im.

===Chinese===
「有人指认戴夫偷腊肉那阵子，迪尔西不在庄园上。老夫人打发她进城去一两个礼拜，伺候她一位刚添了小娃的女儿；庄园上这些糟心事，她回来之前一概不知。戴夫耐着性子，忍受人们的指指戳戳，忍受黑人堆在他头上的种种恶言，因为他笃定迪尔西会站在他这边，决不会信他是个贼，也决不会信黑人们编派他的那些闲话。

===Original===
“W’en Dilsey come back fum town, en got down fum behine de buggy whar she b’en ridin’ wid ole mars, de fus’ nigger ’ooman she met says ter her﻿—

“ ‘Is yer seed Dave, Dilsey?’

“ ‘No, I ain’ seed Dave,’ says Dilsey.

“ ‘Yer des oughter look at dat nigger; reckon yer wouldn’ want ’im fer yo’ junesey no mo’. Mars Walker cotch ’im stealin’ bacon, en gone en fasten’ a ham roun’ his neck, so he can’t git it off’n hisse’f. He sut’nly do look quare.’ En den de ’ooman bus’ out laffin’ fit ter kill herse’f. W’en she got thoo laffin’ she up’n tole Dilsey all ’bout de ham, en all de yuther lies w’at de niggers be’n tellin’ on Dave.

===Chinese===
「迪尔西从城里回来，从她一路陪着老主人坐的马车后座上下来，迎头碰见的头一个黑人婆娘便对她说——

「『迪尔西，见着戴夫没有？』

「『没有，俺没见着戴夫。』迪尔西说。

「『你真该去瞧瞧那黑人；只怕你再也不想要他做你的心上人了吧。沃克老爷逮着他偷腊肉，往他脖子上拴了只火腿，他自己可取不下来。他那副模样，瞧着可真够滑稽的。』说着那婆娘放声大笑，笑得直不起腰来。她笑够了，便把火腿的事，连同黑人们编派戴夫的种种谎话，一五一十全倒给了迪尔西。

===Original===
“W’en Dilsey started down ter de quarters, who should she meet but Dave, comin’ in fum de cotton-fiel’. She turnt her head ter one side, en purten’ lack she didn’ seed Dave.

“ ‘Dilsey!’ sezee.

“Dilsey walk’ right on, en didn’ notice ’im.

“ ‘*Oh*, Dilsey!’

===Chinese===
「迪尔西动身往黑奴住区去，你道迎面遇上的是谁——正是戴夫，从棉田里收工回来。她把头扭向一边，只装没瞧见。

「『迪尔西！』他喊。

「迪尔西只管朝前走，不搭理他。

「『*哎*，迪尔西！』

===Original===
“Dilsey didn’ paid no ’tention ter ’im, en den Dave knowed some er de niggers be’n tellin’ her ’bout de ham. He felt monst’us bad, but he ’lowed ef he could des git Dilsey fer ter listen ter ’im fer a minute er so, he could make her b’lieve he didn’ stole de bacon. It wuz a week er two befo’ he could git a chance ter speak ter her ag’in; but fine’ly he cotch her down by de spring one day, en sezee:﻿—

===Chinese===
「迪尔西还是毫不理会，戴夫这才明白，已有黑人把火腿的事讲给她听了。他心里难受极了，可他寻思，只要能让迪尔西听他讲上一分钟，他就能叫她相信，腊肉不是他偷的。过了一两个礼拜，他才又得到跟她说话的机会；终于有一天，他在泉水边堵着了她，便说——

===Original===
“ ‘Dilsey, w’at fer yer won’ speak ter me, en purten’ lack yer doan see me? Dilsey, yer knows me too well fer ter b’lieve I’d steal, er do dis yuther wick’ness de niggers is all layin’ ter me﻿—yer *knows* I wouldn’ do dat, Dilsey. Yer ain’ gwine back on yo’ Dave, is yer?’

===Chinese===
「『迪尔西，你为什么不肯跟俺说话，只装看不见俺？迪尔西，你了解俺，知道俺不会偷东西，黑人们栽在俺头上的那些别的坏事，俺也一件没干——你*知道*俺决不做那种事，迪尔西。你总不会背弃你的戴夫，是不是？』

===Original===
“But w’at Dave say didn’ hab no ’fec’ on Dilsey. Dem lies folks b’en tellin’ her had p’isen’ her min’ ’g’in’ Dave.

“ ‘I doan wanter talk ter no nigger,’ says she, ‘w’at be’n whip’ fer stealin’, en w’at gwine roun’ wid sich a lookin’ thing ez dat hung roun’ his neck. I’s a ’spectable gal, *I* is. W’at yer call dat, Dave? Is dat a cha’m fer ter keep off witches, er is it a noo kine er neckliss yer got?’

===Chinese===
「可戴夫这番话对迪尔西全无效力。人们灌进她耳朵的那些谎话，早已把她的心对戴夫下了毒。

「『俺可不想理一个偷东西挨了鞭子的黑人，』她说，『还成天脖子上挂着那么个怪物四处走。俺是个体面姑娘，*俺*是的。你管那叫什么，戴夫？那是挡女巫的符咒呢，还是你新得的什么稀罕款式的项链？』

===Original===
“Po’ Dave didn’ knowed w’at ter do. De las’ one he had ’pended on fer ter stan’ by ’im had gone back on ’im, en dey didn’ ’pear ter be nuffin mo’ wuf libbin’ fer. He couldn’ hol’ no mo’ pra’r-meetin’s, fer Mars Walker wouldn’ ’low ’im ter preach, en de darkies wouldn’ ’a’ listen’ ter ’im ef he had preach’. He didn’ eben hab his Bible fer ter comfort hisse’f wid, fer Mars Walker had tuk it erway fum ’im en burnt it up, en say ef he ketch any mo’ niggers wid Bibles on de plantation he’d do ’em wuss’n he done Dave.

===Chinese===
「可怜的戴夫不知如何是好。他指望来撑他的最后一个人，也背弃了他，活着似乎再没有什么可盼的了。祷告会他也不能再主持了，沃克老爷不许他讲道，就算他讲，黑人也没人肯听了。连那本能给他一点安慰的《圣经》也没有了，沃克老爷把它从他手里夺走，一把火烧了个精光，还说再叫他在庄上逮着哪个黑人揣着《圣经》，管教得比整治戴夫还狠。

===Original===
“En ter make it still harder fer Dave, Dilsey tuk up wid Wiley. Dave could see him gwine up ter Aun’ Mahaly’s cabin, en settin’ out on de bench in de moonlight wid Dilsey, en singin’ sinful songs en playin’ de banjer. Dave use’ ter scrouch down behine de bushes, en wonder w’at de Lawd sen’ ’im all dem tribberlations fer.

===Chinese===
「更叫戴夫难熬的是，迪尔西同威利好上了。戴夫眼看他往马哈利大娘的小屋去，同迪尔西一道坐在月光下的长凳上，唱着邪门的歌，弹着班卓琴。戴夫常蹲在灌木丛后头，心想上帝降给他这一场又一场苦难，究竟是为了什么。

===Original===
“But all er Dave’s yuther troubles wa’n’t nuffin side er dat ham. He had wrap’ de chain roun’ wid a rag, so it didn’ hurt his neck; but w’eneber he went ter wuk, dat ham would be in his way; he had ter do his task, howsomedever, des de same ez ef he didn’ hab de ham. W’eneber he went ter lay down, dat ham would be in de way. Ef he turn ober in his sleep, dat ham would be tuggin’ at his neck. It wuz de las’ thing he seed at night, en de fus’ thing he seed in de mawnin’. W’eneber he met a stranger, de ham would be de fus’ thing de stranger would see. Most un ’em would ’mence’ ter laf, en whareber Dave went he could see folks p’intin’ at him, en year ’em sayin’:﻿—

“ ‘W’at kine er collar dat nigger got roun’ his neck?’ er, ef dey knowed ’im, ‘Is yer stole any mo’ hams lately?’ er ‘W’at yer take fer yo’ neckliss, Dave?’ er some joke er ’nuther ’bout dat ham.

===Chinese===
「可戴夫别的种种苦楚，同那只火腿一比，又都不算什么了。他在链子上缠了破布，倒不磨脖子；可每回上工，那火腿总在跟前碍事；话虽如此，他的活计还是照旧得做完，跟没挂火腿一个样。每回躺下，那火腿又横在跟前。睡着翻个身，它就拽他的脖子。夜里合眼前见的最后一样东西是它，清早睁眼见的头一样东西也是它。每回碰上生人，人家头一眼瞧见的准是那只火腿。多半的人当场就笑出声来；戴夫走到哪儿，都能看见人家对他指指点点，听见他们嚷嚷——

「『那黑人脖子上套的是什么箍儿？』认识他的就说：『近来又偷火腿了没有？』再不就问：『你那项链什么价，戴夫？』再不然，就是拿那只火腿编的别的什么玩笑。

===Original===
“Fus’ Dave didn’ mine it so much, caze he knowed he hadn’ done nuffin. But bimeby he got so he couldn’ stan’ it no longer, en he’d hide hisse’f in de bushes w’eneber he seed anybody comin’, en alluz kep’ hisse’f shet up in his cabin atter he come in fum wuk.

===Chinese===
「起先戴夫还不很放在心上，因为他知道自己没做亏心事。可到后来，他实在受不住了，远远望见有人来，就往灌木丛里躲，收工以后，更是成天把自己关死在小屋里。

===Original===
“It wuz monst’us hard on Dave, en bimeby, w’at wid dat ham eberlastin’ en etarnally draggin’ roun’ his neck, he ’mence’ fer ter do en say quare things, en make de niggers wonder ef he wa’n’t gittin’ out’n his mine. He got ter gwine roun’ talkin’ ter hisse’f, en singin’ corn-shuckin’ songs, en laffin’ fit ter kill ’bout nuffin. En one day he tole one er de niggers he had ’skivered a noo way fer ter raise hams﻿—gwine ter pick ’em off’n trees, en save de expense er smoke-’ouses by kyoin’ ’em in de sun. En one day he up’n tole Mars Walker he got sump’n pertickler fer ter say ter ’im; en he tuk Mars Walker off ter one side, en tole ’im he wuz gwine ter show ’im a place in de swamp whar dey wuz a whole trac’ er lan’ covered wid ham-trees.

===Chinese===
「这桩事把戴夫熬煎苦了。到后来，那只火腿没日没夜、无穷无尽地拖在脖子上，他渐渐说起古怪话、做出古怪事来，叫黑人疑心他是不是失了心窍。他渐渐满地里走着自言自语，唱起剥玉米壳时唱的那种歌，无缘无故笑得要死要活。有一天，他告诉一个黑人，说他发现了一个生火腿的新法子——要从树上摘火腿，搁太阳底下晒干，熏肉房的开销全省下了。又有一天，他忽然凑到沃克老爷跟前，说有句要紧话要单独对他讲；他把沃克老爷拉到一旁，说要在沼泽里指给他一处地方，那里有一整片地，长满了火腿树。

===Original===
“Wen Mars Walker hearn Dave talkin’ dis kine er fool-talk, en w’en he seed how Dave wuz ’mencin’ ter git behine in his wuk, en w’en he ax’ de niggers en dey tole ’im how Dave be’n gwine on, he ’lowed he reckon’ he’d punish’ Dave ernuff, en it mou’t do mo’ harm dan good fer ter keep de ham on his neck any longer. So he sont Dave down ter de blacksmif-shop en had de ham tuk off. Dey wa’n’t much er de ham lef’ by dat time, fer de sun had melt all de fat, en de lean had all swivel’ up, so dey wa’n’t but th’ee er fo’ poun’s lef’.

===Chinese===
「沃克老爷听戴夫说这样的浑话，又见他干活渐渐落了下风，再问过黑人、听他们讲了戴夫这些日子种种行状，便寻思给戴夫的责罚也受得够久了，再叫他把火腿挂下去，只怕坏处比好处还多。于是他把戴夫打发到铁匠铺，把火腿卸了下来。到那时候，火腿已剩下不多了：日头把肥膘都晒化了，瘦肉也都干缩了，统共只剩三四磅。

===Original===
“W’en de ham had be’n tuk off’n Dave, folks kinder stopped talkin’ ’bout ’im so much. But de ham had be’n on his neck so long dat Dave had sorter got use’ ter it. He look des lack he’d los’ sump’n fer a day er so atter de ham wuz tuk off, en didn’ ’pear ter know w’at ter do wid hisse’f; en fine’ly he up’n tuk’n tied a lighterd-knot ter a string, en hid it under de flo’ er his cabin, en w’en nobody wuzn’ lookin’ he’d take it out en hang it roun’ his neck, en go off in de woods en holler en sing; en he allus tied it roun’ his neck w’en he went ter sleep. Fac’, it ’peared lack Dave done gone clean out’n his mine. En atter a w’ile he got one er de quarest notions you eber hearn tell un. It wuz ’bout dat time dat I come back ter de plantation fer ter wuk﻿—I had be’n out ter Mars Dugal’s yuther place on Beaver Crick for a mont’ er so. I had hearn ’bout Dave en de bacon, en ’bout w’at wuz gwine on on de plantation; but I didn’ b’lieve w’at dey all say ’bout Dave, fer I knowed Dave wa’n’t dat kine er man. One day atter I come back, me’n Dave wuz choppin’ cotton tergedder, w’en Dave lean’ on his hoe, en motion’ fer me ter come ober close ter ’im; en den he retch’ ober en w’ispered ter me.

===Chinese===
「火腿从戴夫身上卸下来之后，人们对他的议论也就渐渐少了。可那火腿在他脖子上挂了那么久，戴夫竟有些习惯了它。卸下来的头一两天，他倒像丢了件什么东西似的，茫然不知拿自己怎么办才好；到后来，他竟拿绳子拴上一个松明疙瘩（lighterd-knot），藏在小屋的地板底下，趁没人瞧见，就掏出来挂上脖子，独自跑进树林子里又喊又唱；睡觉的时候，也总把它系在脖子上。说实在的，看那样子，戴夫是整个儿失了心神了。又过了一些时候，他得了一个你这辈子都没听说过的最古怪的念头。就在那前后，俺回庄园上工来了——先前俺被派到杜加尔老爷在比弗溪的另一处庄子上，干了个把月。戴夫和腊肉的事，还有庄园上的种种光景，俺都听说了；可大伙儿讲戴夫的那些话，俺一概不信，因为俺知道，戴夫不是那样的人。俺回来后的一天，俺和戴夫一起锄棉花，戴夫拄着锄头，朝俺招手，叫俺挨近他些；然后他探过身子，跟俺咬耳朵。

===Original===
“ ‘Julius,’ sezee, ‘did yer knowed yer wuz wukkin’ long yer wid a ham?’

“I couldn’ ’magine w’at he meant. ‘G’way fum yer, Dave,’ says I. ‘Yer ain’ wearin’ no ham no mo’; try en fergit ’bout dat; ’t ain’ gwine ter do yer no good fer ter ’member it.’

===Chinese===
「『朱利叶斯，』他说，『你可知道，你是在跟一只火腿一块儿锄地？』

「俺猜不透他这话什么意思。『去你的吧，戴夫，』俺说，『你早就不挂那火腿了；想法子把它忘了吧；老记着它，对你没什么好处。』

===Original===
“ ‘Look a-yer, Julius,’ sezee, ‘kin yer keep a secret?’

“ ‘Co’se I kin, Dave,’ says I. ‘I doan go roun’ tellin’ people w’at yuther folks says ter me.’

“ ‘Kin I trus’ yer, Julius? Will yer cross yo’ heart?’

“I cross’ my heart. ‘Wush I may die ef I tells a soul,’ says I.

===Chinese===
「『你听着，朱利叶斯，』他说，『你能守住秘密么？』

「『那当然能，戴夫，』俺说，『人家跟俺讲的话，俺可从不搬出去到处说。』

「『俺信得过你不，朱利叶斯？你敢画十字起誓么？』

「俺画了十字。『俺要是讲出去一个字，就叫俺死了。』俺说。

===Original===
“Dave look’ at me des lack he wuz lookin’ thoo me en ’way on de yuther side er me, en sezee:﻿—

“ ‘Did yer knowed I wuz turnin’ ter a ham, Julius?’

===Chinese===
「戴夫望着俺，那眼神像是穿透了俺这个人，望到俺身背后老远的地方去了，随后说道——

「『你可知道，俺正在变成一只火腿，朱利叶斯？』

===Original===
“I tried ter ’suade Dave dat dat wuz all foolishness, en dat he oughtn’t ter be talkin’ dat-a-way﻿—hit wa’n’t right. En I tole ’im ef he’d des be patien’, de time would sho’ly come w’en eve’ything would be straighten’ out, en folks would fine out who de rale rogue wuz w’at stole de bacon. Dave ’peared ter listen ter w’at I say, en promise’ ter do better, en stop gwine on dat-a-way; en it seem lack he pick’ up a bit w’en he seed dey wuz one pusson didn’ b’lieve dem tales ’bout ’im.

===Chinese===
「俺竭力劝戴夫，说那全是糊涂念头，他不兴这么讲话——那不像话。俺还告诉他，只要耐心等着，总有一天水落石出，偷腊肉的到底是谁，大家总会查个明白。戴夫像是听进了俺的话，答应往好里做，不再那样疯癫下去了；而且见总算还有一个人不信那些闲话，他仿佛也稍稍振作了一点。

===Original===
“Hit wa’n’t long atter dat befo’ Mars Archie McIntyre, ober on de Wimbleton road, ’mence’ ter complain ’bout somebody stealin’ chickens fum his hen-’ouse. De chickens kep’ on gwine, en at las’ Mars Archie tole de han’s on his plantation dat he gwine ter shoot de fus’ man he ketch in his hen-’ouse. In less’n a week atter he gin dis warnin’, he cotch a nigger in de hen-’ouse, en fill’ ’im full er squir’l-shot. W’en he got a light, he ’skivered it wuz a strange nigger; en w’en he call’ one er his own sarven’s, de nigger tole ’im it wuz our Wiley. W’en Mars Archie foun’ dat out, he sont ober ter our plantation fer ter tell Mars Dugal’ he had shot one er his niggers, en dat he could sen’ ober dere en git w’at wuz lef un ’im.

===Chinese===
「此后没过多久，威明顿大道那头的阿奇·麦金太尔老爷（Mars Archie McIntyre）开始抱怨，说有人偷他鸡窝里的鸡。鸡接二连三地丢，末了阿奇老爷对他庄上的劳力放了话：谁要叫他堵在鸡窝里，他就开枪崩谁。这警告放过不出一个礼拜，他就在鸡窝里堵住了一个黑人，一枪打松鼠的铅砂，把他打得满身都是。等点上灯一瞧，才认出是个外庄的黑人；叫来自家一个仆人一问，那仆人说，这正是我们庄上的威利。阿奇老爷查明这一节，便差人上我们庄园来报信，说他打了一个我们庄的黑人，叫我们过去把剩下的那半个人抬回来。

===Original===
“Mars Dugal’ wuz mad at fus’; but w’en he got ober dere en hearn how it all happen’, he didn’ hab much ter say. Wiley wuz shot so bad he wuz sho’ he wuz gwine ter die, so he up’n says ter ole marster:﻿—

“ ‘Mars Dugal’,’ sezee, ‘I knows I’s be’n a monst’us bad nigger, but befo’ I go I wanter git sump’n off’n my mine. Dave didn’ steal dat bacon w’at wuz tuk out’n de smoke-’ouse. *I* stole it all, en I hid de ham under Dave’s cabin fer ter th’ow de blame on him﻿—en may de good Lawd fergib me fer it.’

===Chinese===
「杜加尔老爷起先动了气；可等他赶到那边，听明白事情的前前后后，也就无话可说了。威利伤得极重，自料必死，便对老主人开口道——

「『杜加尔老爷，』他说，『俺知道自己一直是个顶顶坏的黑人，可俺咽气之前，想卸下心里一件事。熏肉房里丢的腊肉，不是戴夫偷的。全是*俺*偷的，那只火腿，也是俺藏到戴夫小屋底下去的，为的是把罪名栽到他头上——愿仁慈的上帝饶恕俺吧。』

===Original===
“Mars Dugal’ had Wiley tuk back ter de plantation, en sont fer a doctor fer ter pick de shot out’n ’im. En de ve’y nex’ mawnin’ Mars Dugal’ sont fer Dave ter come up ter de big house; he felt kinder sorry fer de way Dave had be’n treated. Co’se it wa’n’t no fault er Mars Dugal’s, but he wuz gwine ter do w’at he could fer ter make up fer it. So he sont word down ter de quarters fer Dave en all de yuther han’s ter ’semble up in de yard befo’ de big house at sun-up nex’ mawnin’.

===Chinese===
「杜加尔老爷叫人把威利抬回庄园，又请来医生，把他身上的铅砂一颗颗剜出来。第二天一早，杜加尔老爷便派人叫戴夫到大宅去；他想起戴夫受的这些委屈，心里老大过意不去。固然这不是杜加尔老爷的过错，可他愿意尽力弥补。于是他捎话到黑奴住区，叫戴夫和全体劳力第二天日出时分到大宅前的院子里集合。

===Original===
“Yearly in de mawnin’ de niggers all swarm’ up in de yard. Mars Dugal’ wuz feelin’ so kine dat he had brung up a bairl er cider, en tole de niggers all fer ter he’p deyselves.

“All de han’s on de plantation come but Dave; en bimeby, w’en it seem lack he wa’n’t comin’, Mars Dugal’ sont a nigger down ter de quarters ter look fer ’im. De sun wuz gittin’ up, en dey wuz a heap er wuk ter be done, en Mars Dugal’ sorter got ti’ed waitin’; so he up’n says:﻿—

===Chinese===
「一大清早，黑人们就涌满了院子。杜加尔老爷心肠正好，抬出来一小桶苹果酒，叫黑人们各自随便喝。

「全庄的劳力都到了，独独不见戴夫；等了一阵，眼看他是不来了，杜加尔老爷便派一个黑人下住区去找。太阳越升越高，地里的活还压着一大堆，杜加尔老爷等得有些不耐烦，便开口道——

===Original===
“ ‘Well, boys en gals, I sont fer yer all up yer fer ter tell yer dat all dat ’bout Dave’s stealin’ er de bacon wuz a mistake, ez I s’pose yer all done hearn befo’ now, en I’s mighty sorry it happen’. I wants ter treat all my niggers right, en I wants yer all ter know dat I sets a heap by all er my han’s w’at is hones’ en smart. En I want yer all ter treat Dave des lack yer did befo’ dis thing happen’, en mine w’at he preach ter yer; fer Dave is a good nigger, en has had a hard row ter hoe. En de fus’ one I ketch sayin’ anythin’ ’g’in’ Dave, I’ll tell Mister Walker ter gin ’im forty. Now take ernudder drink er cider all roun’, en den git at dat cotton, fer I wanter git dat Persimmon Hill trac’ all pick’ ober ter-day.’

===Chinese===
「『好了，小伙子们、姑娘们，我把你们大伙儿叫到这儿来，是要告诉你们：说戴夫偷腊肉的种种，全是一场误会——这个想必你们如今都听说了——事情落到这步田地，我心里很过意不去。我一向要好好待我这些黑人，也要叫你们知道，凡是诚实又机灵的劳力，我都顶顶看重。我要你们待戴夫，还同这事没发生之前一模一样，他讲道你们要好好听；因为戴夫是个好黑人，他那份苦日子也熬得够久了。往后叫我逮住头一个说戴夫闲话的，我就叫沃克先生赏他四十鞭。来吧，都再满上一杯苹果酒，喝完就下地去摘棉花——柿子山（Persimmon Hill）那片地，我要它今天全摘完。』

===Original===
“W’en de niggers wuz gwine ’way, Mars Dugal’ tole me fer ter go en hunt up Dave, en bring ’im up ter de house. I went down ter Dave’s cabin, but couldn’ fine ’im dere. Den I look’ roun’ de plantation, en in de aidge er de woods, en ’long de road; but I couldn’ fine no sign er Dave. I wuz ’bout ter gin up de sarch, w’en I happen’ fer ter run ’cross a foot-track w’at look’ lack Dave’s. I had wukked ’long wid Dave so much dat I knowed his tracks: he had a monst’us long foot, wid a holler instep, w’ich wuz sump’n skase ’mongs’ black folks. So I follered dat track ’cross de fiel’ fum de quarters ’tel I got ter de smoke-’ouse. De fus’ thing I notice’ wuz smoke comin’ out’n de cracks; it wuz cu’ous, caze dey hadn’ be’n no hogs kill’ on de plantation fer six mont’ er so, en all de bacon in de smoke-’ouse wuz done kyoed. I couldn’ ’magine fer ter sabe my life w’at Dave wuz doin’ in dat smoke-’ouse. I went up ter de do’ en hollered:﻿—

“ ‘Dave!’

“Dey didn’ nobody answer. I didn’ wanter open de do’, fer w’ite folks is monst’us pertickler ’bout dey smoke-’ouses; en ef de oberseah had a-come up en cotch me in dere, he mou’t not wanter b’lieve I wuz des lookin’ fer Dave. So I sorter knock at de do’ en call’ out ag’in:﻿—

“ ‘O Dave, hit’s me﻿—Julius! Doan be skeered. Mars Dugal’ wants yer ter come up ter de big house﻿—he done ’skivered who stole de ham.’

===Chinese===
「黑人们散去的时候，杜加尔老爷吩咐俺去寻戴夫，把他带到宅子里来。俺先到戴夫的小屋去看，屋里不见人。俺又在庄园四下里、林子边上、大路两旁都寻了个遍，还是不见戴夫的一点踪影。俺正要撒手不找了，恰好撞见一串脚印，瞧着像是戴夫的。俺同戴夫搭伙做过那么多活，认得他的脚印：他那只脚长得出奇，脚心凹得深，这在黑人里头是少有的。俺便顺着那串脚印，从住区一路穿过田地，直跟到熏肉房跟前。俺头一眼留意的，是板缝里正往外冒烟；这就怪了，庄园上已有六个月上下没宰过猪，熏肉房里的腊肉全数熏制停当了。俺想破了脑袋，也想不出戴夫在那熏肉房里做什么。俺走到门口，喊了一声——

「『戴夫！』

「没有人应声。俺本不想开门，因为白人们对自家的熏肉房顶顶讲究；要是监工恰好撞来，逮着俺待在里头，他未必肯信俺不过是来找戴夫。于是俺在门上轻轻敲了敲，又喊了一声——

「『喂，戴夫，是俺——朱利叶斯！别怕。杜加尔老爷叫你上大宅去——偷火腿的贼已经查出来了。』

===Original===
“But Dave didn’ answer. En w’en I look’ roun’ ag’in en didn’ seed none er his tracks gwine way fum de smoke-’ouse, I knowed he wuz in dere yit, en I wuz ’termine’ fer ter fetch ’im out; so I push de do’ open en look in.

“Dey wuz a pile er bark burnin’ in de middle er de flo’, en right ober de fier, hangin’ fum one er de rafters, wuz Dave; dey wuz a rope roun’ his neck, en I didn’ haf ter look at his face mo’ d’n once fer ter see he wuz dead.

===Chinese===
「可戴夫还是不应声。俺再四下一望，不见有他的脚印走出熏肉房去，便知道他还在里头，于是打定主意，非把他弄出来不可；俺推开门，朝里望去。

「屋子当中烧着一堆树皮，正对着火头上、从一根房梁上垂下来的，正是戴夫；他脖子上套着一根绳子，俺只消朝他脸上望上一眼，就知道他已经死了。

===Original===
“Den I knowed how it all happen’. Dave had kep’ on gittin’ wusser en wusser in his mine, ’tel he des got ter b’lievin’ he wuz all done turnt ter a ham; en den he had gone en built a fier, en tied a rope roun’ his neck, des lack de hams wuz tied, en had hung hisse’f up in de smoke-’ouse fer ter kyo.

“Dave wuz buried down by de swamp, in de plantation buryin’ groun’. Wiley didn’ died fum de woun’ he got in Mars McIntyre’s hen ’ouse; he got well atter a w’ile, but Dilsey wouldn’ hab nuffin mo’ ter do wid ’im, en ’t wa’n’t long ’fo’ Mars Dugal’ sol’ ’im ter a spekilater on his way souf﻿—he say he didn’ want no sich a nigger on de plantation, ner in de county, ef he could he’p it. En w’en de een’ er de year come, Mars Dugal’ turnt Mars Walker off, en run de plantation hisse’f atter dat.

===Chinese===
「到这时俺才明白事情的原委。戴夫的心病一日重似一日，到后来竟真的认定，自己已经整个儿变成了一只火腿；于是他生了火，往自己脖子上套了根绳，像捆扎那些火腿一样把自己捆好，吊进熏肉房里，等着一道熏制。

「戴夫葬在沼泽边上的庄园坟地里。威利在麦金太尔老爷家鸡窝里挨的那一枪，倒没要了他的命；将养了一阵，他好了，可迪尔西再不肯同他有半点干系；没过多久，杜加尔老爷就把他卖给了一个正往南边去的人贩子——他说，只要有法子，这样心术的黑人，他庄园上不要，连县里也不留。到了年底，杜加尔老爷把沃克老爷辞了，打那以后，庄园便由他亲自打理。

===Original===
“Eber sence den,” said Julius in conclusion, “w’eneber I eats ham, it min’s me er Dave. I lacks ham, but I nebber kin eat mo’ d’n two er th’ee poun’s befo’ I gits ter studyin’ ’bout Dave, en den I has ter stop en leab de res’ fer ernudder time.”

There was a short silence after the old man had finished his story, and then my wife began to talk to him about the weather, on which subject he was an authority. I went into the house. When I came out, half an hour later, I saw Julius disappearing down the lane, with a basket on his arm.

===Chinese===
「打那以后，」朱利叶斯这样收尾，「俺每回吃火腿，都要想起戴夫。俺爱吃火腿，可每回吃到两三磅，就免不了想起戴夫来，只好就此打住，剩下的留待下回再吃。」

老人讲完故事，沉默了一小会儿，随后我妻子便同他聊起天气来——在这门学问上，他可是权威。我进了屋。半小时后再出来，我望见朱利叶斯胳膊上挎着一只篮子，正消失在巷子尽头。

===Original===
At breakfast, next morning, it occurred to me that I should like a slice of ham. I said as much to my wife.

“Oh, no, John,” she responded, “you shouldn’t eat anything so heavy for breakfast.”

I insisted.

“The fact is,” she said, pensively, “I couldn’t have eaten any more of that ham, and so I gave it to Julius.”

===Chinese===
第二天早上吃早饭时，我忽然起意，很想吃一片火腿，便照实对妻子说了。

「哦，不行，约翰，」她答道，「早饭你不该吃这么油腻的东西。」

我坚持要。

「实情是，」她若有所思地说，「那火腿我再也吃不下去了，所以就给了朱利叶斯。」

### Lonesome Ben / 孤零零的本

===Original===
There had been some talk among local capitalists about building a cotton mill on Beaver Creek, a few miles from my place on the sand hills in North Carolina, and I had been approached as likely to take an interest in such an enterprise. While I had the matter under advisement it was suggested, as an inducement to my cooperation, that I might have the brick for the mill made on my place—there being clay there suitable for the purpose—and thus reduce the amount of my actual cash investment. Most of my land was sandy, though I had observed several outcroppings of clay along the little creek or branch forming one of my boundaries.

One afternoon in summer, when the sun was low and the heat less oppressive than it had been earlier in the day, I ordered Julius, our old colored coachman, to harness the mare to the rockaway and drive me to look at the clay-banks. When we were ready, my wife, who wished to go with me for the sake of the drive, came out and took her seat by my side.

===Chinese===
当地资本家中间一度有个议论，说要在比弗溪上建一座棉纺厂——那地方离我在北卡罗来纳州沙冈上的住处只有几英里——也有人来找我接洽，看我很可能有意在这类事业里入一股。这事我还拿在手里斟酌，有人便提出一个诱我合作的条件：厂里用砖大可就地在我庄上烧制——因为那里有适于烧砖的黏土——这样便可折减我实际投入的现款。我的地大半是沙土，不过沿着构成我一处地界的那条小溪——本地人管它叫支溪（branch）——我见过几处露头的黏土。

夏日的一个午后，日头已经偏西，暑气也不似白天早些时候那般逼人，我吩咐朱利叶斯——我家的老黑人车夫——套好四轮敞篷马车，载我去看那几处黏土坡。一切齐备时，我妻子想趁这趟车兜兜风，也出来在我身旁坐下了。

===Original===
We reached our first point of destination by a road running across the plantation, between a field of dark-green maize on the one hand and a broad expanse of scuppernong vines on the other. The road led us past a cabin occupied by one of my farm-hands. As the carriage went by at a walk, the woman of the house came to the door and curtsied. My wife made some inquiry about her health, and she replied that it was poor. I noticed that her complexion, which naturally was of a ruddy brown, was of a rather sickly hue. Indeed, I had observed a greater sallowness among both the colored people and the poor whites thereabouts than the hygienic conditions of the neighborhood seemed to justify.

After leaving this house our road lay through a cotton field for a short distance, and then we entered a strip of woods, through which ran the little stream beside which I had observed the clay. We stopped at the creek, the road by which we had come crossing it and continuing over the land of my neighbor, Colonel Pemberton. By the roadside, on my own land, a bank of clay rose in almost a sheer perpendicular for about ten feet, evidently extending back some distance into the low, pine-clad hill behind it, and having also frontage upon the creek. There were marks of bare feet on the ground along the base of the bank, and the face of it seemed freshly disturbed and scored with finger marks, as though children had been playing there.

===Chinese===
我们走一条横穿庄园的路，到了头一处目的地：路的一边是一田墨绿的玉米，另一边是漫开一大片的斯卡珀农葡萄藤。这条路经过一座雇工住的小木屋。马车缓步经过时，屋里的主妇来到门口，屈膝行了个礼。妻子问了问她的身体，她回答说不大好。我注意到她的脸色——天生是红润的棕褐色——此刻却透着一层病容。的确，这一带的有色人和穷白人中间，我见到的蜡黄气色，比左近卫生条件所能解释的还要重得多。

过了这座小屋，我们的路又在棉田里穿行了一小段，随后进入一带树林，我见到黏土的那条小溪便从林中流过。我们在溪边停了车——来路在这里跨过小溪，继续伸进我邻居彭伯顿上校的地界。路旁，在我自己的地上，一道黏土坡近乎笔直地拔起约莫十英尺，看样子向后延展进去一段，没入背后那座覆着松树的低冈，坡脚也紧临着溪水。坡脚一带的地上印着赤脚的痕迹，坡面上似乎新近被人翻动过，划满一道道指痕，仿佛有孩子们在那里玩耍过。

===Original===
"Do you think that clay would make good brick, Julius?" I asked the old man, who had been unusually quiet during the drive. He generally played with the whip, making little feints at the mare, or slapping her lightly with the reins, or admonishing her in a familiar way; but on this occasion the heat or some other cause had rendered him less demonstrative than usual.

"Yas, suh, I knows it would," he answered.

"How do you know? Has it ever been used for that purpose?"

"No, suh; but I got my reasons fer sayin' so. Ole Mars Dugal useter hab a brickya'd fu'ther up de branch—I dunno as yer noticed it, fer it's all growed ober wid weeds an' grass. Mars Dugal said dis yer clay wouldn' make good brick, but I knowed better."

===Chinese===
「朱利叶斯，你看那黏土能烧出好砖吗？」我问老人。这一路上他出奇地安静。平日里他总爱摆弄马鞭，朝母马虚抽那么几下，或是拿缰绳轻轻拍她一记，或是熟稔地数落她两句；可这一回，许是暑气，许是别的什么缘故，他比往常收敛得多。

「是的，老爷，俺晓得准能。」他答道。

「你怎么晓得的？那土从前可曾烧过砖？」

「没有，老爷；可俺这么说自有俺的道理。老杜加尔老爷从前在支溪再往上一点儿的地方有过一座砖窑场——俺不知道您留意过没有，如今那儿全长满荒草了。杜加尔老爷说这黏土烧不出好砖，可俺心里比他清楚。」

===Original===
I judged from the appearance of the clay that it was probably deficient in iron. It was of a yellowish-white tint and had a sort of greasy look.

"Well," I said, "we'll drive up to the other place and get a sample of that clay, and then we'll come back this way."

"Hold on a minute, dear," said my wife, looking at her watch, "Mabel has been over to Colonel Pemberton's all the afternoon. She said she'd be back at five. If we wait here a little while she'll be along and we can take her with us."

"All right," I said, "we'll wait for her. Drive up a little farther, Julius, by that jessamine vine."

While we were waiting, a white woman wearing a homespun dress and slat-bonnet, came down the road from the other side of the creek, and lifting her skirts slightly, waded with bare feet across the shallow stream. Reaching the clay-bank she stooped and gathered from it, with the aid of a convenient stick, a quantity of the clay which she pressed together in the form of a ball. She had not seen us at first, the bushes partially screening us; but when, having secured the clay, she turned her face in our direction and caught sight of us watching her, she hid the lump of clay in her pocket with a shamefaced look, and hurried away by the road she had come.

===Chinese===
看这黏土的成色，我判断它多半缺铁。土色白里透黄，看去有种油润的感觉。

「好吧，」我说，「咱们先把车赶到上面那处窑场，取一点那边的土样，回头再打这条道回来。」

「等一等，亲爱的，」妻子看了看表，说道，「梅贝尔一下午都在彭伯顿上校家。她说五点回来。咱们在这儿稍等一会儿，她就会打这条路过，正好带她一道去。」

「行，」我说，「那就等等她。朱利叶斯，把车再往前赶一点儿，到那丛素馨藤旁边。」

我们等着的工夫，一个穿家织布衣裙、戴硬檐遮阳帽的白人妇女，从小溪对面的路上走来，微微提起裙摆，赤着脚蹚过浅浅的溪水。到了黏土坡前，她弯下腰，借着顺手的一根木棍，从坡上抠下一块黏土，团成个球。起初她没瞧见我们——灌木半掩着我们的车；等土到了手，她转过脸来，一眼望见我们正瞧着她，便一脸羞赧地把那团黏土塞进衣袋，顺着来路匆匆走了。

===Original===
"What is she going to do with that, Uncle Julius?" asked my wife. We were Northern settlers, and still new to some of the customs of the locality, concerning which we often looked to Julius for information. He had lived on the place many years and knew the neighborhood thoroughly.

"She's gwineter eat it, Miss Annie," he replied, "w'en she gits outer sight."

"Ugh!" said my wife with a grimace, "you don't mean she's going to eat that great lump of clay?"

"Yas'm I does; dat's jes' w'at I means—gwineter eat eve'y bit un it, an' den come back bimeby fer mo'."

===Chinese===
「朱利叶斯大叔，她拿那黏土做什么用？」妻子问。我们是北方来的新住户，对本地的一些风俗还不摸门，常常要仰仗朱利叶斯指点。他在这一带住了多年，四邻八舍摸得透熟。

「她要拿去吃，安妮太太，」他答道，「等走到人家瞧不见的地方就吃。」

「哎呀！」妻子做了个苦脸，「你不会是说，她要把那么一大团黏土吃下肚吧？」

「正是，太太，俺就是这个意思——一点不剩全吃下去，过不多会儿还要回来再拿呢。」

===Original===
"I should think it would make them sick," she said.

"Dey gits use' ter it," said Julius. "Howsomeber, ef dey eats too much it does make 'em sick; an' I knows w'at I'm er-talkin' erbout. I doan min' w'at dem kinder folks does," he added, looking contemptuously after the retreating figure of the poor-white woman, "but w'eneber I sees black folks eat'n' clay of'n dat partic'lar clay-bank, it alluz sets me ter studyin' 'bout po' lonesome Ben."

"What was the matter with Ben?" asked my wife. "You can tell us while we're waiting for Mabel."

===Chinese===
「照我看，这东西是要吃出病来的。」她说。

「吃惯了就没事了。」朱利叶斯说。「不过吃多了，也真会吃出病来；俺说这话是有实底的。那号人干啥，俺不放在心上，」他望着那穷白人妇女远去的背影，轻蔑地补了一句，「可俺每回瞧见黑人上那道黏土坡挖土吃，就总不由得想起可怜的、孤零零的本（Ben）。」

「本是怎么回事？」妻子问，「正好趁等梅贝尔的工夫，讲给我们听听。」

===Original===
Old Julius often beguiled our leisure with stories of plantation life, some of them folklore stories, which we found to be in general circulation among the colored people; some of them tales of real life as Julius had seen it in the old slave days; but the most striking were, we suspected, purely imaginary, or so colored by old Julius's fancy as to make us speculate at times upon how many original minds, which might have added to the world's wealth of literature and art, had been buried in the ocean of slavery.

===Chinese===
老朱利叶斯时常拿种植园生活的故事给我们的闲工夫解闷：有的是民间传说，我们发现在有色人中间流传甚广；有的是他在早先奴隶年代亲眼见过的真事；但最出彩的那些，我们疑心纯属虚构，或是让老朱利叶斯的想象渲染得走了样——以至我们时常揣想：不知有多少本可为世界文学与艺术的财富添彩的独创头脑，就这样埋葬在奴隶制的汪洋大海里。

===Original===
"W'en ole Mars Marrabo McSwayne owned dat place ober de branch dere, w'at Kunnel Pembe'ton owns now," the old man began, "he useter hab a nigger man name' Ben. Ben wuz one er dese yer big black niggers—he was mo'd'n six foot high an' black ez coal. He wuz a fiel'-han' an' a good wukker, but he had one little failin'—he would take a drap er so oncet in a w'ile. Co'se eve'ybody laks a drap now an' den, but it 'peared ter 'fec' Ben mo'd'n it did yuther folks. He didn' hab much chance dat-a-way, but eve'y now an' den he'd git holt er sump'n' somewahr, an' sho's he did, he'd git out'n de narrer road. Mars Marrabo kep' on wa'nin' 'm 'bout it, an' fin'lly he tol' 'im ef he eber ketch 'im in dat shape ag'in he 'uz gwineter gib 'im fo'ty. Ben knowed ole Mars Marrabo had a good 'memb'ance an' alluz done w'at he said, so he wuz monst'us keerful not ter gib 'm no 'casion fer ter use his 'memb'ance on him. An' so fer mos' a whole yeah Ben 'nied hisse'f an' nebver teched a drap er nuffin'.

===Chinese===
「当年老马拉博·麦克斯韦恩老爷还拥有支溪那头这座园子——就是如今彭伯顿上校的那处地方，」老人开了腔，「他家有个黑奴叫本。本属于那种高大魁梧的黑人——六英尺开外的个子，黑得像块炭。他是把田里的好手，活儿也干得漂亮，只有一样小毛病——隔三差五爱来上一口。当然喽，谁不时想喝上一盅，可那口酒对本的劲儿，比对旁人的都大。他平日里不大有这样的机会，不过时不时也能从哪儿弄到一点，而只要一沾上，保准就走岔了道儿。马拉博老爷一再为这事警告他，末了干脆撂下话：再要逮着他那副模样，就赏他四十鞭。本知道老马拉博老爷记性好，说出的话从来作数，所以格外小心，绝不敢给他一个由头，把那份好记性使到自己头上。就这样，差不多整整一年，本管住了自己，滴酒不曾沾唇。

===Original===
"But it's h'ad wuk ter larn a ole dog new tricks, er ter make him fergit de ole uns, an' po' Ben's time come bimeby, jes' lak ev'ybody e'se's does. Mars Marrabo sent 'im ober ter dis yer plantation one day wid a bundle er cotton-sacks fer Mars Dugal', an' wiles he wuz ober yere, de ole Debbil sent a' 'oman w'at had cas' her eyes on 'im an' knowed his weakness, fer ter temp' po' Ben wid some licker. Mars Whiskey wuz right dere an' Mars Marrabo wuz a mile erway, an' so Ben minded Mars Whiskey an' fergot 'bout Mars Marrabo. W'en he got back home he couldn' skasely tell Mars Marrabo de message w'at Mars Dugal' had sent back ter 'im.

"Mars Marrabo listen' at 'im 'temp' ter tell it; an' den he says, kinder col' an' cuttin'-like—he didn' 'pear ter get mad ner nuffin':

===Chinese===
「可是，教老狗学新把戏，或是叫它忘掉老把戏，都是难事；可怜的本的劫数迟早也到了，跟谁的一个样。有一天，马拉博老爷打发他到这座园子来，给杜加尔老爷捎一捆棉花口袋；他在这儿耽搁的当口，老魔鬼差来一个女人——她早看上了本，也知道他的软处——拿酒去引诱可怜的本。威士忌老爷就在眼前，马拉博老爷却在一英里之外；于是本听了威士忌老爷的话，把马拉博老爷忘在了脑后。等回到家，杜加尔老爷托他捎的口信，他几乎递不明白。

「马拉博老爷听着他结结巴巴地转述，然后开了口，口气有点儿冷，带着刺——倒不见得是真动气：

===Original===
"'Youer drunk, Ben.'

"De way his marster spoke sorter sobered Ben, an' he 'nied it of co'se.

"'Who? Me, Mars Marrabo? *I* ain' drunk; no, marster, *I* ain' drunk. I ain' teched a drap er nuffin' sence las' Chris'mas, suh.'

"'Youer drunk, Ben, an' don't you dare ter 'spute my wo'd, er I'll kill you in yo' tracks! I'll talk ter you Sad'day night, suh, w'en you'll be sober, an' w'en you'll hab Sunday ter 'flect over ou' conve'sation, an' 'nuss yo' woun's.'

===Chinese===
「『你喝醉了，本。』

「主人说话的这副腔调，倒把本吓醒了几分，他当然一口否认。

「『谁？俺吗，马拉博老爷？俺没醉；没有，主人，俺没醉。打去年圣诞节起，俺滴酒也没沾过，老爷。』

「『你喝醉了，本。你敢驳我的话，我就地要你的命！礼拜六夜里咱们再谈，先生，到时候你酒也醒了，礼拜天正好可以回味回味咱们这番谈话，养一养你的伤。』

===Original===
"W'en Mars Marrabo got th'oo talkin' Ben wuz mo' sober dan he wuz befo' he got drunk. It wuz Wednesday w'en Ben's marster tol' 'im dis, an' 'twix' den an' Friday night Ben done a heap er studyin'. An' de mo' he studied de mo' he didn' lak de way Mars Marrabo talked. He hadn' much trouble wid Mars Marrabo befo', but he knowed his ways, an' he knowed dat de longer Mars Marrabo waited to do a thing de wusser he got 'stid er gittin' better lak mos' folks. An' Ben fin'lly made up his min' he wa'n't gwineter take dat cowhidin'. He 'lowed dat ef he wuz little, like some er de dahkies on de plantation, he wouldn' min' it so much; but he wuz so big dey'd be mo' groun' fer Mars Marrabo ter cover, an' it would hurt dat much mo'. So Ben 'cided ter run erway.

===Chinese===
「马拉博老爷这一通话讲完，本醒得比他没喝醉之前还彻底。主人交代这话是在礼拜三；从那时到礼拜五夜里，本翻来覆去琢磨了个够。越琢磨，他越不喜欢马拉博老爷说话的那副腔调。他从前跟马拉博老爷没闹过多少别扭，可他熟知老爷的脾性，知道马拉博老爷一件事拖得越久，下手就越狠，不像多数人那样火气渐消。本终于打定主意：那顿牛皮鞭，他不伺候。他寻思，倘若自己个子小些，像庄上有些黑人那样，挨一顿也不打紧；可他块头这么大，马拉博老爷要抽的地面就宽，要多疼上多少倍。于是本决计逃走。

===Original===
"He had a wife an' two chil'en, an' dey had a little cabin ter deyse'ves down in de quahters. His wife Dasdy wuz a good-lookin', good-natu'd 'oman, an' 'peared ter set a heap er sto' by Ben. De little boy wuz name' Pete; he wuz 'bout eight er nine years ole, an' had already 'menced ter go out in de fiel' an' he'p his mammy pick cotton, fer Mars Marrabo wuz one er dese yer folks w'at wants ter make eve'y aidge cut. Dis yer little Pete wuz a mighty soople dancer, an' w'en his daddy would set out in de yahd an' pick de banjo fer 'im, Pete could teach de ole folks noo steps—dancin' jes seemed to come nachul ter 'im. Dey wuz a little gal too; Ben didn' pay much 'tention ter de gal, but he wuz monst'us fond er Dasdy an' de boy. He wuz sorry ter leab 'em, an' he didn' tell 'em nuffin' 'bout it fer fear dey'd make a fuss. But on Friday night Ben tuk all de bread an' meat dey wuz in de cabin an' made fer de woods.

===Chinese===
「他有老婆，还有两个孩子，一家人住在黑奴住区里自己的一座小木屋。他的妻子达丝迪（Dasdy）是个模样周正、脾气随和的女人，看得出她把本看得很重。小儿子名叫皮特，八九岁年纪，已经能下地帮他娘拾棉花——因为马拉博老爷是那种巴不得人人都把力气使到尖上的主儿。这个小皮特跳舞跳得灵巧极了；他爹坐在院子里为他拨班卓琴的时候，皮特还能教老人们几步新舞——跳舞这桩事在他身上，就像是娘胎里带来的。家里还有个小闺女；本对闺女不怎么上心，对达丝迪和儿子却疼得不行。要撇下他们，他心里也难受，只是怕他们哭闹坏了事，一个字也没吐露。礼拜五夜里，本揣上屋里所有的面包和肉，一头扎进了树林。

===Original===
"W'en Sad'day come an' Ben didn' 'pear, an' nobody didn' know nuffin' 'bout 'im, Mars Marrabo 'lowed of co'se dat Ben had runned erway. He got up a pahty an' tuk de dawgs out an' follered de scen' down ter de crick an' los' it. Fer Ben had tuk a go'd-full er tar 'long wid' 'im, an' w'en he got ter de crick he had 'n'inted his feet wid tar, an' dat th'ewed de houn's off'n de scent. Dey sarched de woods an' follered de roads an' kep' watchin' fer a week, but dey couldn' fin' no sign er Ben. An' den Mars Marrabo got mo' stric', an' wuked his niggers hahder'n eber, ez ef he wanted ter try ter make up fer his loss.

"W'en Ben stahted out he wanted ter go ter de No'th. He didn' know how fur it wuz, bet he 'lowed he retch dar in fo' er five days. He knowed de No'th Stah, an' de fus night he kep' gwine right straight to'ds it. But de nex' night it was rainin', an' fer two er th'ee nights it stayed cloudy, an' Ben couldn' see de No'th Stah. Howsomeber, he knowed he had got stahted right' an' he kep' gwine right straight on de same way fer a week er mo' 'spectin' ter git ter de No'th eve'y day, w'en one mawin' early, atter he had b'en walkin' all night, he come right smack out on de crick jes whar he had stahted f'om.

===Chinese===
「到了礼拜六，本没露面，谁也说不清他的去向，马拉博老爷当然断定他是跑了。他张罗起一伙人，带上狗，循着气味一路追到小溪边，线索断了。原来本随身带了一葫芦瓢焦油，到溪边时拿焦油抹了脚，就这样把猎狗的鼻子甩脱了。他们搜树林、追大路，一连守望了一个礼拜，可连本的一点影子也没找着。打那以后，马拉博老爷管束得更严了，驱使庄上的黑奴比先前更狠，仿佛想把自己这份损失找补回来。

「本刚动身的时候，一心要去北方。他不知道北方有多远，寻思着四五天总能走到。他认得北极星，头一夜便一直照着它直走。可第二夜下起了雨，接连两三个夜里满天阴云，本看不见北极星了。不过他想，起初的方向没错，便照着原来的方向一直走了一个多礼拜，天天都指望当天就能到北方。谁知一天清早，走了一整夜的他，竟不偏不倚又走回了小溪边，正是他动身的地方。

===Original===
"Co'se Ben wuz monst'us disapp'inted. He had been wond'rin' w'y he hadn' got ter de No'th befo', an' behol', heah he wuz back on de ole plantation. He couldn' un'erstan' it at fus', but he wuz so hongry he didn' hab time ter study 'bout nuffin' fer a little w'ile but jes' ter git sump'n' ter eat; fer he had done eat up de bread an' meat he tuk away wid 'im, an' had been libbin' on roas'n-ears an' sweet'n taters he'd slip out'n de woods an' fin' in co'n fiel's 'an 'tater-patches. He look 'cross de crick, an' seed dis yer clay-bank, an' he waded ober an' got all he could eat, an' den tuk a lump wid 'im, an' hid in de woods ag'in 'til he could study de matter ober some.

"Fus' he 'lowed dat he better gib hiss'ef up an' take his lammin'. But jes' den he 'membered de way Mars Marrabo looked at 'im an' w'at he said 'bout Sad'day night; an' den he 'lowed dat ef Mars Marrabo ketch 'im now, he'd wear 'im ter a frazzle an' chaw up de frazzle, so de wouldn' be nuffin' lef' un 'im at all, an' dat Mars Marrabo would make a' example an' a warnin' of 'im fer all de niggers in de naberhood. Fac' is Mars Marrabo prob'ly wouldn' a' done much ter 'im fer it 'ud be monst'us po' 'couragement fer runaway niggers ter come back, ef dey gwineter git killed w'en dey come. An' so Ben waited 'til night, an' den he went back an' got some mo' clay an' eat it an' hid hisse'f in de woods ag'in.

"Well, hit wuz quare 'bout Ben, but he stayed roun' heah fer a mont', hidin' in de woods in de daytime, an' slippin' out nights an' gittin' clay ter eat an' water f'om de crick yanker ter drink. De water in dat crick wuz cl'ar in dem days, stidder bein' yallar lak it is now."

===Chinese===
「本自然失望透顶。他一直纳闷怎么总走不到北方，你瞧，这不又转回老庄园来了嘛。起初他也闹不明白，可他实在饿坏了，好一阵子顾不上琢磨别的，一心只想弄点东西吃——随身带的面包和肉早吃光了，这些天全靠夜里溜出树林，到玉米地和红薯地里刨些嫩玉米和红薯过活。他朝溪对面一望，望见了这道黏土坡，便蹚过溪去，吃了个够，又揣上一块，重新躲进树林，好把这件事细细思量思量。

「起先他想，不如自首回去，领他那一顿好打。可紧接着他就想起马拉博老爷望着他的那副神气，想起他撂下的关于礼拜六夜里的狠话；于是又想，这会儿要是让马拉博老爷逮着，非把他抽成一绺丝、再把丝嚼个稀碎不可，叫他浑身上下连点儿渣都不剩，还要拿他给左近所有的黑人立个榜样、做个警戒。实情是，马拉博老爷多半也不会真把他怎么样——逃奴要是回来就得送命，往后谁还敢回来？这于劝逃奴回头，未免太亏了。于是本捱到天黑，又回去挖了些黏土吃下，重新躲进树林。

「唉，本的事就是这般古怪：他在这一带盘桓了整整一个月，白天躲在树林里，夜里溜出来挖黏土吃，再从那边的溪里舀水喝。那些日子，这条溪的水还是清的，不像如今这样黄。」

===Original===
We had observed that the water, like that of most streams that take their rise in swamps, had an amber tint to which the sand and clay background of the bed of the stream imparted an even yellower hue.

"What did he do then, Julius?" asked my wife, who liked to hear the end of a story.

===Chinese===
我们确实注意到，这溪水同大多数发源于沼泽的溪流一样，泛着一层琥珀色；溪床上沙土与黏土的底色，又给它添上一重更深的黄。

「那他后来怎么样了呢，朱利叶斯？」妻子问。她听故事，向来要听到结局。

===Original===
"Well, Miss, he made up his min' den dat he wuz gwineter staht fer de No'th ag'in. But wiles he b'en layin' roun' in de woods he had 'mence ter feel monst'us lonesome, an' it 'peared ter him dat he jes' couldn' go widout seein' Dasdy an' little Pete. Fus' he 'lowed he'd go up ter de cabin, but he thought 'bout de dogs 'roun' de yahd, an' dat de yuther dahkies mought see 'im, an' so he 'cided he'd better watch fer 'em 'til dey come long de road—it wuz dis yer same road—w'en he could come out'n de woods an' talk ter 'em.
